# Life sim spec

> **Moved 2026-10-28.** This spec came from the Wurld app (`arneyjfs/wurld-app`), where Wurlds ran behind the `lifeSimBeta` flag. It now lives here with the code. Paths and ids that name other Wurld surfaces refer to that repo; see [README.md](./README.md#reading-them-outside-wurld) and [../porting-notes.md](../porting-notes.md). The `/wurlds` routes are no longer beta-gated here.


> Status: **draft** — invariants are reserved, contract bodies are MVP-grade. Implementation lands in a vertical slice (see `LIFE-WIP-*`); each slice phase will tighten this spec, not the other way around.

Owns the new `/life` surface: an AI-powered language-learning life simulator. Users inhabit a character in an AI-generated world and acquire the practice language by living that life — conversations with NPCs, choose-your-own-adventure beats, embedded multi-choice exercises, and a persistent world state. The visual identity is **scene-first**: every beat happens against a Gemini-rendered SVG background (apartment, café, market, fantasy academy, etc.), with NPC speech overlaid as a single bubble. Not a chat thread — an RPG.

The code is asserted against these invariants — not the other way around. **A failing test means the spec was violated; loosening the test requires a spec update first.**

## Coordinates with

- [`practice-mode.md`](./practice-mode.md) — the user's existing flashcards (`users/{uid}/flashcards/{cardId}`) are the seed vocabulary the life-sim LLM weaves into scenarios. Save-word from a life bubble lands a flashcard the same way chat does (`MSG-VOCAB-*` write path). Topics surfaced in life-sim feed the practice planner's recency signal (`PRACTICE-PLAN-*` consumes `recentSessionTopics`).
- [`wurld-tutor.md`](./wurld-tutor.md) — closest existing pattern. Life-sim borrows the conversation-memory + compaction model (`TUTOR-MEM-*` / `TUTOR-COMPACT-*`) and the dual-language single-call generation idea.
- [`message-pipeline.md`](./message-pipeline.md) — life bubbles reuse the bubble translation + vocab-tag + ExplainModal infrastructure (`MSG-VIEW-*`, `MSG-VOCAB-*`). They do NOT route through `processMessagePipeline` — life bubbles are written directly with their translations + complexWords + wordTranslations already populated by the life-step generator (same shortcut tutor uses, `TUTOR-INV-3`).
- [`cards-screen.md`](./cards-screen.md) — flashcards added in /life appear in /cards under the active language same as any other card (`CARDS-LIST-1`).
- [`i18n.md`](./i18n.md) — chrome strings ("Travelling to…", "Arrived at…", choice-chip placeholders) follow the standard catalogue + regen workflow.
- §"Design decisions" below — rationale for backdrop-first UI, per-world backdrop cache, transient/persistent entity model, and local-first LLM dev.

| `LIFE-NPC-*` | NPC handling | Transient vs persistent, avatar generation, roster maintenance |
| `LIFE-UI-*` | Screen layout | Scene-first composition, action bar, chip grid, free-text gating, brand naming (Wurlds) |

| `LIFE-CONTRACT-*` | Interaction contract | Bubble-vs-system taxonomy, practice intercept rule, non-mutation, never-repeat, CYOA classification |

| `LIFE-WORLD-ARC-*` | World arcs (chapters) | Chapter through-lines spanning multiple scenes; chapter completion; "Continue your wurld" flow |

| `LIFE-NPC-RECUR-*` | Recurring NPCs | scope: scene / world / transient; model-driven promotion + retirement; soft 8-cap |
| `LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-*` | Chapter vocab recap | "Words you practised" list on the scene + chapter completion screens; client-side derivation from `correction` / `flashcard_added` events |

| `LIFE-EX-PLAN-*` / `LIFE-EX-GEN-*` | Two-stage graded-question generation | lifeStep emits a `gradedPlan`; a separate `gradedQuestionGenerator` call authors `practicePrompt` + chips |

---

## Product requirements

The features this spec covers, captured so the spec can be re-derived without the originating conversation context.

**Scene-first RPG identity.** Each beat renders a Gemini-generated SVG background, not a chat thread. The scene IS the headline feature.

**Persistent world.** A user's life persists across sessions. Characters, places, decisions, and learned vocabulary all carry over. The world remembers.

**Vocabulary woven into narrative.** The user's existing flashcard deck seeds the world. The LLM is encouraged (not forced) to surface saved vocabulary in conversations + exercises.

**Two interaction modes.** Choose-your-own-adventure beats (all choices valid) and graded multi-choice questions (one correct answer), mixed throughout. Wrong answers handled in-narrative — NPC corrects, OR the wrong choice has consequence.

**Embedded learning, not interruption.** Exercises are part of the conversation, not a separate quiz mode.

**Bilingual surface.** Same view-mode controls Wurld already has (`MSG-VIEW-*`): show practice, show native, show both. Per-bubble inspection on tap.

**Wide-range world seeds.** From a SIMS-style office life to medieval kingdoms to sci-fi colonies. User can also write their own seed manually. A nudge keeps users away from worlds incompatible with their flashcards.

**Cache-aware design.** Persistent scenes + NPCs render their SVG once; LLM prompt prefix stays stable across turns; transient entities are cheap.

---

## 1. Data model — `LIFE-DATA-*`

All Firestore paths live under `users/{uid}/…` so reads + writes follow the existing owner-only security rules.

| Path | Schema | Lifetime |
|---|---|---|
| `users/{uid}/lifeWorld/main` | `ZLifeWorld` | Singleton — one life per user in v1 |
| `users/{uid}/lifeEvents/{eventId}` | `ZLifeEvent` | Append-only memory log (see `LIFE-EVENT-*`) |
| `users/{uid}/lifeNpcs/{npcId}` | `ZLifeNpc` | Persistent NPCs only (transients live in events alone) |
| `users/{uid}/lifeBackdrops/{backdropId}` | `ZLifeBackdrop` | Persistent scenes only (transients live in events alone) |
| `users/{uid}/lifeWorlds/{worldId}/completedArcs/{arcId}` | `ZWorldArc` | Archived chapters, one doc per chapter (`arcId = chapter-${chapterNumber}`) — LIFE-WORLD-ARC-1. Replaces the former unbounded `world.completedArcs[]` inline array; the world doc keeps only the `completedArcCount` scalar. |
| `users/{uid}/flashcards/{cardId}` | `ZFlashCard` (existing) | Existing collection, unchanged |
| Firebase Storage: `lifeAssets/{uid}/scenes/{backdropId}.svg` | binary | SVG bytes for persistent scenes |
| Firebase Storage: `lifeAssets/{uid}/npcs/{npcId}.svg` | binary | NPC avatar SVG |
| Firebase Storage: `lifeAssets/{uid}/avatar.svg` | binary | User's own avatar |

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-DATA-1** | `ZLifeWorld` holds: character (name, age?, traits[]), city, locale (the practice language at world-creation time, frozen), worldSeed (the onboarding chip key or manual prompt), `worldMode` (REQUIRED since 2026-09-29 — the migration stamped `realistic` on pre-toggle worlds, so `ZLifeWorld.worldMode` has no `.optional()`), currentLocation (backdropId or descriptor), currentGoals[], createdAt, updatedAt. **Player avatar off the hot doc:** the world doc carries ONLY the cheap `characterAvatarStatus` flag; the avatar BYTES (a `data:image/svg+xml` URI, multi-KB) live in the `lifeWorlds/{worldId}/assets/character` SIDECAR doc (`{ dataUri }`, `src/lib/life/lifeAvatarAssets.ts`), read ONCE via `useCharacterAvatar` when status is `'ready'` (getDoc, not a listener; module-level cache + in-flight dedup mirroring `LIFE-BACKDROP-2`). The world doc is written many times per turn and re-streams in full to the world listener — keeping the avatar bytes off it saves re-streaming ~KB every turn. `commitNode` writes the sidecar BEFORE flipping status to `'ready'`, so a `'ready'` reader always finds it. Clean break — pre-existing worlds are disposable. |
| **LIFE-DATA-2** | `ZLifeEvent` holds: id, kind (`beat` / `flashcard_added` / `npc_introduced` / `backdrop_visited` / `decision` / `correction`), payload (kind-specific), createdAt. Append-only — events never get edited or deleted. The LLM context sees only the last `STEP_EVENT_WINDOW` (20) events (`LIFE-CTX-13`); older history lives in the append-only `storyLog` spine (`LIFE-STORY-LOG-2`). All rows remain in Firestore for audit and to drive the client-side derivations. (Compaction — which used to prune old events into a `rollingSummary` rewrite — was removed in WS1-C, `LIFE-MEM-1`.) |
| **LIFE-DATA-3** | `ZLifeNpc` holds: id, name, relationshipToUser, traits[], oneLineMemory (authored inline by the step model — LIFE-NPC-MEM-1; also written by compaction until it retires), **avatarStatus** (the avatar SVG BYTES live in the `assets/npc-{id}` sidecar per LIFE-DATA-1 — never inline on this roster-listened doc; resolved via `useNpcAvatars`), description, scope, firstMetAt, lastSeenAt. Created only when the step generator OR compaction promotes an NPC from transient. |
| **LIFE-DATA-4** | `ZLifeBackdrop` holds: id, descriptor (normalised cache key), locationLabel (display), **svgInline** (the rendered SVG markup cached INLINE on the doc — there is no Storage upload), createdAt, lastVisitedAt. Persistent scenes are reused on revisit; transient scenes render fresh each time and aren't stored. |
| **LIFE-DATA-5** | The user's `practiceLanguage` for the life world is frozen at world creation. Changing the user's app-wide `learningLanguage` does NOT retroactively switch the life world's language — that would invalidate every event in the log. To switch, the user must delete their current life and start a new one. |
| **LIFE-DATA-6** | A user has at most ONE life world in v1. Multi-life is out of scope. The "delete + restart" affordance fully wipes `lifeWorld`, `lifeEvents`, `lifeNpcs`, `lifeBackdrops`, and the associated Storage assets. Flashcards are NOT deleted (they belong to the deck, not the life). |

---

## 2. Session lifecycle — `LIFE-FLOW-*`

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-FLOW-1** | A "session" is one app foreground span. Session start = cold-open OR returning from background after >30 min. Session end = backgrounding the app, OR a hard reload. No explicit "end session" UI. |
| **LIFE-FLOW-2** | On session start with a life world present: replay the last N=20 events into the in-memory context buffer, fetch any flashcards created since `lifeWorld.updatedAt` and append them as `flashcard_added` events to `lifeEvents` (so the LLM sees them as recent memories), reload the current scene (cached) + last 2 bubbles, then await user input. The `/life` route is otherwise resumable from snapshot. |
| **LIFE-FLOW-3** | On session start with NO life world: route auto-redirects to onboarding (`LIFE-ONBOARD-*`). No "you have no world" empty state — onboarding IS the empty state. |
| **LIFE-FLOW-4** | If a `lifeStep` was in-flight when the app was backgrounded, the optimistic UI state (bubble + chip grid placeholder) re-renders on resume. The Cloud Function call resumes from where it left off (idempotent on the `stepId` the client provides). |
| **LIFE-FLOW-5** | **Resume recap (wurlds-v2 Phase C).** Re-entering a world whose event log is NON-empty overlays `WurldResumeScreen` before any dialogue seeding, assembled ENTIRELY from stored data (zero LLM on the resume path): saga title + tone; `saga.playerFacingTeaser` ("The story"); `currentArc.premise` + chapter number; `resumeRecap.text` — else the `storyLog` spine, else the section hides (`rollingSummary` fallback RETIRED 2026-09-29, `LIFE-MEM-2`); and the last ≤3 `beat` events as verbatim mini-bubbles ("Moments ago"). **Languages (LIFE-LANG-8):** the "Previously" recap renders in the SYSTEM language ONLY (`resumeRecap.text` is a single system-language paragraph); the "Moments ago" mini-bubbles show narration in the system language and each npc/user line as practice-language + a compact system-language gloss (`data-testid="LifeScreen-WurldResume-MomentGloss"`, rendered only when `nativeText` is present). Tapping Continue releases the LIFE-FLOW-2 seeding and **AUTO-CONTINUES** straight into the resume live turn (v2.1) — `buildResumeSeed` still seeds the recent context bubbles, but there is NO manual `'__resume__'` placeholder chip to tap (its `▶️` emoji was removed and the chip is gone); LifeScreen fires `continueTurn()` (`resetTurn` keeps the seeded bubbles) so the story resumes without a second tap. Fresh worlds (0 events) keep `WorldIntroScreen` and never see the recap. The recap text is polished FIRE-AND-FORGET on world exit (LifeScreen unmount / back-nav): a flash-lite call (category `life_resume_recap`; input = rollingSummary + chapter premise + last ≤10 beat/decision events; SI parameterised on the effective `systemLanguage = world.systemLanguage ?? world.nativeLanguage`) writes `world.resumeRecap = { text: <a single system-language "Previously on…" paragraph>, lastEventId: newest event id in the input, generatedAt }`. It fires ONLY when ≥5 events landed since `resumeRecap.lastEventId` (no recap yet, or an anchor id that fell out of the 40-event listener window, counts as stale ⇒ fire). Every generation failure is tolerated — no retry, no UI; the next world exit is the retry. |
| **LIFE-FLOW-6** | **"This world has never been played" must be SERVER-CONFIRMED before anything bills on it.** Entering a resumed world fired TWO `life_step` calls — `<turn kind="opening">` and then `<turn kind="continue">` — measured 2026-08-03 on the emulator by logging every step request's turn kind. `useCollectionSnapshot` reports `loading: false` as soon as it holds ANY snapshot, **including an empty cache-only one on a cold load**, so `!events.loading && events.length === 0` is not the claim it reads as: for a world that HAS history it is briefly a false "fresh world", and every branch keyed on `eventsData.length === 0` believes it. The `LIFE-BACKDROP-21` opening prefetch acted on exactly that window. Its result is never consumed by the resume path (which runs its own `continueTurn`), so it was **a full billed generation discarded on every resume that loaded this way** — the same waste `LIFE-EX-PLAN-5` holds a graded turn to one call to prevent, on the one path nothing was counting. It also produced the visible failure: the wasted call had eaten the turn, so the player got *"The story snagged for a moment"* on re-entry. **The fix is the gate the hook already documents** — `fromCache === false`, exposed for precisely this and described in `useCollectionSnapshot` as "the definitive gate". `eventsConfirmed = !loading && !fromCache` now guards BOTH billing effects: the prefetch, and the opening-beat fire (whose failure mode is worse — the player would ARRIVE at a brand-new opening node in a world that already has a story, rather than resuming it). **Deliberately NOT applied to the render gating** (`introShowing` / `recapShowing`): those key on the same count and can flash the intro card on a resumed world, but holding the whole screen back until the server confirms trades a brief wrong overlay for a longer blank one. Tracked separately. **Asserted by counting, not by rendering** — `expect(resumeMock.stepCalls()).toBe(1)` in the LIFE-FLOW-5 E2E, whose fixture list is deliberately ONE entry. The rendered-text assertion passed just as well when two calls were made and the second happened to land; the saving IS the call that does not happen. |
| **LIFE-FLOW-6.1** | **A test that asserts on an aggregate DERIVED from a world's whole event log gets its OWN world.** Fixing `LIFE-FLOW-6` let the life-flow serial block run past position 14 for the first time, and position 19 (`LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-2`) went red — not from any product change, but because tests 15-18 now execute and one of them (the graded listen-and-fill) logs `voudrais` **correct** with a live timestamp, inside the scene window that test derives its recap from and newer than the miss it seeded. The row it asserts is `data-correct="false"` read `true`. The test had a comment claiming this was safe — *"the assertions below check presence, never the correct flag"* — which was true when written and was silently invalidated later when `LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-7` added an assertion on exactly that flag. **A comment cannot hold that invariant**: it describes assertions that other people go on to add, in a file where 24 tests share one world by design. Presence assertions tolerate a shared log; anything that derives a VALUE from it (correct/incorrect, counts, scores, per-type tallies) does not. Such tests now call `seedWorld` for themselves — the pattern the newer specs already use — so the window contains only what the test put there. The shared world stays for the flow tests it genuinely serves. |
| **LIFE-FLOW-7** | **An ENTRY overlay must never cover a live dialogue. ⚠ OPEN — REPRODUCED, the remount is confirmed, the input that triggers it is NOT yet measured, NOT fixed.** `WorldIntroScreen` and `WurldResumeScreen` are both `fixed inset-0` over a translucent `bg-(--color-bg)/85 backdrop-blur-sm`, so one of them up mid-turn does not HIDE the option grid — it dims it and eats every pointer event. On 2026-09-19, under heavy box load, `LIFE-GRID-4/5` died on its 120-second budget clicking a chip, and the failure screenshot showed the recap overlay over a live grid on a world the test had seeded fresh moments earlier. ⚠ **THE MECHANISM FIRST RECORDED FOR IT IS WRONG** (`practice-mode.md`, batch 8; corrected there). It blamed the two-gate asymmetry `LIFE-FLOW-6` left open — the overlay renders on `eventsLoaded`, the `setRecapDismissed(true)` guard sits behind `eventsConfirmed` — claiming the opening turn's own events could satisfy the overlay from cache while the guard still waited on the server. **They cannot.** `setRecapDismissed(true)` and `engine.actions.startOpening()` are CONSECUTIVE STATEMENTS in one effect body (`LifeScreen.tsx`), so the latch is enqueued before the turn is even started, and the turn is a network round trip: `recapDismissed` is true many renders before `commitNode` can write the first event. Reading `eventsConfirmed` at the render site would not have changed any conjunct's value at the moment of failure — **the fix that note proposed is a no-op that would have looked like a fix.** The reachable failure state is `eventsLoaded && events.length > 0 && !recapDismissed`, and on a fresh world `recapDismissed` has exactly ONE setter path, so it requires an opening that did not fire IN THIS MOUNT — a remount. Four remount vectors were checked and all are clean: the route key (`<LifeScreen key={worldId}>`, `routes.tsx`) is stable per world; `settleNavSlots` spreads `{...last}` so a nav-transition settle preserves `slot.id` and reconciles rather than remounts; the route carries no `Suspense`/`lazy`; the `ErrorBoundary` would paint a fallback instead of the app. The listener's other writers were checked too — `useArcSelfHeal` touches the world doc only, and `appendFlashcardEvent` (the `LIFE-FLOW-2` flashcard sync) has **no caller at all**. ✅ **REPRODUCED 2026-09-20**, in a DIFFERENT test, by re-running `life-flow.spec.ts` twice under ~150% CPU oversubscription (53 passed, 1 flaky; `LIFE-GRID-4/5` itself passed both times): `LIFE-SPEECH-6` lost its fill-blank prompt to the recap dialog on a world it had seeded fresh and played two turns into. **The accessibility snapshot in `error-context.md` settles the argument the screenshot could not** — the recap is up (*"Previously, in this wurld" / "Moments ago Sophie is waiting at the café." / Continue*) and **there are NO dialogue bubbles in the tree at all.** The bubbles live in `LifeScreen`'s own `useState`; the engine's in `useNodeEngine`. Both gone ⇒ the screen REMOUNTED mid-turn, `recapDismissed` reset to `false`, and the in-memory `snapshotCache` (session-scoped, explicitly "lives between hook unmount and remount") re-seeded the events listener SYNCHRONOUSLY on the first render as `{loading:false, fromCache:true, data:<the opening beats>}` — all three conjuncts, in one frame, with no server round trip to wait for. **So the overlay is the SYMPTOM; the remount is the defect, and it silently discards the player's turn.** ⚠ **The fifth vector, and the one that was never on the list because it is not on this route:** `AppRoutes` is a `switch (gate.phase)` in which `case 'app'` is the ONLY arm that renders the shell, so ANY transient flip out of `'app'` — for one render — unmounts every screen in the app. `decideGate`'s branch 6 reaches `complete-profile` off `profile.data === null && !profile.loading`, and `useDocSnapshot` publishes exactly that state on a Zod parse failure (`setState({data: parsed, …})` where `parsed` may be `null`) as well as on a genuine `!snap.exists()`; `profileGapsAreTrustworthy` then returns `true` because "loaded, data === null → genuinely empty profile → gate", and `getProfileGaps(null)` reports every field missing. **Which input actually flips is NOT yet measured** — the harness forwards no page console, which is why this cost two full runs to get this far. **Shipped now:** `expectNoEntryOverlay` in `life-flow.spec.ts`, so the next occurrence costs one DOM query and NAMES the overlay instead of a 120-second timeout. **NOT shipped:** any product change, because the correct fix depends on which gate input flips, and a fix aimed at the wrong one is indistinguishable from the no-op this row already documents. **Next step is instrumentation, not a patch** — forward page console in the E2E harness, log gate-phase transitions, re-run the speech block under load. **A fix for a disproved mechanism is worth exactly as much as a guard whose fixtures cannot tell the bug from its absence.** |

---

## 3. Onboarding — `LIFE-ONBOARD-*`

Mounts at `/life/start` when no `lifeWorld` exists. Three steps, all on one screen (scroll between sections, no multi-page flow).

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-1** | Step 1: name input. Placeholder text suggests using a different name for immersion. Empty = use the user's display name. Accepts any unicode string ≤ 32 chars. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-2** | Step 2: practice-language **selection**. The row is a button opening the SHARED `LanguagePicker` — the same component and the same `bucket="practice"` recents that Settings uses — so any supported language is reachable, not just the profile's. **Defaults to `profile.learningLanguage`**, and the screen **WAITS for that doc** (`profile.loading` → a spinner, `data-testid="LifeStart-ProfileLoading"`) rather than rendering a guess. The gate covers the WHOLE form, not just the row: a seed chip tapped while the doc was in flight created a wurld in the hard-coded `'French'` fallback, so gating the row alone would have left the write race open. `useDocSnapshot` serves a cached doc synchronously, so the spinner is only ever seen on a first visit. The override is additionally held as `null`-means-follow rather than seeded into `useState` — belt and braces, and it keeps the row tracking a profile that changes under it. The chosen language is what is written to the world doc AND what `resolveSystemLanguage` reads (`LIFE-LANG-7`). Still locked once onboarding completes (`LIFE-DATA-5`). |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-3** | Step 3: the **world-mode toggle** — a full-width two-segment control (`role="radiogroup"`, each segment `role="radio"` + `aria-checked`), rendered ABOVE the seed input. Segments: **Realistic** (`Building2` icon) and **Fantasy** (`Wand2` icon). **Both subtitles render at all times**, on the selected and unselected segment alike — the player is weighing two trade-offs against each other and a subtitle that only appears after choosing cannot help with that. Realistic is the default (`DEFAULT_WORLD_MODE`). The chosen mode is **persisted on the world doc as `worldMode`** for EVERY seed, not just free-text ones, and is rendered into the saga + bio prompts. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-4** | The built-in seed examples (emoji + label + 1-line hint) render **ABOVE the free-text input**, directly under the mode toggle, headed "*Start from an example*", and are **filtered to the active mode** (`chipsForMode` in `screens/LifeScreen/worldModes.ts`). The membership of the two rows is `LIFE-ONBOARD-12` (realistic) and `LIFE-ONBOARD-13` (fantasy) — this row deliberately no longer enumerates them, because it went stale twice. **🎲 Surprise me appears in BOTH** — it is the one chip whose key carries no register of its own, which is precisely why `worldMode` must be persisted rather than derived from the seed key. Each chip maps to a world-seed key the step engine knows. **The order is the third arrangement of these two sections and the reasoning is recorded so it stops flipping** (owner, 2026-07-31): *"Let's put the example wurld cards above the free text input again so the user has to scroll past them."* Original design: chips first. Redesign: box first, to make the player's own idea the primary path — which left the chips reachable only by a scroll that OVERSHOT the box (`LIFE-ONBOARD-11`). Now: chips first again, because discovery-by-overshoot is a hope, whereas discovery-on-the-way-down is structural. The box is still the last thing the player reaches, so it is still the destination; the chips are simply no longer skippable. Asserted as DOM order (chips precede the textarea; the textarea's `<section>` is the LAST one), not as "both render" — both rendered under every arrangement. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-5** | The free-text seed is a **permanently visible** textarea (**≥ 6 rows** — see `LIFE-ONBOARD-11`, max 500 chars) sitting **LAST, below the examples** and headed "*Or write your own*", with a Begin CTA disabled until it has content — NOT a disclosure the player has to open. **The "or" belongs to whichever heading comes SECOND** and moves with the order: while the box led, the chips were "*Or* start from an example"; read above the box that phrasing offered an alternative to something the player had not been shown yet, so the "or" moved onto the free-text heading with the reorder (`LIFE-ONBOARD-4`). Its placeholder is a **worked example** naming the three things a usable seed carries — a person, a situation, and a source of drama — and is **tailored per mode** (`life.onboard.seed.manual.placeholder.{realistic,fantasy}`) so the example matches the register the toggle just set. The realistic one deliberately **spans several situations in different registers** rather than one scene — a conference talk, questions from the floor, finding your way around the venue, the drinks evening afterwards (owner, 2026-07-31) — because a single-scene example teaches a single-scene seed and leaves the saga generator less to pull on. Both placeholders open `e.g. ` (asserted in unit + E2E). Manual seeds bypass the chip-key lookup and are passed to the step generator verbatim as the world seed; the mode travels alongside as `worldMode`, never mixed into the player's text. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-6** | Tapping a chip OR submitting a manual seed: writes the `lifeWorld` doc with placeholder fields, kicks off the first-scene + first-beat generation in parallel, navigates to `/life`, and shows the "Building your wurld…" placeholder (`LIFE-BACKDROP-7`) until the first beat arrives. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-9** | **The header owns the safe area and sits OUTSIDE the scroll container** (matching `LifeOverviewScreen`), with `pt-safe-top` on the `<header>` and the scroller carrying only `pb-keyboard`. It was previously `sticky top-0` INSIDE a scroller that carried `pt-safe-top`: sticky offsets resolve against the scrollport, so the header parked BELOW that padding while scrolled content ran up THROUGH it — the subtitle rendered over the status bar with the header underneath (owner screenshot, 2026-07-31). Taking the header out of the scroller makes the strip structurally unreachable rather than merely covered by a matching background. Asserted structurally (`pt-safe-top` is on the header, not the scroller; the header is not a descendant of it) — a jsdom render has no layout to measure and "the header still renders" passes against the bug. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-10** | **`COMP-KB-22` — the free-text seed is lifted clear of the keyboard by scrolling ITS OWN container.** It sits low in a long form, so on a phone the keyboard opens over the very box the player was asked to type into. `.pb-keyboard` on the scroller makes the room; this moves the field into it. The full rule — container-only, instant, minimum-distance, no-op once clear, triggered by the SETTLED `--kb-h` rather than a bare timer — is `COMP-KB-22` in [composer.md](./composer.md), which also records why the previous deferred `scrollIntoView({block:'center',behavior:'smooth'})` made the field **jitter** when the keyboard's top edge bisected it (owner, 2026-07-31). |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-11** | **The form is spacious ON PURPOSE and does not fit on one screen ON PURPOSE.** Every section carries at least `mb-8`, and the free-text seed is at least 6 rows. This is the reverse of an earlier pass that tightened the sections to `mb-5` and cut the textarea to 3 rows chasing more of the form above the fold — which read as crammed (owner, 2026-07-31): *"Forget about trying to get the example world cards above the fold. It makes the interface too crammed."* The reasoning is that the player must scroll to reach the seed box regardless, so the scroll is a given and is better spent than fought — **fitting everything onto a cramped first screen buys nothing that the scroll does not already deliver.** There is deliberately **no "scroll down" affordance**; the spaciousness is the mechanism. Asserted as a FLOOR (`mb-N` with N ≥ 8 on every `<section>`, `rows` ≥ 6), so the page may be opened up further without editing a test but cannot be quietly closed back down. **This survived the `LIFE-ONBOARD-4` reorder unchanged, but what the scroll is FOR changed with it**: while the box led, the scroll had to *overshoot* the box for the chips to be seen at all, which is a hope rather than a mechanism. With the chips above the box the same scroll passes through them by construction — so the spacing is now what guarantees that pass-through, and tightening it would undo `LIFE-ONBOARD-4` as well as this row. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-7** | The world seed picker is the ONLY required step. Skipping name → use display name. Skipping language confirm → use profile language. Pre-filled defaults are valid choices. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-12** | **The realistic row is named for the REASON someone is learning, not for a place.** Membership: 🏙️ `city_life` · 🎓 `studying_abroad` · 🌾 `country_life` · ✈️ `just_moved_here` · 💼 `work_secondment` · 🌳 `ancestral_roots` · 🎒 `backpacker_hostels` · 🫖 `retired_village` · 🎲 `surprise` (LAST, always). The original four all name a PLACE; the four added 2026-08-01 name a situation a learner arrives with already (owner: *"a few chucked in that people might genuinely want to practice for — work secondment abroad, visiting your families ancestral roots, young backpacker hopping through hostels, university term abroad, retired to a small village"*). The four originals are kept, not replaced: this widens the row. Asserted on `chipsForMode('realistic')` keys + `surprise` being last. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-13** | **The fantasy row carries eight distinct WORLDS, not four genres.** Membership: 🪄 `magic_apprentice` · 🎭 `wandering_bard` · 🚀 `frontier_colony` · 🕵️ `noir_detective` · 🌊 `drowned_court` · ⛵ `sea_voyage` · 🚂 `last_train` · 🕯️ `saints_relic` · 🎲 `surprise` (LAST). The four added 2026-08-01 exist because four genre labels gave the saga generator a *category* and no *place* (owner: *"fantasy needs more examples too, and more creative"*). `sea_voyage` replaced a `cartographers_guild` seed that read as an occupation rather than a world (owner: *"What on earth is the map makers. That sounds terrible too. What about a sea voyage with mutiny etc"*) — a ship at sea is confined, hierarchical and long, so mutiny, superstition and landfall arise from the setting rather than having to be seeded as plot. **Renaming a seed key is a schema change**: `ZWorldSeedKey` in [`src/types/worldSeedKeys.ts`](../../src/types/worldSeedKeys.ts) is the source of truth, and the exhaustive `Record<WorldSeedKey, …>` maps in `backdropDescriptorForLife.ts` (vibe) and `WorldCard.tsx` (label) fail to COMPILE if a key is added or renamed without them — that compile error is the enforcement, not a test. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-14** | **A chip hint describes the UNIVERSE, not the storyline.** One line of the world's furniture — what is around you, what the days are made of, what the register is — and no plot. Explicitly NOT: a hook, a reveal, a secret, an antagonist, or anything that has already happened to "you". Reported 2026-08-01 after the first pass went the other way (owner: *"Descriptions are still rubbish for most Wurlds. Backpacking sounds like someone's stalking you which is horrible. Retirement has lost its peaceful whimsy. Most give tired cliche hooks. These descriptions should actually set the universe, not the storyline."*). **Why the universe and not the hook:** a hint is read at the moment of CHOOSING, so its job is to let the player picture living there — a hook picks the story for them, and a hook in nine chips is nine cliffhangers competing at once. It also loses on its own terms: `backpacker_hostels` reading *"someone from the first hostel who keeps turning up in the next town"* is a stalking premise, and `retired_village` reading *"the village … will not discuss the family who left it"* traded the whole appeal of the seed (slowness, warmth) for menace. The drama is generated downstream anyway — `worldSaga` gets the same universe brief and invents the plot from it, which is where variety between playthroughs comes from; a hint that names the plot pre-empts that for every player who picks the chip. **Asserted mechanically** in `seedCopy.test.ts`: every chip key has a non-empty label + hint in `en.ts`; hints are ≤ 90 chars; and no hint matches the reported plot-hook shapes (a second-person reveal, another character acting on the player, a secret/lie/forgery, a change already underway). The old copy is kept in the test as negative fixtures, so the assertions are known to fail on the thing they exist to prevent — that fixture set is what surfaced the fourth shape, which the first three checks passed clean. Applies to the saga briefs in [`worldSaga.ts`](../../src/lib/life/prompts/worldSaga.ts) too — those describe the world's texture and register, and leave the story to the generator. |

### Design decisions — the 2026-07-31 seed-picker redesign

**What it replaced.** A flat nine-chip grid, a standalone "realism nudge" floating above it, and "Write your own" tucked underneath as a closed disclosure. The two things that actually shape a wurld — which register, and the player's own idea — were the two least prominent things on the screen.

**The realism nudge is gone as a standalone element.** Its content now rides on the Realistic segment's subtitle. The old nudge argued for realism *above a grid the player had not been asked to categorise*, so it was advocacy with nothing to attach to; worse, it only ever made the case for one side. Putting the trade-off on the segments states both cases at the moment of choice — Realistic sells transfer to real situations, Fantasy sells the pull that gets you back tomorrow — which is the actual decision. The old `life.onboard.seed.realism` key and the `LifeStart-RealismHint` testid are retired.

**`worldMode` is persisted rather than derived.** Seven of the nine chip keys imply their own register, so deriving would have worked for them — but not for the two that matter most. `surprise` has to know which register to roll in, and `manual` is pure free text whose register is otherwise invisible. Storing the field for every seed keeps the prompt's read uniform and costs one string. It is **optional in the schema**: worlds created before this date have no mode, and both prompts explicitly instruct the model to fall back to inferring register from the seed key when the attribute is absent.

**The mode reaches generation via the saga and bio prompts only.** `worldArc`, `sceneGenerate`, `sceneReplan` and `step` all read the saga, which already encodes the register — threading the attribute into all six would have been redundant tokens in four stable prefixes for no added signal.

**The mode is never mixed into the player's text.** `LIFE-ONBOARD-5` keeps free-text seeds verbatim; the mode travels beside them as its own attribute. Prefixing "In a fantasy setting: …" onto the player's own words would have been cheaper and is exactly the kind of silent rewriting that makes a seed stop reading like the thing the player wrote.

---

## 4. LLM context structure — `LIFE-CTX-*`

Cache-friendliness is load-bearing. Gemini's prompt cache works on prefix match — anything stable at the TOP of the prompt stays cached across turns. Anything volatile at the BOTTOM invalidates only itself. The context assembler obeys this ordering rigidly.

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-CTX-1** | The full LLM prompt for every `lifeStep` call is assembled in this exact order (most-stable to most-volatile): (1) system instruction, (2) world seed + character sheet + `<saga>`, (2.5) `<canon>`, (3) NPC roster (sorted by id, **identity only** — see `LIFE-CTX-13`), (3.5) `<recurringNpcs>`, (4) scene index, (4.5) active scene outline (idle marker when none), (5) rolling summary, (5.2) `<npcMemory>` — per-NPC one-line memory, rare-volatile, split out of the roster row, (5.5) `<currentState>` — VOLATILE world-state (current location + goals), (5.9) `<storySoFar>` — the append-only chapter spine, (6) recent events (oldest→newest, **render-capped to last 20** — `STEP_EVENT_WINDOW`), (7) the user's current input. Anything from (1)–(4.5) only changes on chapter transitions, scene lifecycle changes, or character-sheet edits, so the prefix stays cached across most turns. **`currentLocation` + `goals` live at (5.5), NOT in the section-(2) stable prefix** (latency/data audits): they change every few nodes (a scene move) or on any `worldDelta`, and keeping them up top evicted the roster + scene + summary from Gemini's prefix cache on ~20–30% of turns. `<storySoFar>` and `<npcMemory>` were likewise MOVED out of the stable prefix into the volatile tail (`LIFE-CTX-13`). |
| **LIFE-CTX-9** | **No per-turn counter may render above `<summary>`.** `turnsSinceReplan` rode on the `<scene>` tag at §4.5 and increments on EVERY live turn, invalidating the prefix from that block down — the outline AND the summary — on every single call. It and `currentPhase` now render in the volatile `<currentState>` block as `<scenePhase/>` (renamed from `<sceneProgress/>` in 2026-08, when `LIFE-SCENE-25` claimed that name for the model's own completeness estimate — two different quantities under one tag would have been unreadable to the model and to us). Relatedly the NPC roster is now **id-sorted** as `LIFE-CTX-5` always required: the code never sorted, so a new NPC landed mid-list in Firestore doc order and shifted every later byte, evicting the up-to-2k-token flashcard block below it. |
| **LIFE-CTX-10** | **History renders what actually happened.** (a) A `beat` event carries `npcId`, binding the line to its `<npcRoster>` row — rendering the display NAME alone forced the model to re-match speakers by name across turns, which is the attribution drift `LIFE-CANON-4` also guards. (b) Beat text falls back to `nativeText` when `practiceText` is empty: narrator beats authored under the pre-2026-07 two-channel model (`LIFE-LANG-8`) rendered as EMPTY elements, so the model could not see that narration had happened at all. (c) `decision` events carry `actor="player"` — the label is a system-language menu string, so without it the model read the player's own choices as narration. |
| **LIFE-CTX-11** | **A graded exchange does NOT enter the narrative history.** `correction` events render as the empty string in `<recentEvents>`. What happened in-world — the player saying the completed line, or the NPC's utterance being revealed — is already committed as a `beat` with the correct speaker; the correction row echoed the same moment a second time in a different shape with no speaker attached, which is how a graded answer resurfaced later as different text attributed to the wrong person. Tested targets still reach the model via `<recentlyPracticed>`, and the raw events still drive anti-reuse, card-match sourcing and SRS credit — this drops the row from the PROMPT only. |
| **LIFE-CTX-13** | **Every-turn-mutating blocks render BELOW the semi-stable prefix; NPC identity is decoupled from NPC memory.** Two blocks used to sit in the stable prefix while changing on (nearly) every turn, evicting the roster + scene from Gemini's prefix cache on almost every call: (a) `<storySoFar>` (the append-only chapter spine) grew by one `<happened>` line each turn — MOVED from its old §2.6 position (above the roster) into the volatile tail, just above `<recentEvents>`; (b) each per-NPC `oneLineMemory` was embedded INSIDE its `<npcRoster>` row, so a single memory change (~1-in-5 turns) rewrote the whole otherwise-byte-stable roster block. Roster rows are now **identity-only self-closing** `<npc id name scope recurring? relationship?/>`, and the memory renders in a separate `<npcMemory npcId="…">…</npcMemory>` block (`LIFE-CTX-10` id-binding) placed at the top of the volatile tail — above `<currentState>` so it stays cached on the ~4/5 turns it doesn't change, below the roster so a memory change never touches the prefix. Additionally, `<recentEvents>` renders only the last `STEP_EVENT_WINDOW` (= 20) events (`slice(-20)`); the caller loads a wider window purely to feed the derivations (`recentlyPracticedTargets` / `cyoaStreak` / `recentFillBlankTargets`), which still read the full `input.events`. Older verbatim history is redundant with the terse `<storySoFar>` spine. |
| **LIFE-CTX-19** | **The `<npcRoster>` is BOUNDED at `STEP_ROSTER_CAP` (48), and the bound RETAINS the rows the rest of the prompt points at.** The roster used to render `input.npcs` whole — no slice, no cap — under a comment claiming *"Filter is applied by the caller (LifeScreen) so we just render what was passed"*. **There was no such filter**: `useLifeNpcs` subscribes to the entire `npcs` subcollection with no `limit()` and no `where()`, and `npcSpec` maps every row. The roster grew monotonically for the life of a world. ⚠ **Batch 8 is what made it bite.** Moving `life_step` onto the BUILT path caps `prompt + systemInstruction` as a SUM, where the legacy path capped them SEPARATELY — `SRV-PROMPT-9`'s *"migrating a category halves its byte budget"*, landing on the app's most failure-sensitive call. Measured: the roster crossed `MAX_PROMPT_BYTES` at ~81 rows against `LIFE-CAST-3`'s ~30 by chapter five, and a 400-npc world was REJECTED `too-large` at 80,322 B — which `SRV-PROMPT-10` turns into the player's hard loop, strictly worse than `LIFE-WORLD-ARC-7`'s silent `trivialArc`. ⚠ **A prefix `slice` would have been wrong**, because `LIFE-CTX-10`(a) binds a `beat` to its roster row by `npcId` so the model never re-matches a speaker by NAME. The cap therefore retains (i) ids named by the RENDERED event window, (ii) `recurringNpcIds`, and (iii) the current scene's cast, then fills the remainder. That set is bounded by construction — `<recentEvents>` renders only `STEP_EVENT_WINDOW` (20) events, so history can name at most ~20 — which is what makes the cap safe rather than merely smaller. ⚠ **The roster is now the SOURCE OF TRUTH for who exists, and `<npcMemory>` derives from it.** The first fix left the cap doing nothing: the keep-set was seeded from `canonWorkingSetIds`, which is itself seeded with *every world-scope npc*, so "retain everything referenced" retained all of them. That also meant `<npcMemory>` — which carries PROSE where the roster carries identity — was unbounded by the same set. Pinning the canon working set from the CAPPED roster bounds both blocks and makes it impossible to render a memory for someone with no row to bind it to. A roster at or under the cap is returned by identity, so the common path stays byte-identical. |
| **LIFE-CTX-12** | **Every event the SI tells the model to read must actually be written.** `backdrop_visited` is written by `commitNode` on each scene change; it had NEVER been emitted by any code path even though the SI instructs the model to check the most recent one to decide whether `backdropIntent` is required. Likewise the SI referenced `<event kind="hook_declined">` while the renderer emits `scene_hook_declined`, so the 5-turn no-re-offer rule could never fire. An SI↔renderer tag mismatch is a silent dead rule; adding an event kind means adding its writer, its renderer, and its SI reference together. |
| **LIFE-CTX-14** | **The implicit prefix cache is granted in ~4,090-token BLOCKS, and the last ~3-4k tokens of any prompt are never cached.** Measured against real Gemini 2026-07 (`docs/archive/model-migration-2026-07.md` §28.3): a **byte-identical** repeat of a 16,388-token prompt caches 12,262 tokens — 74.8%, not 100%. Nothing caches at all below ~7.3k total input. Three consequences that govern all future prompt work: **(a)** the cache boundary currently stops INSIDE the system instruction, so every byte of the `LIFE-CTX-1` stable-prefix ordering is billed at the full uncached rate — that ordering is correct and costs nothing to keep, but it is not buying a discount today and no amount of further reordering will change that (`LIFE-CTX-1`/`-13` are at ~99.6% of the achievable cache already). **(b)** A token trimmed from the uncached TAIL (`<recentEvents>`, `<practiceCadence>`, `<storySoFar>`) is worth ~10× one trimmed from the cached head of the SI. **(c) Trimming is NOT monotonic** — shrinking a prompt can cost a whole 4,090-token block and make the call DEARER. Observed, not theorised: the D1/D2 output trim shrank the SI by ~1,500 chars and cached tokens fell 12,210 → 11,338 while billed-in rose 3,636 → 4,334. **Any prompt-size change must be re-measured with `tests/experiments/life-cost/`, never assumed.** Explicit context caching does not rescue this: it would move only the ~1,100 SI tokens the implicit cache misses (~$0.00026/call) against $1.00/1M-tokens/hour storage, and the `life_step` SI is parameterised on `(practiceLanguage, nativeLanguage, systemLanguage, difficulty)` so the caches fragment per language-pair × 7 levels and never reach break-even. The SCENE SI is the opposite case and IS worth caching explicitly — it is a byte-constant global, so one cache serves every user (`LIFE-BACKDROP-*`). **The "never reach break-even" conclusion in this row is SUPERSEDED — read `LIFE-CTX-18` before acting on it.** It was computed against a 13,345-token SI where implicit caching already covered more than the invariant part; the SI is now ~18,031 tokens, the boundary stops inside it, and the implicit cache turns out not to survive a normal gap between turns. |
| **LIFE-CTX-2** | Within "recent events" the order is **strictly chronological by `createdAt`**. New flashcards added between sessions are appended as `flashcard_added` events on session resume (`LIFE-FLOW-2`) — they land at the BOTTOM of the recent-events block, making the freshest vocabulary the closest to the model's attention. |
| **LIFE-CTX-3** | The system instruction is parameterised on `(practiceLanguage, nativeLanguage, systemLanguage, difficulty)` but otherwise static. It tells the model: (a) the response schema, (b) the encouragement-not-mandate to use saved vocabulary, (c) the wrong-answer narrative-correction rules, (d) the transient/persistent flag contract for new NPCs and scenes, (e) the **two-channel output requirement** (`LIFE-LANG-8`): narration goes in `nativeText` in the SYSTEM language (unconstrained); npc/player dialogue goes in `practiceText` in the practice language WITH a system-language gloss in `nativeText` + rich `complexWords`/`wordTranslations` (same shape `MSG-VOCAB-*` uses), (f) **difficulty (1-7) calibrates the DIALOGUE + graded questions, not the narration and not the story** (`LIFE-LANG-9` — narration is unconstrained/unleveled) — concrete per-level guidance (short high-frequency sentences + mostly present/simple past at low levels → subordinate clauses, richer vocabulary and the full tense/mood range at high) with an explicit **bias toward TOO SIMPLE** at the low end (level 1-2: ~3-8-word sentences, present tense, most-common words, one idea per sentence, no subordinate clauses, key words reused, most words glossed) anchored by a worked level-1 example. The **floor** only stops word-salad / robotic filler / translationese — it must NOT add words or clauses to sound sophisticated; at level 1 an easy scene done well is the goal. **A wurld FOLLOWS the live global `profile.difficulty` by default** (default 2 — the beginner band): the effective level is resolved per generation as `difficultyPinned ?? profile.difficulty ?? 2` (`resolveWurldDifficulty`), so changing the level in Settings applies to all future beats + graded questions of every unpinned wurld, mid-story. An optional per-wurld **pin** (`ZLifeWorld.difficultyPinned`, edited from the ⋯ menu — `LIFE-SETTINGS-7`) freezes a wurld to a fixed 1-7 level. The legacy `difficulty` field is now a dormant world-creation snapshot, no longer the generation source; existing wurlds with no pin therefore follow global. Resolution happens ONCE at the LifeScreen props boundary (`useEffectiveWorld` overrides `world.difficulty` before it reaches the node engine), so `generateStepOptions`, `buildGradedSceneContext`, and `runSceneGenerate` consume the effective value with no change to those readers, and world-delta writes never re-freeze the raw snapshot. The **graded-question generator's** SI (`gradedQuestionSystemInstruction`) is likewise parameterised on difficulty AND `systemLanguage` — both ride in the graded scene context (`buildGradedSceneContext` → `GradedQuestionSceneContext.difficulty` + `.systemLanguage`, default 2 / `nativeLanguage` for legacy world docs) so the tested target + distractors match the level and the instruction/interpretation language is the system language (`LIFE-LANG-8`). |
| **LIFE-CTX-4** | The character sheet block is rendered as a stable XML/structured block so its byte-shape doesn't drift between turns even when fields update. Example: `<character name="Marc" age="32" traits="curious;impulsive" city="Lyon"/>`. Adding a trait re-renders this block and invalidates the cache from that block down — acceptable because trait changes are rare. |
| **LIFE-CTX-15** | **`life_step` does NOT send a `responseSchema`, and the response shape stays in the SI as prose.** Tried and rejected on measurement, 2026-07-29 (`docs/archive/model-migration-2026-07.md` §28.7). The motivation was sound — a `responseSchema` costs **0 input tokens** (probed: `promptTokenCount` identical with and without a 1,404-char schema attached), and the SI's `OUTPUT SCHEMA (enforced)` block sits PAST the prefix-cache boundary, so deleting it would have saved the full uncached rate on ~1,433 tokens, ~$0.000428/call, ~20% of `life_step`. A faithful schema was expressible: `anyOf`, `minItems`/`maxItems`, `nullable` and free-form maps all probed as supported. **It broke the contract anyway, and the cause is the schema itself — the same three failures appeared with the SI trimmed AND with the original SI completely intact:** (a) **runaway generation** — one call in four exceeded 58,000 output tokens and returned truncated invalid JSON, ~$0.15 for a single turn, 72× a normal one; a tail risk that large is disqualifying on its own; (b) **optional nested objects stopped being emitted** — under structured-output mode the model fills `required` fields and skips the rest, so `saysLine` was absent on every `say` option in every run, failing `LIFE-SPEECH-1` and taking the retry rate from 0% to 100% (the exact thing the change was meant to prevent); (c) **narrative output halved** (645 → ~295 tokens) — structured-output mode trades prose quality for shape compliance, which is the wrong trade on the app's core narrative call at any price. (b) is fixable by encoding the say-variant as an `anyOf` on the option; (a) and (c) are not, and (a) alone closes it. **Re-open only with evidence that Gemini's structured-output mode no longer runs away on `anyOf` and no longer suppresses optional nested objects — and then judge output QUALITY before cost.** The saving itself is still on the table via a different route: compressing the block's notation rather than moving the shape onto the wire (untested, and subject to `LIFE-CTX-14(c)` — the rejected attempt shrank the SI to 49,951 chars and landed on a WORSE cache boundary than the baseline, `cached` 12,212 → 10,421 and `billed-in` 3,881 → 4,931). |
| **LIFE-CTX-16** | **Three event rows do not render into `<recentEvents>`, because the model already has their content elsewhere.** Same mechanism as `LIFE-CTX-11`'s `correction` rows — the raw events stay in Firestore and still drive every derivation; only the PROMPT row goes. (a) **`npc_introduced`** is redundant with `<canon>` and `<npcRoster>`, both of which carry that NPC's id and name authoritatively on every turn (`LIFE-CANON-5` keeps id + name even for entities long out of play). (b) **`flashcard_added`** restated a word the model had already seen as the beat it was tapped in, with its own `wordTranslations`, and again via `<recentlyPracticed>` once tested; `vocabRecap` reads the raw events so the recap surface is untouched. (c) The **`choice="…"` slug** on a `decision` row was an internal option id (`"examine-copper"`) that the SI references nowhere — the label text is what tells the model what the player did. **Sizing, stated honestly: ~$0.000008/call.** This is a rounding error, not a saving. It was investigated on the hypothesis that the 20-event window carried junk worth gating — and the measurement refuted that: across the captured run every `decision` row was a substantive player action ("Keep the secret to yourself"), `beat` rows are 59% of the block and irreducible, and roughly half of what remains is XML scaffolding shared by all rows. **The event window is already information-dense; there is no fat left in it.** Getting more would mean a terser tag vocabulary across every event kind, which the SI references by name in several rules (`scene_hook_declined`, `backdrop_visited`) — a prompt-suite-sized change for a small return. Recorded so the "trim the event window" idea is not re-proposed as a cost lever. The real bound on this block is that it is **already O(1)**: capped at `STEP_EVENT_WINDOW`, it plateaus at ~2,420 chars around node 12 and never grows again (`LIFE-CANON-5` has the section-by-section growth table). |
| **LIFE-CTX-17** | **The implicit cache DOES serve `life_step`, and WHERE the constant block sits makes no difference.** Two contradictory claims were live and neither had been measured against the current SI: `explicitSiCache.ts` implied `life_step` gets nothing, and the v2 engine branch attributed its cost advantage to sending everything as append-only `contents:` instead of `systemInstruction:`. Probed 2026-08-09 on `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` with real archived turns (`tests/playthrough/implicitCache.probe.ts`), three arms differing ONLY in placement of the same 74,140 characters: **A** `systemInstruction:` (what we ship), **B** `contents:[user SI, model ack, user prompt]` (the v2 shape), **C** `contents:[user SI + prompt]` as one turn. All three cached **16,305–16,312 tokens — a spread of 7 tokens, 0.03%** — steady-state 75.6% on every arm. **There is no request-shape win to port; the v2 cost story has to be attributed to something else** (fewer/smaller calls, not placement). Sizing for anything that touches this block: `life_step` is **88% of all life-sim spend** (95 of 158 calls in a three-world run; 22,240 in / 694 out, 32:1 — an INPUT problem), and regression across those 95 calls puts the fixed part at `inTokens ≈ 18,031 + 0.2636 × promptChars` (n=95, prompts spanning 10.6k–19k chars), i.e. **81% of every request is byte-identical to the one before it**. The SI is per-world constant — 3 distinct SIs across 95 calls, one per `(practiceLanguage, nativeLanguage, systemLanguage, difficulty)` (`LIFE-CTX-3`). |
| **LIFE-CTX-18** | **The implicit cache does NOT survive a normal gap between turns — it is gone somewhere between 30s and 120s.** The 75.6% of `LIFE-CTX-17` is an UPPER bound measured back-to-back, not what play banks. Escalating-ladder probe, each call re-warming for the next (`tests/playthrough/implicitCache.probe.ts`): **+30s → 16,307 cached (75.9%); +120s → 0 cached (0.0%)**. Google documents implicit caching as automatic and best-effort and does not publish a retention window, so this is an observed number that could move under them — re-measure before relying on it. **Consequences.** (a) Per-turn input cost is bimodal, not average: **7,565 billed-equivalent tokens on a fast turn vs 22,240 on a cold one**, a 2.9× spread decided purely by how long the player took. (b) This RE-OPENS `docs/archive/model-migration-2026-07.md` §28.3.2, which closed explicit SI caching for `life_step` and named its own two re-open triggers — *"the SI grows well past 4 cache blocks"* and *"the observed cold-miss rate rises far above 7%"*. **Both have fired:** the SI is now ~18,031 tokens against 13,345 when that was written (so the implicit boundary stops INSIDE it at ~16.3k, stranding ~1,700 SI tokens even on a hit), and the 7% figure was random cold-start noise whereas the real driver turns out to be inter-turn timing. (c) The arithmetic at today's size: an explicit cache covering the whole SI bills **6,012 tok/turn flat**, beating both the fast implicit turn (7,565) and the cold one (22,240), against 18,031 tok × $1.00/1M/hour = **$0.018/hour of storage per `(language-pair × difficulty × model)` variant**. At one active player per variant (~30 turns/hour) it **wins once more than ~30% of turns currently miss**. **The gating number is the production cold-miss rate, which is already being recorded** — `recordTokenUsage` writes `life_step.cached` daily per user (`LIFE-COST-*`), so this is a dashboard read, not a build. Do not implement explicit `life_step` caching before reading it. |
| **LIFE-CTX-5** | The NPC roster + scene index render in **id-sorted order**, never insertion order, so newly-promoted entities slot deterministically rather than appending and shifting the byte offsets of every later entity. Roster rows are **identity-only self-closing** (`LIFE-CTX-13` — memory lives in `<npcMemory>`): `<npc id="n1" name="Marie" scope="world" relationship="fruit-seller"/>`; the line "She remembers you bought mangoes last week." renders separately as `<npcMemory npcId="n1">…</npcMemory>` in the volatile tail. |
| **LIFE-CTX-6** | Events use a typed XML/structured shape so the model can distinguish a `flashcard_added` event ("the user has this word available now") from a `decision` event ("the user chose to take the metro"). All events carry `createdAt` so the model can reason about elapsed in-world time. |
| ~~**LIFE-CTX-7**~~ (RETIRED, WS1-C) | Described compaction rewriting the oldest events into `rollingSummary` as "the only time the prefix cache fully invalidates". Compaction is deleted (`LIFE-MEM-1`); there is no longer any wholesale prefix invalidation on the turn loop. Memory is append-only (`storyLog`/`canon`) so the prefix only ever EXTENDS. |
| **LIFE-CTX-8** | New flashcards added BETWEEN sessions are NOT inserted into the existing event log retroactively — they're appended as `flashcard_added` events on session start with the actual session-start `createdAt`. The model sees them as "the user just learned X" memories, not historical edits. |

---

## 4.5 Narrative hierarchy + the canon — `LIFE-UNIVERSE-*` / `LIFE-CANON-*` / `LIFE-STORY-LOG-*`

Story-consistency defects (referencing un-introduced entities, logically-invalid
options, forgetting established events, wrong-character attribution) were being
fought with SI prose. Every rule existed and none was enforced — `validateNodeResponse`
computed issues and only `log.warn`'d them, so the offending turn shipped anyway.
This section is the machinery that replaced the pleas with state and checks.

The layers, most-stable first — which is also their prompt order, because
stability IS cache order (`LIFE-CTX-1`):

| Layer | What it is | Lifetime |
|---|---|---|
| `<universe>` | the SETTING and its LAWS | authored once, never regenerated |
| `<saga>` | the through-line: conflict, twist, climax, endings | once per world |
| `<canon>` | every entity the story has established | append-only |
| `<storySoFar>` | what has happened this chapter | append-only |
| `<scene>` / `<summary>` | current arc + folded memory | per chapter / per compaction |
| `<currentState>` … `<turn>` | volatile tail | every turn |

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-UNIVERSE-1** | `ZWorldSaga.universe` = `{setting, era, laws[], places[], figures[]}` — the setting a saga happens IN (Middle-earth, not the story of the Ring). Authored in the SAME one-shot `worldSaga` call, so the extra hierarchy layer costs **no additional API round-trip**, and never regenerated — making it the most stable block in the prompt, rendered FIRST (`renderUniverse`, its own section above `<saga>`). **`laws[]` is the load-bearing field**: 3-6 standing rules in the present tense that constrain what a person can DO here ("the hush-spirits cannot speak aloud; they answer by moving objects"). A rule the model re-reads every single turn is far harder to contradict than one buried in a beat forty events ago; for ordinary contemporary settings the SI asks for ordinary social laws rather than invented fantasy. Absent on legacy sagas ⇒ no block rendered. |
| **LIFE-CANON-1** | `ZLifeWorld.canon` = `{characters[], places[], objects[], facts[]}`, each entry `{id, name, kind, note, canSpeak?, chapter?}`. **APPEND-ONLY in the normal case** — entries are never rewritten or reordered, so the rendered block extends and stays free in the prefix cache. The ONE exception is the `LIFE-CANON-5` cap: if a bucket ever exceeds `CANON_BUCKET_CAP` (48) the middle is dropped, which is a one-time byte-shift (a rare cache eviction) — a chapter almost never introduces 48+ distinct objects/facts, so this is a 1 MiB-ceiling backstop, not the normal path. Because a mute character (`canSpeak:false`) or a `targetId`-referenced entity could in principle be a dropped middle entry, the head+tail retention keeps the founding + most-recent entities; a dropped reference self-heals (an `unknown-entity-ref` retry). Characters DERIVE from the NPC roster and places from the current location (`resolveCanon`), costing zero output tokens; only objects, facts and non-standard characters are model-declared, via `introduces[]` (max 4) on the step response. A stored entry WINS the dedup over a derived one, so a recorded affordance is never overwritten by a plain roster row. Written by `applyTurnSignals` — turn-level like `worldDelta`, applied when the generating response lands (`LIFE-NODE-2/3`). Model-authored names/notes are XML-escaped. |
| **LIFE-CANON-5** | **Only canon entities IN PLAY carry their `note`; everything else renders self-closing.** `<canon>` is append-only and uncapped, and — because the implicit prefix-cache boundary stops inside the system instruction (`LIFE-CTX-14a`) — every byte of it is billed at the FULL uncached rate. Measured 2026-07-30: it is the largest unbounded term in the prompt body, ~15,900 chars at 120 entities and ~40,000 at 300, i.e. a world's prompt got permanently dearer the longer it was played. An entity is **in play** when (a) its `id` or `name` appears in the volatile context text (the last `STEP_EVENT_WINDOW` event payloads, `<storySoFar>`, current location, current goals), or (b) it is a world-scope (recurring) NPC, which are pinned because they carry the chapter. In-play entities render as before; the rest render `<entity id="…" name="…" kind="…" canSpeak="…"/>` — **`id`, `name`, `kind` and `canSpeak` are preserved for EVERY entity**, so every canon rule the SI states still holds universally: `targetId` validation (`LIFE-CANON-2`), the don't-name-what-doesn't-exist rule (`LIFE-INTRO-OPT`), the mute-entity affordance, and don't-re-declare. Only the descriptive `note` — useful solely while an entity is on stage — is withheld. The SI states explicitly that a self-closing entity is established exactly as much as a described one, so the model never reads a missing description as "unknown" and never re-introduces it. The same working set gates `<npcMemory>` (`LIFE-CTX-13`), whose per-NPC line has the identical profile: ~90 chars, useful only on stage. Matching is deliberately crude substring matching with a 3-char minimum name length: a false positive costs ~90 chars, a false negative costs one entity's description for one turn, and neither can break a contract. **Measured saving: 55% of the canon block** — $0.00065/call at 120 entities, $0.0016/call at 300. **Growth is slowed, not stopped** (~60 chars/cold entity instead of ~132). Getting to ~84% would need a compact `id=name` index block instead of self-closing rows; that is NOT done, deliberately — it splits canon across two tags while `LIFE-CANON-2` tells the model a `targetId` outside `<canon>` is rejected, so the model could stop targeting entities it can only see in the index even though the client-side validator would accept them. Revisit only with a prompt-suite run behind it. A hard CAP that drops entities entirely is also rejected: an evicted entity can be re-introduced as a duplicate, which breaks the dedupe `LIFE-CANON-1` exists for. |
| **LIFE-CANON-2** | Options carry `targetId` (a canon id). A `targetId` not in `<canon>` ∪ this response's `introduces` is an **unknown-entity-ref** violation — the machine-checkable form of `LIFE-INTRO-OPT` ("Show the book to Marc" offered before Marc exists). Exact and language-independent by design: a proper-noun heuristic over option prose would misfire on every noun in German. It **spends a retry** with the offending option in the hint (previously the validator only logged), arms only once the ledger is non-empty (a turn-one world cannot satisfy it), and the FINAL attempt always ships — the player is never stuck behind a validator (`LIFE-NODE-8`). In practice it almost never fires (real-Gemini measurement: 100% of options targeted real ids), which is what a safeguard should be. |
| **LIFE-CANON-3** | An entity recorded `canSpeak: false` (`canSpeak="no"` on its `<canon>` row) produces **no spoken words** — a hush-spirit that answers by moving objects, a corpse, a growling animal, someone sharing no language with the player. The player MAY still address it; that is a legitimate option and is **NOT rejected**. Its RESPONSE is narration describing what it does — a `narrator` beat, never an `npc` speech bubble. The SI states this, and `commitNode` enforces it deterministically: a beat whose `npcId` resolves to a `canSpeak:false` character is converted to a `narrator` beat (`muteEntityIds`), with no retry. **Why not reject the "speak" option?** The pre-2026-07 design rejected an option with `intent:'speak'` at a mute entity and spent a retry. Measuring it against real Gemini (`tests/experiments/narrative-machinery/mute-spirit.mjs`) showed the model already keeps such an entity mute — it answers non-verbally — so the rejection only suppressed a legitimate action and burned a retry on the majority of such turns, while the ACTUAL defect (the non-verbal action tagged as an `npc` bubble) went unaddressed. The `intent` enum was dropped along with the rejection: its only consumer was that check. |
| **LIFE-CANON-4** | Deterministic speaker attribution in `commitNode`, no model call and no cost. (a) A beat naming a KNOWN `npcId` with a drifted `speakerName` is rewritten from the roster — "the line was attributed to the wrong character / the character was renamed mid-story" becomes structurally impossible. (b) A beat with an UNKNOWN `npcId` whose `speakerName` already exists on the roster is re-bound to the established id — otherwise the model re-slugging a character mints a SECOND row for the same person, and the story then carries two Sophies whose memories and avatars diverge. |
| **LIFE-CANON-5** | The STORED canon is BOUNDED (`capCanon`, `CANON_BUCKET_CAP` = 48 per bucket, head+tail retention like `capStoryLog`) — it rides the stable prefix of every prompt and the world doc's 1 MiB ceiling, so it cannot grow without limit. Growth-detection runs on the PRE-cap lengths (a genuinely-new entry at the cap rolls content without changing length, and a length-only check silently dropped it). A `faction` introduction files under `facts` with a `grp-` slug prefix, so it no longer collides with a `fact` of the same name. |
| **LIFE-CANON-6** | Only model-DECLARED entities (via `introduces`) are PERSISTED in `world.canon`. Characters derive from the live NPC roster and the current location is derived from `currentLocationLabel` — both are merged in by `resolveCanon` at render/validate time and are NEVER written into the stored ledger. Baking them in (the prior behaviour) persisted retired NPCs forever and grew the doc with every scene move. |
| **LIFE-STORY-LOG-1** | `ZLifeWorld.storyLog` = `{chapterNumber, entries[]}`, one terse third-person `logLine` per response (~15 output tokens), rendered as `<storySoFar>`. **Append-only within a chapter**, reset at a chapter boundary. Because it only appends, everything above the newest line stays in the cached prefix — and since WS1-C moved it into the volatile tail (`LIFE-CTX-13`) it no longer evicts the roster/scene prefix each turn. |
| **LIFE-STORY-LOG-2** | **`storyLog` is the SOLE durable narrative spine** (WS1-C — `rollingSummary` compaction retired, `LIFE-MEM-1`). It is the long-range memory `recentEvents` cannot be (that window renders only the last 20) and that the deleted `rollingSummary` failed to be (wholesale-rewritten, lossy, and it never reset at chapter boundaries so it leaked stale prior-chapter prose forward). **Bounded** at `STORY_LOG_CAP` (raised 40→60 in WS1-C, reclaiming the deleted `<summary>` token budget) — a chapter is only tens of nodes, so the cap rarely bites, and 60 stays well under the old 80 (~4.8k tokens) the owner flagged. When it overflows, `capStoryLog` keeps the earliest `STORY_LOG_HEAD` (raised 6→8) founding lines AND the most recent, dropping the superseded MIDDLE — plain `.slice(-cap)` dropped the chapter's founding facts ("you were given the key"), the load-bearing ones. Cross-chapter carryover is NOT this log's job (it resets per chapter): it rides `world.bio`'s capped `<completedScene>` tail (`LIFE-SCENE-13`) + `currentArc.completedSceneMemories[]` + the archived `completedArcs/{chapter-N}` doc, all authored by the model's deliberate `keyMomentsForArc` distillation. |
| **LIFE-COMPACT-6** | The compactor is given `<universe>` + the saga's `centralConflict` + the current chapter premise. It previously saw only the character sheet, location and goals — it was asked to decide what mattered without being told what the story was ABOUT, so it preserved whatever looked locally interesting and quietly dropped the through-line. |

---

## 5. Step engine — `LIFE-STEP-*`

The orchestrator that drives every turn. One Cloud Function (`lifeStep`) per user action.

> **Superseded 2026-07 by the node/lookahead engine (§10.46, `LIFE-NODE-*`).** `LIFE-STEP-1/2/3/7/8` described the pre-node response shape (`beats + choices + graded`) and its retry/fallback mechanics — those are replaced by `LIFE-NODE-1/2/8` (`LIFE-NODE-5` is itself now retired — previews removed). **Still binding:** `LIFE-STEP-4` (every beat carries the full bilingual payload) and `LIFE-STEP-5`'s write ordering (events first, world deltas second — now enforced by `commitNode`, `LIFE-NODE-3`). `LIFE-STEP-6` (scene handoff) applies per-node via `node.backdropIntent`.

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-STEP-1** | A `lifeStep` call takes: `{ worldId, userInput, stepId }`. `userInput` is either a chip selection (`{ kind: 'choice', choiceId }`) or free-text (`{ kind: 'free', text }`). `stepId` is a client-minted nonce for idempotency (re-runs return the cached response). |
| **LIFE-STEP-2** | The Cloud Function fetches world state, assembles the prompt per `LIFE-CTX-*`, calls Gemini ONCE with structured output, and returns the response object below. No parallel calls per turn; scene gen fires AFTER the response lands, in the background (`LIFE-BACKDROP-*`). |
| **LIFE-STEP-3** | The structured response shape: `{ beats: Beat[], choices: Choice[], exercise?: Exercise, backdropIntent?: BackdropIntent, newNpcs?: NpcDelta[], newFacts?: WorldDelta }`. `beats` is 1-3 narrator/NPC lines (each line tagged with speakerId — `narrator` or an existing/new NPC id). `choices` is 2-4 chip options OR the literal `freeText: true` marker. `exercise` is set when one of the choices is graded (`LIFE-EX-*`). `backdropIntent` is set on scene change (`LIFE-BACKDROP-*`). `newNpcs` lists characters introduced this beat with their `transient: boolean` flag. `newFacts` is character/world deltas (new goal, changed location, etc.). |
| **LIFE-STEP-4** | EVERY beat in the response carries the full bilingual payload — practice text + native text + complexWords[] + wordTranslations{} — so `LIFE-BUBBLE-*` can render translation modes without a follow-up call. This mirrors `TUTOR-INV-3`'s single-call shortcut. |
| **LIFE-STEP-5** | After the response lands, the orchestrator writes the beats as `beat` events to `lifeEvents`, writes any `transient: false` NPCs to `lifeNpcs` (kicks off avatar gen for them, `LIFE-NPC-*`), and applies `newFacts` to `lifeWorld`. Order: write events FIRST, world deltas SECOND, kick off async asset gen LAST. Failures after the first write surface as a degraded-state toast but don't roll back the beat. |
| **LIFE-STEP-6** | If `backdropIntent` indicates a scene change, the orchestrator: (a) records the transport mode in `lifeEvents`, (b) flips UI to the transport loop (`LIFE-TRANS-*`), (c) resolves the scene from cache OR kicks off a fresh render (`LIFE-BACKDROP-*`), (d) flips to the new scene + first beat on the new scene WHEN BOTH the scene and the next-beat response are ready. The scene render does NOT block the bubble rendering once the scene is on-screen. |
| **LIFE-STEP-7** | The response schema is strict — schema violations cause a single retry with the schema attached as a system note, then a fallback narrator beat ("Something feels strange. Take a moment.") + the same choices as the previous turn. The world doesn't progress on permanent schema failure; the user can try again. |
| **LIFE-STEP-8** | `freeText: true` choices route to a different prompt template on the next call (one that explicitly grades grammatical correctness + accepts mixed-language input). The orchestrator never tries to grade free text client-side. |

---

## 6. Backdrop rendering — `LIFE-BACKDROP-*`

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-1** | A scene SVG is generated by a separate `generateBackdrop` Cloud Function call — distinct from `lifeStep`. Input: `{ descriptor, persistent, worldStyle }`. Output: SVG bytes uploaded to Storage + a Firestore doc in `lifeBackdrops/{backdropId}` for persistent scenes. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-2** | The cache key for a persistent scene is `{worldId}::{descriptor.label \|\| locationType::vibe}` — per-world (`LIFE-DATA-1`); `flavour` is deliberately excluded so cosmetic variants reuse one SVG. Returning to the same descriptor in the same world reuses the same SVG byte-for-byte. Different users → different SVGs even for "the corner café". **In-flight dedup:** `useLifeBackdrop` shares ONE Gemini render across concurrent triggers for the same key (a module-level in-flight promise map). Without it, React StrictMode's dev double-invoke of the effect fired the scene gen TWICE (2× tokens) on every cache MISS — the effect's `cancelled` flag drops only the late RESULT, never the already-fired network call. Production builds don't double-invoke, but the dedup also hardens against any rapid re-trigger. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-3** | ALL generated scenes cache — including transient ones. `descriptor.persistent` is a generation HINT (it shapes the SI: "keep it simple, atmospheric, pass-through" for transient) but it is NOT a cache gate. Rationale: refresh / resume in the middle of a transient scene must hit the cache, not re-burn a Gemini call. localStorage's 64-entry LRU handles bloat; Firestore scene docs are cheap. Earlier behaviour (transient scenes uncached) regressed resume and was reverted on 2026-06-01. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-4** | **ALL scene + NPC-avatar renders pin `thinkingLevel: 'minimal'`** — there is no persistent/transient thinking split. This invariant previously specified `'medium'` for persistent and `'minimal'` for transient, on the reasoning that "the persistent budget buys composition + reusable detail; transient is cheap because it won't be seen again". That split was never implemented (`generateBackdrop` has always sent one flat level for every descriptor) and, until `LIFE-COST-4` fixed the SDK plumbing, the level never reached the wire at all — every scene ran at flash-preview's DEFAULT dynamic thinking, ~790 unbudgeted reasoning tokens billed at the output rate. So the premise was never tested. It has been now: on the same prompt, `'minimal'` produced **0** thinking tokens with the visible SVG essentially unchanged in size (3,129 → 2,896 tokens) and no reviewable difference in composition, i.e. the reasoning was not buying the detail the old text assumed. Pinned minimal (`docs/archive/model-migration-2026-07.md` §28.3.5). The base branch's independent A/B is stronger evidence still and agrees: on `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` across three scenes, `'medium'` spent 2,365 thinking tokens (**59% of the bill**) and drew LESS for it — 25 drawing nodes vs 43 at `'minimal'`. Native thinking was worse on BOTH axes here, so this is not a quality/cost trade. **Revisit trigger:** a *visual* quality complaint about scene composition — not a cost review. Re-raising the level is a per-descriptor branch in `generateBackdrop`, and per CLAUDE.md it needs captured screenshots read side-by-side, not a token count. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-5** | The SVG-gen prompt instructs Gemini to: (a) emit a single `<svg viewBox="0 0 1200 800" preserveAspectRatio="xMidYMin meet">…</svg>` document whose FIRST painted element is a full-canvas `<rect class="bleed" … fill="#hex"/>` plate (`LIFE-BACKDROP-12`), (b) use `class="sky"`, `class="lighting"`, `class="streetlamp"` on atmospheric elements — these are dormant hooks (the day-night tint was removed; harmless to keep for a future revisit), (c) include simple `<animate>` / `<animateTransform>` declarations for atmospheric motion (drifting clouds, birds, swaying trees) where it makes sense, (d) never include `<script>` or external references. Output is sanity-checked against a strict allowlist before upload. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-6** | Time-of-day variants are CSS class layers applied to the rendered SVG container, NOT separate renders. `[data-time="morning"]`, `[data-time="midday"]`, `[data-time="evening"]`, `[data-time="night"]` styles target the tagged elements. If the LLM didn't tag elements reliably (failure mode), a translucent coloured overlay fallback can be applied to the whole stage — that fallback is noted as a SECONDARY path in the implementation and not the default. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-7** | "Building your wurld…" placeholder: a stylised silhouette of the scene's location category (apartment, café, market, fantasy academy, …) renders during gen. The category comes from the descriptor and selects from a small hand-drawn fallback library (8 categories minimum).
