The placeholder also covers the first-render-after-onboarding case (no cache yet). **Gen gate:** the silhouette shows WITHOUT generating until a REAL scene exists — a beat's `backdropIntent` or a persisted `currentBackdrop` (`useLifeBackdrop`'s `generate` param, driven by `LifeScreen`'s `canGenerateBackdrop`). A fresh world's descriptor is only a seed GUESS (`fallbackDescriptor`); rendering then discarding a Gemini scene for it (the first beat's real `backdropIntent` immediately replaces it) wasted a gen — this gate closes that. **Placeholder affordance:** the "Building your wurld…" pill (`data-testid="LifeScreen-BackdropGenStatus"` — spinner + `life.buildingWorld`) PLUS a `.life-scene-building` shimmer sheen over the silhouette are shown whenever no real scene exists yet — during an active render (`status==='generating'`) OR the fresh-world placeholder window (`backdropBuildingVisible`: `status==='fallback'` AND `!canGenerateBackdrop` AND zero events). Previously the pill was gated on `status==='generating'` ONLY, so it NEVER showed during the fresh-world silhouette window (status `'fallback'`) — the polished fallback SVG then read as a finished scene, not a placeholder (playtest bug). It is NOT shown for the LIFE-BACKDROP-8 terminal-failure silhouette (`canGenerateBackdrop===true`) or a resumed world momentarily lacking `currentBackdrop` (events > 0). **What the silhouette is painted ON is now narrowed by `LIFE-BACKDROP-15` (a scene already on screen is HELD instead — the silhouette never replaces it) and `LIFE-BACKDROP-16` (before any scene has ever rendered, the placeholder art is the planet, not a location silhouette).** The pill + shimmer gating in this invariant is unchanged; only the artwork underneath it moved. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-8** | The fallback library is also the failure path (`LIFE-FAIL-*`). If a persistent scene gen fails twice (retry policy), the orchestrator pivots to the fallback silhouette for that location category AND fills the silhouette with the user's avatar (so the user is at least looking at themselves). The descriptor is marked `failed: true` in `lifeBackdrops` so retries don't loop forever; the user can manually re-request from the character sheet. **Presentation carve-out (2026-07-31):** the ORCHESTRATOR still pivots exactly as described — `useLifeBackdrop` still resolves to `getFallbackBackdrop(pickFallback(descriptor))` — but the STAGE no longer necessarily paints it. Per `LIFE-BACKDROP-15` a scene already on screen is held instead (a failed regeneration must not throw the player back to a silhouette of a place they can already see), and per `LIFE-BACKDROP-16` a world that has never rendered a scene shows the planet placeholder instead of the category silhouette. The silhouette library therefore still backs `useWorldThumbnail` (`LIFE-WORLD-9`) and remains the orchestrator's terminal value, but on `BackdropStage` it is now an internal signal ("this is not a real scene") rather than something the player sees. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-9** | *(Removed 2026-07 — the day-night feature was never built. The sim-time (`lifeWorld.currentTime`) that was to drive the tint was never written or read; `BackdropStage` rendered a hardcoded `'morning'` into a `data-time` attribute that no CSS consumed. The `timeOfDay` prop + `data-time` attribute were deleted as dead plumbing. Revisit as a real feature — cheapest path is deriving from the player's local clock — if ambiance is wanted. Id retired; see git history.)* |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-14** | **The scene system instruction is served from an EXPLICIT Gemini context cache, shared across every user of the deployment.** Scene calls send a ~1,600-token constant SI with a ~500-char prompt — total input ~1,645 tokens, far below the ~7.3k floor at which the IMPLICIT prefix cache engages (`LIFE-CTX-14`), so before this they cached **0** tokens on every call. Mechanics (`functions/src/explicitSiCache.ts`, wired into `geminiGenerate`): eligibility is a server-side allowlist on the telemetry `category` (**`life_backdrop` only** — ⚠ this entry said `life_scene` until 2026-09-18, which is the PRE-RENAME name of today's `life_backdrop`; `LIFE-BACKDROP-22` three rows below records that rename and the removal of the `life_scene` SI-cache aliases, so the stale name here read as though today's scene-CLOSE category were cached, and it is not) — NOT "cache whatever SI the client sent", because the `life_step` SI is parameterised on four dimensions and would fragment into a cache per language-pair × difficulty. Keyed by `(model, SI digest)` in a bounded pool with cross-instance handoff through `system/backdropContextCache` (also corrected 2026-09-18 — the doc path was renamed with the category) (a warm instance ADOPTS via a metadata fetch rather than paying a second create), a 1h TTL with a 10min refresh margin, and a 15min negative memo so an SI Gemini refuses to cache costs one failed round-trip per window instead of one per call. **Caching is a pure optimisation and must never block a render:** every failure path — create rejected, adopt 404, publish denied, `getGenerativeModelFromCachedContent` throwing — returns null and the call proceeds with the SI inline, exactly as before. Also disabled under `GEMINI_STUB` so an emulator run cannot create billed caches. **Measured 2026-07-29** (10 interleaved calls, real Gemini, `gemini-3-flash-preview`): 1,600 of 1,645 input tokens served cached on every single cached-arm call → **$0.00054/call, 5.6%** of a mean-output scene, against $0.0016/hour storage → **break-even at ~3 scene calls/hour globally**. Output was unchanged (arm means 3,048 vs 2,885 tokens sit inside the 2,362–3,346 spread of the run) and rasterised SVGs were reviewed side-by-side with no quality difference — the saving is input-side only; do not claim the output delta. **Adding a category is a cost decision, not a cleanup:** the SI must interpolate nothing per-user and the category must clear ~3 calls/hour deployment-wide. `life_npc_avatar*` is deliberately excluded — constant SI, but roughly one render per NPC ever. **Regression signal:** `life_backdrop` cache% falling to 0 on the admin dashboard means creates are being refused (most likely the SI was trimmed below the model's minimum cacheable size). |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-10** | A user can request a regenerate from the character sheet for any persistent scene or NPC ("change my hair colour", "rebuild this café"). The regenerate routes to `generateBackdrop` / `generateNpcAvatar` with a delta prompt + the existing descriptor and updates the doc's inline SVG (`svgInline` for scenes, the avatar data URI for NPCs). Regeneration is rate-limited per user per day. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-11** | **40-node shape budget, free animations, serialisation rules.** The SI caps the document at 40 drawing nodes — one ink-emitting tag (`rect`/`circle`/`ellipse`/`line`/`polyline`/`polygon`/`path`/`text`/`use`) — and clarifies that rule 9's "8-15 distinct visual elements" counts OBJECTS, not nodes. **`<animate>`/`<animateTransform>`, `<g>`, `<defs>`, gradients and `<stop>`s are explicitly FREE** so the 2-4 atmospheric animations of rule 10 (chimney smoke, drifting cloud, flickering lantern) are never traded away for budget; if over, a static shape is cut instead. Serialisation rules (integer coordinates, 3-digit hex, no pretty-printing, `<use>` for repeats, one-decimal opacity) cut tokens without touching what is drawn. **Structural coherence** is a separate short rule (7b): a roof spans the SAME x-range as the structure beneath it at that structure's top line; anything rising above the main roofline gets its own cap; nothing floats without visible support; doors/windows sit inside their wall and a door meets the ground. It exists because of an observed castle whose spire covered only the left third of the hall, overhung the wall by 120 units into open air, and left the central tower uncapped. Adding it IMPROVED every axis at once — node mean fell 46 -> 39 (onto the budget), and cost fell to **$0.006756/scene**, because coherent structures need fewer corrective stray shapes. **Scene requests NO native thinking** (`thinkingLevel: minimal`, guarded by a test). Measured on `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`: at `medium` — what the site sent before — scene generation burned **2365 thinking tokens per scene ($0.005913, 59% of the entire bill)** and drew LESS for it: 25 drawing nodes vs 43 at `minimal`, 1318 drawing tokens vs 2458. Native thinking was worse on BOTH axes, so this is not a quality/cost trade. Raising the node cap to spend the freed budget was tried (40 -> 52) and rejected: the mean stayed at 43 nodes for 8% more cost, so the model simply draws what it draws. Measured clean on `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`: **$0.006967/scene at 43 nodes**, vs $0.010029 for the same prompt at `medium` and $0.012547 for the pre-migration baseline — **-44%**. **Adherence is approximate** — the cap plus rule 7b pulls the mean to ~39 nodes with a 33-49 spread; prompt-level counting is not reliably enforced by this model, so the budget is a steer, not a guarantee. **Measurement caveat:** an earlier round concluded node budgets were inherently harmful and produced placeholder scenes. That was WRONG — the generator used for those runs appended its own hard-coded 30-node cap to the file's SI, so no edit to `scene.ts` could move the result. Any future prompt experiment must read the SI verbatim from the file. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-12** | **Square canvas, bleed colour, top-anchored, self-clipping.** Scenes are drawn on a SQUARE 1000x1000 canvas. `BackdropStage` sizes the `<svg>` ELEMENT BOX to that square (`aspect-square h-full w-auto max-w-full mx-auto`), flush to the top and centred horizontally, and paints the surround with the flat colour the scene declares on its full-canvas `<rect class="bleed">` plate (`backdropBleedColor`). Sizing the element to the square is load-bearing beyond layout: an outer `<svg>`'s default `overflow:hidden` clips at the ELEMENT box, **not** the viewBox — so while the element was stretched to fill the stage, any geometry the model drew outside 0..1000 painted out into the surround (observed; the model still emits some, so this is a renderer guarantee, not a prompt promise). The SI also carries a STAY INSIDE THE CANVAS rule as belt-and-braces. Top-anchoring puts all the slack in one band BELOW the scene, where the option chips render. Bleed extraction tolerates combined classes (`class="bleed sky"`) and free attribute order, and rejects `url(#gradient)` fills since the surround is a flat CSS colour. Legacy 3:2 scenes declare no plate, resolve to `null`, and fall back to `--color-bg`. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-13** | *(withdrawn 2026-07-25)* A blurred, cover-scaled copy of the scene was trialled as the margin fill so the drawing itself reached the screen edges. It worked, but the flat bleed colour of `LIFE-BACKDROP-12` was preferred on review — cleaner, and one less full-canvas composite per scene on mobile. Id-collision note retained for anyone retrying it: it must be a CSS `background-image` data URI, never a second inline `<svg>`, or duplicate gradient ids break `url(#…)` in the foreground copy. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-15** | **A scene swap HOLDS the outgoing scene and cross-fades; the stage never blanks or drops to a placeholder mid-swap.** `useLifeBackdrop` still sets `svg` to the bundled silhouette the moment a render starts (`status: 'generating'`) — `BackdropStage` deliberately IGNORES that. The stage classifies every incoming string with `isBundledSilhouette` (exact string match against `getFallbackBackdrop` for all 8 `FallbackBackdropKey`s; a generated or cached scene can never collide, because only gen output is ever written to the cache) and a silhouette NEVER replaces a real scene that is already painted. Consequences, in the three orders they occur: **(a) generating** — the outgoing scene stays up and the EXISTING `.life-scene-building` veil that `LifeScreen` already renders whenever `backdropBuildingVisible` is true (which includes `status==='generating'`) sweeps over it. There is exactly ONE shimmer in the app for this; do not add a second inside the stage. **(b) success** — the incoming SVG fades in over the outgoing one (`.life-scene-fade-in`, `SCENE_FADE_MS` = 420ms, `prefers-reduced-motion` → instant), which stays painted underneath for the duration and is then dropped. **(c) failure** (`LIFE-BACKDROP-8` / `LIFE-FAIL-3/4`) — the terminal silhouette also fails the "real scene" test, so the outgoing scene simply STAYS; the shimmer removes itself because `backdropBuildingVisible` is already `false` on that path. Net effect: a failed regeneration is invisible to the player rather than a blank-out. **Id namespacing is load-bearing:** for the length of the fade there are TWO inline `<svg>`s in the DOM, which is precisely the `url(#…)` collision hazard recorded in `LIFE-BACKDROP-13`. The OUTGOING layer's ids are rewritten with a per-swap prefix (`namespaceSvgIds(svg, 'lifebackdrop-prevN-')`, covering `id=`, `url(#…)`, `href="#…"` and `xlink:href="#…"`) so the INCOMING scene keeps its original ids and both layers resolve their own gradients. Rewriting the outgoing copy rather than the incoming one is deliberate — the incoming scene is the one that persists, so it must never depend on a transform. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-16** | **Before a world has EVER shown a real scene, the stage renders a planet — not a location silhouette — and no location text.** The placeholder is `PLANET_PLACEHOLDER_SVG` (`src/screens/LifeScreen/backdropPlanetPlaceholder.ts`): a hand-authored, self-contained, square (`viewBox 0 0 1000 1000`) inline SVG of a zoomed-out planet — a soft disc, a faint atmosphere ring, amorphous surface blobs, a slow drifting band, a few twinkling stars. Deliberately NON-DESCRIPT: no continents that read as Earth, no landmark, nothing that implies a genre, because at this moment the world's genre is exactly what has not been decided yet. It is authored by hand as a string — never fetched, generated, or base64'd. **Theme-awareness** is via `style="fill:var(--color-…)"` inline declarations, NOT presentation attributes: `fill="var(--x)"` as an ATTRIBUTE does not resolve, only CSS declarations take `var()`. The SVG declares no `<rect class="bleed">`, so `backdropBleedColor` returns `null` and the surround stays `--color-bg` in both themes (`LIFE-BACKDROP-12`). **The location ribbon is suppressed while the placeholder is up** — the seeded default `life.locationRibbon.apartment` ("Your apartment · morning") is a guess about a world that has not started, and printing it next to the "Building your wurld…" pill claimed a place the player had not been to. The pill (`LIFE-BACKDROP-7`) is then the only text on the stage. `LifeScreen` still computes and passes `locationLabel` (it also feeds the flashcard story-context stamp, `LIFE-BUBBLE-7`); the STAGE decides not to paint it. The planet stands down permanently the first time a real scene arrives — after that `LIFE-BACKDROP-15`'s hold governs and the player never sees a placeholder again for that world. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-17** | ⚠️ **REVERSED 2026-08-01 — the placeholder is ALWAYS the planet, including on a terminal failure.** The owner rejected the silhouette outright: *"what happened to my zoomed out planet view that used to show when the wurld was loading? I don't like that you changed the fallback back to the apartment. There's Wurlds like old kingdoms that don't suit this."* The location silhouettes are a small fixed library (apartment, café, market…), so on a world they do not fit they do not read as "this scene failed", they read as **the wrong place** — a worse answer than a neutral one. `nextStageState` therefore returns `PLANET_PLACEHOLDER_SVG` for every unpainted case and `generating` is now unused there (kept in the signature, `void`-ed, so the call sites do not churn). **The defect this row was written for is NOT re-opened, but its remedy moved:** a terminally-failed first scene must still be distinguishable from one still arriving, and that is now the pill's job (`LIFE-BACKDROP-7`) rather than the artwork's — the artwork is identical in both states by design. The original reasoning is kept below because the failure it records (*"the spinner stops and nothing shows"*) is the thing any future change here has to keep solving. **Original (superseded): a TERMINAL scene failure paints the silhouette; only an IN-FLIGHT render is held off.** Reported 2026-08-01: *"it's generating and I see the spinner, but when the spinner stops, nothing shows."* `LIFE-BACKDROP-8` emits the bundled location silhouette when generation fails for good — that is the whole reason the fallback library exists — but `LIFE-BACKDROP-15`/`-16` suppressed it, because the generating window and the terminal failure **emit identical bytes** and the state machine was classifying on bytes alone. On a FIRST scene that failed the player was left on the `LIFE-BACKDROP-16` planet with the spinner gone and nothing further coming: indistinguishable from broken. `nextStageState` therefore takes a `generating` flag (`BackdropStage` prop, fed from `scene.status !== 'fallback'` in `LifeScreen`) — the ONE thing the bytes cannot carry. Still generating → the planet; failed → the incoming silhouette. **The `LIFE-BACKDROP-15` hold is unchanged and still outranks both**: a failure must never blank a scene already painted, so the branch is reached only when nothing is up yet. `BackdropStage` keys its render-phase state on BOTH `svg` and `generating`, since a terminal failure changes only the flag — the silhouette was already the in-flight placeholder's value, so watching the svg alone would miss the transition that matters. Verified in-browser (Chromium, 2026-08-01): both the planet and the silhouette paint at full container size, so the defect was never the SVGs. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-19** | **A looping background-position shimmer must travel a WHOLE number of background tiles.** Reported 2026-08-01: *"the shimmer on the placeholder planet as a scene loads has a sudden animation restart glitch once per cycle."* A percentage `background-position` resolves against (container − image), **not** against the container, so how far a given ΔP moves the gradient depends on `background-size`: for a size of S container-widths the travel is `ΔP/100 × (S − 1)` container widths, and the loop rejoins itself only when that is an exact multiple of the tile width S. The shared `shimmerGradient` keyframe (ΔP = 400) satisfies this at **S = 2 only** — exactly 2 tiles — which is why `.text-shimmer`, `.skeleton-block` and `.skeleton-chip` all look continuous. `.life-scene-building` is S = 2.5, where ΔP = 400 is **2.4** tiles: the last 0.4 of a tile was discarded on every wrap, and the sheen snapped back once per cycle. It now has its own `backdropBuildingSheen` keyframe at ΔP = 500 (3 tiles exactly). **The rule is enforced arithmetically, not by convention** — `src/styles/shimmerLoop.test.ts` parses `globals.css`, pairs every `background-size: N% 100%` rule with its keyframe, and fails on a fractional tile count, so a future shimmer at a new size cannot silently inherit a keyframe that does not tile for it. Asserted against the stylesheet because jsdom computes no animation and the two halves of the bug live in different places in the file.  |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-20** | **The first scene generates while the saga is being READ, not on Begin.** Owner, 2026-08-01: *"the first scene should render whilst I'm reading the saga. Not wait until I press begin."* `canGenerateBackdrop` gained a `!!worldData.saga` arm, so the gen starts the moment the world has a saga — which is exactly when `WorldIntroScreen` goes up and holds the player for as long as it takes them to read a teaser. A scene call is ~5-8s; spending that window on nothing and then making the player watch the planet is the only thing that changes here. **The number of gens is UNCHANGED.** `LIFE-BACKDROP-18` already generated this same speculative descriptor the instant the first beat landed, so the cost was being paid either way; this moves it from in front of the player to behind their reading. **The second gen on the opening beat's real `backdropIntent` is NOT waste** and is not removed: the observed pair (`apartment_bedroom`/`noir_city` → `office`/`dark_noir`) is two genuinely different places, and the cache is keyed on the descriptor, so an intent that agrees with the seed is a cache hit and costs nothing. Answers the owner's second question on the same log — *"2 scene gen calls seem to be made at the start"* — one speculative seed, one real intent. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-21** | **The opening step runs BEFORE the first scene gen, so the first scene is generated from a REAL `backdropIntent` and never from the seed guess.** `LIFE-BACKDROP-20` moved the gen behind the player's reading, which fixed the timing but not the input: the descriptor was still `fallbackDescriptor` (`apartment_bedroom` + a seed vibe), and the opening beat's actual intent then replaced it — owner, 2026-08-01: *"what triggered the apartment_bedroom/noir_city then, clearly the first actual intent was office. The scene that renders behind the introduction card is immediately discarded. Maybe the first step needs to be run before the first scene gen."* Correct on all three counts. **The seam already existed**: `runLiveTurn` takes a `prefetched` promise (built for the post-feedback turn), so `prefetchOpening()` fires `generateStepOptions` the moment the saga + chapter 1 land — while `WorldIntroScreen` is up — and publishes `node.backdropIntent` to `onBackdropIntent` **as soon as the promise resolves**, without arriving at the node. The scene therefore generates from `office`/`dark_noir` behind the intro card, and Begin hands the SAME promise to `startOpening`, so the node arrives from cache and the beats glide in unwatched-for-the-first-time as before. **`canGenerateBackdrop` loses its `!!worldData.saga` arm** — that arm WAS the speculative gen, and with the real intent arriving during the intro it has nothing left to cover. The `eventsData.length > 0` arm stays and is now the sole `LIFE-BACKDROP-18` backstop: an opening beat that carries no `backdropIntent` at all still gets a scene once its events land, from the seed descriptor, which is the best available reading in that case. **Net: one gen instead of two on a fresh world, from the right descriptor, and Begin is instant.** |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-22** | **The 2026-08 `scene` → `backdrop` rename.** "Scene" was doing two jobs: the generated SVG behind the dialogue, and the story unit the player is living through. The restructure needs the second, so the first was renamed — `LIFE-SCENE-*` → `LIFE-BACKDROP-*`, `sceneIntent` → `backdropIntent`, `SceneStage` → `BackdropStage`, the `life_scene` Gemini category → `life_backdrop`, and both Firestore surfaces: the per-world `lifeWorlds/{worldId}/scenes` subcollection → `backdrops`, and the `scene_visited` event kind → `backdrop_visited`. **NO back-compat, by decision** (owner, 2026-08-02: *"I don't need to support old versions, it's a new feature"*). The first cut carried a read-fallback that served the pre-rename row and copied it forward, plus a `ZLifeEvent` preprocess that renamed the old kind at the parse boundary, plus `life_scene` aliases in the SI-cache set and the observability category list. All of it is gone: pre-rename docs are ORPHANS that nothing reads, and a backdrop miss costs one regeneration. The only place the old names survive is `WORLD_SUBCOLLECTIONS`, so a wurld delete is still total — *"nothing reads it"* is not *"the user agreed to keep it"*, and the delete copy promises a full wipe in as many words. **Deliberately NOT renamed:** the SVG-gen SI's art prose still says "scene" — it addresses an illustrator, where that is the term of art, and rewording it is an unmeasured change to an art generator (see the header of `prompts/backdrop.ts`). Likewise `GradedQuestionSceneContext` and `<npcInScene>`, which snapshot the STORY moment and were always about the other meaning. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-23** | **The sanitiser's element allowlist is compared case-INSENSITIVELY, and the set is lowercased to match.** Reported 2026-08-03: *"none of the backdrop svgs seem to animate anymore despite being told to produce animating scenes."* They were animating — `sanitiseSvg` was deleting the animations. SVG is parsed as XML (`DOMParser(..., 'image/svg+xml')`), so unlike HTML the `tagName` **preserves case**: an `<animateTransform>` reports `animateTransform`, not `animatetransform`. `sanitiseElement` looked up `el.tagName.toLowerCase()` against a set whose entries were written in the spec's camelCase, so EVERY camelCase element missed and was removed — `animateTransform`, `animateMotion`, `linearGradient`, `radialGradient`, `clipPath` and all eight `fe*` filter primitives. Plain-lowercase tags (`rect`, `path`, `animate`) matched, which is why backdrops still rendered and the bug read as "animation doesn't play" rather than "sanitiser is broken". Confirmed against a REAL generated backdrop: the model emitted 3 animations, all `repeatCount="indefinite"`, and 0 survived. **The fix is to lowercase the SET at construction** (`.map((t) => t.toLowerCase())`) rather than to lowercase the entries by hand, so the source list stays readable as the spec spells it and a future addition cannot re-introduce the mismatch. **Do not "fix" this by dropping the `.toLowerCase()` on the lookup instead** — that would then reject a model that emitted `<lineargradient>`, and XML case-sensitivity means both spellings can arrive. Asserted by `sanitizeSvg.test.ts`, which checks a camelCase element of each family survives a round-trip, not merely that the output is non-null. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-24** | **A repeated attribute is DROPPED, not fatal.** MEASURED: one backdrop in twenty of a four-world run died on `90:158: duplicate attribute: fill` — the model had written `fill` twice on one element. XML is strict, `DOMParser` returns a `<parsererror>` root, so `sanitiseSvg` returned null and the WHOLE backdrop was discarded; the player got a fallback silhouette. An HTML parser would take the first value and shrug. `dedupeAttributes` rewrites only what is inside a tag's angle brackets, keeps the first occurrence of each attribute name, and touches nothing else. **Repaired rather than regenerated** — a deterministic tic with an obvious correct reading, where a retry costs a whole image generation to fix a duplicated word. **Repaired rather than parsed as HTML** — switching parsers to dodge one error class would change what the sanitiser sees for every other input, and this file treats its input as adversarial on purpose. The first cut of the regex swallowed the self-closing slash on `<rect …/>`, producing an UNCLOSED element: a repair that broke the document it was fixing. The slash is now taken off the attribute run explicitly rather than trusted to a group that could not carry it. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-25** | **An unclosed element is CLOSED, not fatal.** MEASURED 2026-08-04: a four-world run lost a backdrop to `21:9: unexpected close tag` — the model wrote `<feMerge>`, two `<feMergeNode/>` children, then `</filter>` without ever closing `</feMerge>`. 4.9 KB of authored SVG was discarded for one missing tag, and the judge scored that world's BACKDROP-FIT **1**, noting *"the scene at the village fountain has no SVG source defined, rendering it broken"*. `closeUnclosedElements` runs before the XML parse and does what every HTML parser does: it walks the tag stream keeping a stack, and when a close tag arrives that is not the innermost open element it closes the intervening ones first; a close tag matching nothing open is dropped as a stray; anything still open at the end is closed. **Same two rejected alternatives as `LIFE-BACKDROP-24`** — not an HTML parser (it would change what the sanitiser sees for every other input, and the input is treated as adversarial), and not a retry (a backdrop is the most expensive generation in the sim; re-rolling one to supply a missing `</feMerge>` spends an image to fix a typo). The repair runs BEFORE `sanitiseElement`, so the allowlist still governs what survives — closing a tag never smuggles anything past it. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-26** | **The location ribbon is TAP-TO-REVEAL: it shows the practice-language label, and a tap swaps it to the native for `RIBBON_REVEAL_MS` (2500) before it swaps back.** The ribbon is the one piece of chrome on the stage that is written in the practice language, and a beginner who cannot read "Le comptoir du Vieux-Port" has no way to find out where they are. **Not shown permanently, and that is the owner's call** (2026-08-05: an earlier instruction to show `label · native` inline was superseded the same day by *"forget showing the native location all the time, briefly show it if the location pill is tapped though"*) — the ribbon sits over the artwork, a second line or a `•`-joined pair doubles its footprint on a phone, and the reveal is wanted rarely. **The native label rides the SAME generation, never a separate call:** `ZBackdropIntent.locationLabelNative` is emitted beside `locationLabel` whenever a `backdropIntent` is emitted, persisted by `commitNode` to `world.currentLocationLabelNative` and `currentBackdrop.labelNative`, and mirrored into the `backdrop_visited` event payload. Translating on tap would be a Gemini round-trip for a caption, and pre-translating on arrival would be a second call for a string the step model is already holding. **It is NOT part of the backdrop cache key** (`descriptorCacheKey` reads `locationType`/`vibe`/`persistent` only) — same reasoning as `timeOfDay` under `LIFE-TIME-1`: one place, one SVG. **No native label ⇒ no affordance.** `locationLabelNative` is optional, so a legacy world, a model that omits it, or a world whose practice and system languages coincide renders the ribbon as an inert `<div>` — not a `<button>` that does nothing when pressed, which is the worse failure. When present the ribbon is a `<button>` carrying `aria-label` = the native text, so the reveal is reachable without the tap. Tapping again while revealed restarts the timer rather than toggling back; the timer is cleared on unmount and on a label change (arriving somewhere new must not leave the previous place's translation on screen). |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-28** | **Accents FOLD in the descriptor normaliser; they do not vanish.** `SAFE` is `/[^a-z0-9_]/g` and ran AFTER `toLowerCase`, so every accented character was DELETED rather than folded: `café` → `caf`, `Bibliothèque` → `bibliothque`. `normaliseToken` now NFD-decomposes and strips the combining marks first, keeping the base letter. **Scoped honestly, measured before the fix rather than after.** The v2 engine branch (which hit this independently) frames it as the FALLBACK silhouette — `café` matching no `FALLBACK_MAP` key and landing on `apartment_bedroom` — and our data does NOT support that: `pickFallback` reads `locationType`, and across the archive's 12 distinct locationTypes **zero** carry a non-ASCII character, because the model writes them in English snake_case (`attic`, `repair_shed`, `post_office`). Where it actually bit is `descriptorCacheKey`, which reads the LABEL: **25 of 281 archived labels (9%) are accented** — `Café de la Place`, `The workshop of Elías` — and since the model writes both `Café` and `Cafe` for one place, those minted two keys and two PAID backdrop generations for one location. That collapse is the reason to ship it. **One-time cost, stated because it is real:** an existing world whose label carries an accent gets a new key and regenerates its backdrop once. The module's doc comment had also always been wrong about its own output (it claimed `biblioth_que_municipale` where the code returned `bibliothque_municipale`) — a comment describing behaviour the code never had is how this survived being read. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-29** | **One explicit-cache slot per allowlisted category — the slot is DERIVED from the category, never hard-coded.** Found 2026-08-09 while measuring `life_step` caching. `EXPLICIT_SI_CACHE_CATEGORIES` (`LIFE-BACKDROP-14`) is documented as extensible — *"Adding one is a COST decision, not a cleanup"* — but `getCachedSiModel` passed a hard-coded `"backdrop"` cacheKey and the single `system/backdropContextCache` doc path for whatever cleared the allowlist. `getOrCreateExplicitCache` keys its in-memory slot AND its Firestore doc on exactly those two strings and rotates on an SI-digest mismatch, so a second entry would have made the two categories alternate: X caches → Y sees a digest mismatch → CREATE (billing Y's whole SI) → publish → X sees a mismatch → CREATE → publish, **for ever, every call paying a full-price create — strictly worse than not caching at all.** Nothing would have surfaced it: the `[si-cache] created` logs read as normal and the only symptom is the bill. `explicitSiCacheSlot(category)` now returns `{ cacheKey: \`si:${category}\`, docPath }`, with `life_backdrop` pinned to its already-published `system/backdropContextCache` so a deploy ADOPTS the live cache instead of every warm instance paying a fresh create; new categories get `system/siContextCache_<category>`. Only allowlisted categories reach it and the allowlist is a hard-coded Set of `[a-z_]` identifiers, so the derived path is a valid Firestore document id by construction — the client cannot steer it. **The fix is at the boundary, not a comment telling the next person to remember**, because the defect only exists on the day someone adds the second entry, which is exactly when nobody re-reads the call site. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-30** | **You may only cache a prompt you OWN — the cache allowlist is DERIVED from a server-held SI registry, never written beside it.** `LIFE-BACKDROP-29` closed the two-categories-share-one-slot thrash; this closes the same thrash reached from the other side. `geminiGenerate` reads BOTH `systemInstruction` and `category` from the request body, and the allowlist gated the category STRING while the cache digest was computed over whatever bytes arrived — so the check and the thing being checked were different values. Two consequences, and the boring one is the dangerous one: any authenticated caller could mint unbounded billed caches by varying the SI; and with **no attacker at all**, any ordinary staged rollout would do it — two app versions carrying different prompt bytes are both live for a while, each overwrites the other's shared record, every call misses, and every call pays to create a cache the next call discards. That is `LIFE-BACKDROP-29`'s *"strictly worse than not caching"* state, arriving on a normal deploy rather than on the day someone adds a second category. `BACKDROP_SYSTEM_INSTRUCTION` therefore moved to `functions/src/prompts/lifeBackdrop.ts` — the move its own header had always said it would make *"when the slice hardens"*, brought forward because `LIFE-BACKDROP-11` hardened it early by making the bytes load-bearing. The server substitutes its copy by category for the cached path, the inline fallback AND the fallback model (substituting for only some would make the cache a silent behaviour change rather than an optimisation), and the client sends no `systemInstruction` at all. `EXPLICIT_SI_CACHE_CATEGORIES` is now `Object.keys(SERVER_OWNED_SI)`, so a category cannot become cacheable without the server holding its bytes — the rule is structural, not a comment. The USER prompt (`buildBackdropPrompt`) stays client-side: per-world, never cached, different bytes every call. ⚠ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-18 — the user prompt is server-owned too** (`SRV-PROMPT-8..13` batch 1). "Per-world and never cached" is why it did not need to move for the CACHE's sake, and that reasoning still holds; it is simply not the only reason a prompt moves. Owning the SI alone left the user prompt as the other place prose enters, so the proxy stayed open on this category with half the work done. `buildBackdropUserPrompt` now lives beside the SI it is paired with and the client sends `prompt: ''` plus a validated `life_backdrop` spec. The cache record is untouched by design: the built call returns the same `BACKDROP_SYSTEM_INSTRUCTION` constant and names no `cacheSlot`, so it still adopts `system/backdropContextCache` rather than minting a second one — which is this invariant's whole point, reached through the new path. Cost of the trade: during a rollout an old client's calls are simply uncached, which is the pre-cache price and strictly cheaper than a create per call. Editing the style is now a functions deploy that reaches everyone at once, like every other prompt in this app. |

---

## 7. NPC handling — `LIFE-NPC-*`

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-NPC-1** | The step generator decides per-NPC whether the character is transient (one-scene only) or persistent. The flag is `newNpcs[i].transient: boolean` in the response. Persistent NPCs get a row in `lifeNpcs`, an avatar generated, and stay in the LLM context's roster going forward. Transient NPCs exist only in the beat events. |
| **LIFE-NPC-2b** | **Avatar generation is PARTS-FIRST** (v2, 2026-07). The model no longer draws the portrait — it DRESSES one: `avatarParams` SI lists every option of every slot from the part library (`lib/life/avatar/parts.ts` — hair 19 · headwear 15 · outfit 16 · facialHair 8 · eyes 7 · brows 5 · mouth 10 · accessory 9) and returns a tiny JSON (`ZAvatarParams`, `responseMimeType: 'application/json'`, category `life_npc_avatar_params`) naming one part per slot plus hex colours. `assembleAvatar` composes the library back-to-front into the finished SVG **client-side**. Measured on a 6-character spread: **~122 output tokens vs ~968 for the direct draw (~8× cheaper)**, one consistent art style across every NPC, and structurally unbreakable (no model-authored SVG). **Fallback → the direct draw** (`drawNpcAvatar`, the original `NPC_AVATAR_SYSTEM_INSTRUCTION` path) when: the model returns `{"fallback":true}` (it judges the library can't represent the character — a creature, a masked face), the JSON fails `ZAvatarParams` (incl. a non-hex colour), or it names a part that doesn't exist (`assembleAvatar` returns `svg:null` + the offending slots). The result carries `via: 'parts' \| 'draw'`. **Slot names are a CONTRACT** — the prompt advertises them and stored avatars replay them, so parts may be ADDED but never renamed or removed. ⚠ **SINCE 2026-09-18 THAT CONTRACT SPANS TWO FILES** (`SRV-PROMPT-8..13` batch 2). The prompt is server-owned, so the server holds a NAMES-ONLY copy (`AVATAR_PART_OPTIONS` in `functions/src/prompts/lifeAvatarParams.ts`) while `lib/life/avatar/parts.ts` keeps the SVG GEOMETRY, because the client still draws. Adding a part means editing BOTH, in the same order. This duplication does **not** expire — drawing is client-side by design, so no later batch merges the two — and `src/lib/life/avatar/parts.test.ts` is the guard. Drift is silent in both directions and one direction costs money: a name the server advertises and the client cannot draw makes `assembleAvatar` return `svg: null` and falls back to the direct draw at ~8× the output tokens, on a path that looks like it worked. Shipping the option list up in the request was rejected — it is interpolated verbatim into the system instruction, so a client supplying it could write arbitrary text into an SI. Art-direction invariants that keep the assembly coherent: the head spans `faceTop`→`faceBottom` and OVERLAPS the neck (a gap lets hair show under the chin and reads as a beard on every long style); hair/headwear return `{back, front}` so hair frames the face and hoods sit behind it; side locks stay outside the jaw silhouette. |
| **LIFE-NPC-2c** | **Layer containment** — the assembly rules that keep a dressed avatar reading as ONE drawing. Asserted in `avatar/assemble.test.ts`; each rule encodes a defect that shipped and was visible on screen. (1) The skin torso and EVERY garment render on one shared shoulder path (`SHOULDER_D`) — when they drifted 2px apart, skin leaked above the collar on every avatar. (2) A collar is an OPENING: `collar(garment, skin)` fills the scoop with SKIN so the neck continues into it; garments take `(color, skin)` (`OutfitFn`) for exactly this reason. Painting the scoop in a garment shade read as decapitation and buried the neck under high collars. (3) Garment mass that belongs behind the head (a hoodie's bunched hood) lives in `OUTFIT_BACK` and draws BEFORE `faceBase`, so the neck rises out of the collar. (4) `ENCLOSING_HEADWEAR` (hood, helmet) suppresses the hairstyle ENTIRELY — a hood an afro bursts out of doesn't read as worn. (5) `DOMED_HEADWEAR` suppresses `TALL_HAIR` (spiky, mohawk, dreads, topKnot) fronts, else they skewer the hat. (6) Every part must overlap the anatomy it attaches to: hair masses overlap the skull, side ropes/braids/pigtails overlap the silhouette, hat bodies meet their brims. Free-floating parts (a band wider than the cone it wraps, a hat body 6px above its brim, beads 11px clear of the head) are the recurring failure — check joins at 180px, never at thumbnail size (`tests/experiments/avatar-svg-vs-pixel/zoom.mjs`). |
| **LIFE-TEMP-1..4** | **Sampling temperature is tiered by what is being generated** (`lib/life/modelConfig.ts`, asserted in `modelConfig.test.ts`). **Creative** (`LIFE_TEMPERATURE` = 0.95) — saga, scene outline, replan, world arc, story beats/options, NPC bio + profile: variety and low repetition are the point, and structured JSON keeps the schema stable regardless, so temperature governs WHAT happens, not how it serialises. **Graded** (`LIFE_TEMPERATURE_GRADED` = 0.35) — the graded-question generator: creative variance is a DEFECT here, because a graded item needs exactly one defensibly-correct answer and distractors that are wrong for a reason; at the creative tier the generator wandered into ambiguous options and near-synonym distractors that make a correct answer look wrong. Low, not zero — chips still need lexical variety across turns. **Art** (`LIFE_TEMPERATURE_ART` = 0.7) — NPC avatars + scene SVGs: creative but structurally constrained (avatar params come from a fixed menu; scene output must stay valid SVG). **Faithful** (temperature deliberately UNSET) — the resume recap, which restates what happened and must not invent. A new life-sim generator MUST pick a tier explicitly; the test suite fails a graded path wired to the creative constant. |
| **LIFE-NPC-2** | Persistent-NPC avatar generation uses `thinkingLevel: 'medium'` and runs ASYNC after the beat lands on-screen. While the SVG generates (`avatarStatus: 'pending'`, no `avatarUrl`) the avatar slot shows a lucide person icon (`UserRound`) under a shimmer sweep (`.skeleton-block`, clipped to the circle; `data-testid="LifeScreen-Avatar-Loading"`) so it reads as "loading" not final; subsequent appearances show the cached avatar. Applies to NPC avatars AND the player's own avatar (`characterAvatarStatus`), threaded `LifeScreen` (`npcAvatarPending` set / `userAvatarLoading`) → `DialogueArea` → `Avatar` `loading` prop. The player avatar URL comes from `useCharacterAvatar` (the `assets/character` sidecar, LIFE-DATA-1) — NOT the world doc, which carries only the status flag; `userAvatarLoading` is driven purely by `characterAvatarStatus === 'pending'`. **NPC avatars follow the SAME sidecar pattern** — bytes in `assets/npc-{id}`, the npc doc carries only `avatarStatus`, and `useNpcAvatars` resolves the roster's npcId → URL (read-once via getDoc, module-cached, StrictMode-safe in-flight dedup); `npcAvatarPending` is driven purely by `avatarStatus === 'pending'`. Both hooks share `lib/life/lifeAvatarAssets.ts` (one `assets/{assetId}` doc per avatar). A failed gen (`avatarStatus: 'failed'`) falls back to the name-initials circle. **In-flight dedup** (latency audit): `kickoffNpcAssets` shares ONE gen per `${worldId}:${npcId}:avatar\|profile` key (a module-level guard, mirroring `inFlightBackdropGens`). The `commitNode` roster sweep runs on EVERY node arrival and re-kicks any NPC still `pending` — but a flash-preview avatar gen (2–5s, the priciest tier) routinely outlasts a turn, so without the guard the same NPC's gen re-fired on the next pick (2× tokens/NPC). Cleared when the gen settles. |
| **LIFE-NPC-3** | Transient NPCs always render with the silhouette. Never an avatar. |
| **LIFE-NPC-4** | Compaction (`LIFE-COMPACT-*`) can RETROACTIVELY promote a transient NPC to persistent — if the summariser detects the character has recurred in events, it writes a `lifeNpcs` row and kicks off avatar gen for them at that point. This handles the case where the step generator misjudged transience. |
| **LIFE-NPC-5** | A user can rename / edit / delete an NPC from the character sheet. Delete removes the `lifeNpcs` row + Storage SVG + adds a `npc_forgotten` event to `lifeEvents` so the model treats them as out-of-life going forward. |
| **LIFE-NPC-MEM-1** | **Per-NPC `oneLineMemory` is authored INLINE by the step model, not reconstructed by compaction.** `ZRawNodeResponse.npcMemoryUpdates` (`[{npcId, oneLineMemory ≤240}]`, `.max(3)`, optional) rides the ordinary `life_step` response beside `logLine`/`introduces` — so a memory refresh costs **no separate call** (vs the old `life_compact` reconstruction from a folded digest). The SI rule (`prompts/step.ts`) asks for an update ONLY for a roster NPC whose relationship with the player MATERIALLY advanced THIS turn: one ≤30-word second-person `systemLanguage` line, folding the new development into the EXISTING memory, using the roster `npcId` verbatim, omitting unchanged NPCs. Committed via `applyTurnSignals` → the SHARED `writeNpcMemories` helper (`npcs.ts`), which drops any `npcId` not in the live roster (anti-hallucination) or with empty text and swallows individual write failures — **best-effort, never fails the turn.** `oneLineMemory` is a convenience hint (roster nudge via `<npcMemory>` + `AvatarModal` profile card), NOT a continuity primitive — continuity lives in `storyLog`/`canon`/`recentEvents`. **Transition:** the inline writer ships alongside compaction's writer (both call `writeNpcMemories`); `LIFE-COMPACT-3c` is superseded once compaction is removed (`LIFE-MEM-*`). |
| **LIFE-NPC-6** | Missing-avatar backfill. Some `lifeNpcs` rows carry `avatarUrl == null` — created before avatars were required, or promoted as scene key-NPCs (`acceptSceneHook`) which historically NEVER kicked avatar gen (the biggest source of blank avatars). Two mechanisms close the gap: (a) `acceptSceneHook` now kicks avatar + profile gen for each freshly-promoted key-NPC AND sets `relationship` from `ZSceneKeyNpc.role`; (b) `commitNode` sweeps the PRE-turn roster (`input.npcs`) after the promotion loop and re-kicks avatar gen for any NPC with `avatarUrl == null && avatarStatus === 'pending'`. `'failed'` NPCs are NEVER re-kicked (respects `LIFE-FAIL-5`'s one-retry-then-permanent-silhouette). `kickoffNpcAvatarGen` now passes the NPC's `role` (its `relationship`) into `generateNpcAvatar` so clothing/bearing match (it was omitted before). The sweep is fire-and-forget and never blocks the commit; the pre-turn roster excludes this-turn's just-promoted `isNew` NPCs (already kicked), so no double-fire. |
| **LIFE-NPC-7** | NPC profile description. `ZLifeNpc.description` (string, nullable/optional) holds a 1–2 sentence, second-person, SPOILER-FREE line — "who they are + how they fit into your story" (e.g. "Your upstairs neighbour, a retired chef who…"). Generated by `generateNpcProfile` (flash-lite, strict JSON `{ "description": string }` — ⚠ since 2026-09-18 that schema is built SERVER-side beside its prompt and no longer travels in the request body, `SRV-PROMPT-1`/batch 2) via `kickoffNpcAssets`, which fires whichever of avatar / profile is missing: for every NEW NPC (alongside the avatar), for freshly-promoted scene key-NPCs, and — backfilling legacy rows — in the `commitNode` roster sweep for any NPC with `description == null`. The avatar profile card (`AvatarModal`) renders `description` as a paragraph ABOVE the `oneLineMemory` border-top row: description = "who they are", oneLineMemory = "story so far". The card's role row flows from `ZLifeNpc.relationship`, which for scene NPCs is now populated from `ZSceneKeyNpc.role` (see LIFE-NPC-6). Unlike avatars (`LIFE-FAIL-5`), a failed description has no permanent-failure state — it stays `null` and the sweep re-attempts until it lands (cheap text gen). |

---

## 8. Screen layout — `LIFE-UI-*`

The `/life` screen is full-bleed scene-first. Zero chrome borrowed from `MessageList` / `ChatScreen` — composition is its own component tree.

| **LIFE-NPC-8** | **An NPC's ROLE is resolved before their avatar and profile are generated.** Reported: a beat introduced *"Madame Roux, ta fleuriste"* handing the player a changed script, and the profile under her portrait read *"your formidable vocal coach and mentor"*. **NOT a race** — the trace has the beats at 13:14:00.996 and the description at 13:14:40.267, forty seconds later. It is a CONTEXT gap: `commitNode`'s beat-promotion path called `promoteOrTouchNpc` and `kickoffNpcAssets` with a name and nothing else, so the profile prompt builder (then `buildNpcProfilePrompt`, client-side; since 2026-09-18 `buildNpcProfileUserPrompt` in `functions/src/prompts/lifeNpcProfile.ts`) emitted no `<role>` at all while its own SI says *"Ground the profile in `<role>` and `<worldVibe>`"*. Handed a French name and "theatrical espionage", the model invented a plausible theatre role — the only thing available to it. `LIFE-NPC-6` fixed this exact omission on the SCENE path (*"carry the key-NPC's role onto the row … Was dropped before"*) and never reached the beat path, which is where most NPCs are actually born; the roster sweep could not recover it either, because it reads `npc.relationship`, which the promote had left null. **The same gap hit the PORTRAIT** — `kickoffNpcAvatarGen` passes `role` *"so clothing + bearing match"* and was getting null here too, so she was drawn without anyone knowing she sells flowers. `resolveNpcRole` (`src/lib/life/npcRole.ts`) now answers it, most-specific-first: (1) the turn's own `introduces[].note`, authored by the model that just wrote the scene; (2) the saga's `keyArchetypes`, authoritative for the recurring cast — for this NPC it reads *"The Handler" / "Madame Roux, a florist who delivers coded scripts and sharp critiques"*, which was sitting in the world doc the whole time and was never read by anything; (3) nothing, preserving today's behaviour. **TWO outputs, because the consumers differ:** `relationship` is the avatar card's one-line role row (LIFE-NPC-7) and is capped at 48 chars — a whole archetype sentence there is a layout bug; `archetype` is the fuller line handed to the profile and avatar prompts, where more context is strictly better. **Matching is FULL-NAME substring only**, deliberately: the saga schema is `{role, description}` with the character's name buried in the description prose and no name field to key on, and a single-token match would tie an NPC to any archetype that merely mentions them ("Julian's dressing room"). A wrong archetype is worse than none because it renders with total confidence — so the fallback is silence, and the SI now says so explicitly (*"do not invent a job, a title, or a relationship to the player that nothing here supports"*). The SI also makes `<archetype>` **outrank** the premise, since a grand-sounding saga otherwise out-shouts a line about selling flowers. **Observability:** `introduces` is now logged in the `LIFE/STEP` debug payload — it was absent, so a trace of the exact turn that mis-described an NPC could not show whether the model had supplied a note, which is the first question anyone debugging this asks. |
| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-UI-1** | Three stacked regions, top-to-bottom: (A) `BackdropStage` — the SVG fills the available height, animated where applicable. (B) `DialogueArea` — the two-bubble window (`LIFE-BUBBLE-*`) overlaid on the lower portion of the scene with a subtle gradient backdrop for readability. (C) `ActionBar` — the chip grid OR free-text composer. ActionBar height is fixed; BackdropStage takes the rest. |
| **LIFE-UI-2** | A small top strip carries: the location ribbon (`🧺 le marché · matin`), the character's name + avatar (left-aligned), a back/menu button (right-aligned). The strip is over the scene with a translucent backdrop. |
| **LIFE-UI-3** | The ActionBar area renders one state per turn phase (`LIFE-NODE-*`): (a) chip grid — 2–3 model options (+ the client ✏️ chip on branch nodes, `LIFE-NODE-4`); (b) inline composer — only after the ✏️ chip is tapped, with a cancel affordance back to the chips; (c) graded multi-choice — chips look identical to (a) but tap commits the answer with grading; (d) 3 skeleton chips while the options call is in flight; (e) a single Continue chip in the `options-failed` state. There is no always-visible composer. |
| **LIFE-UI-4** | Tap on a bubble in `DialogueArea` toggles its translation mode INDEPENDENTLY of the global view mode. Long-press opens the existing ExplainModal for that bubble. Both flow through `MSG-VIEW-*` + `MSG-VOCAB-*` infrastructure. |
| **LIFE-UI-5** | Listen-icon (TTS) on every bubble that's in the practice language, wired to the same `prefetchTTS` + `stopPlayback` singleton practice mode + chat already use. |
| **LIFE-UI-6** | The screen has NO bottom safe-area-bottom problem (this surface ends in the ActionBar, which paints its own background into the home-bar zone — same pattern chat composer uses). |
| **LIFE-UI-7** | On orientation change OR window resize the BackdropStage rescales its SVG via `preserveAspectRatio="xMidYMin meet"` (**contain**-fit, **top-anchored** — changed from cover-fit `slice` by `LIFE-BACKDROP-12`). Contain-fit guarantees the whole square scene is visible on every aspect ratio; the leftover space (side strips on a wide desktop, a single band BELOW the scene on a tall phone) is painted with the scene's own bleed colour. Top-anchoring is what puts that band under the options instead of splitting it above and below the scene. Cover-fit was replaced because it left no leftover space only by CROPPING — imperceptible at 3:2, severe on a 9:19.5 phone where it silently ate the scene's edges. The DialogueArea + ActionBar redo their layout but don't trigger a scene-regen. |
| **LIFE-UI-8** | **Brand naming (Wurlds).** User-facing copy brands this surface "Wurlds" / "wurld(s)": tab label (`tabBar.life`) = "Wurlds"; overview title (`life.overview.title`) = "Wurlds" over a subtitle (`life.overview.subtitle`) = "Your other Wurlds" (LIFE-WORLD-10); beta-flag label (`settings.beta.lifeSimBeta.label`) = "Wurlds (beta)"; and no `life.*` i18n VALUE contains the standalone word "world(s)". Narrative uses of "life/lives" inside values (e.g. "Your name in this life", "different lives") remain. The rebrand is copy-only: i18n KEYS, routes (`/life`), Firestore paths, code identifiers, and invariant ids keep the `life` naming. |

---

## 9. Two-bubble dialogue window — `LIFE-BUBBLE-*`

The sliding-2 window is the centrepiece of the dialogue surface. Never more than two bubbles visible at once.

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-BUBBLE-1** | The DialogueArea shows AT MOST two items. When a new turn arrives, items from the previous turn slide off the top and are no longer visible. Items from the CURRENT turn never get pushed off — the model is capped to 2 beats per turn (`LIFE-STEP-3`) so the new turn always fits. |
| **LIFE-BUBBLE-2** | Each beat from a lifeStep response is its OWN item — there is no beat-folding. A turn that emits a narrator beat + an NPC beat renders as TWO stacked items, not one combined bubble. |
| **LIFE-BUBBLE-3** | Three visual treatments by speaker — they must read as distinct at a glance: **narrator** beats render as a CENTERED, greyed, italic card with NO avatar (system / world cue); **npc** beats render as a LEFT-aligned chat bubble with the NPC's avatar to the left and their name as a small label above; **user** beats render as a RIGHT-aligned bubble in the mine colour (`bg-(--color-bubble-mine)` white text). v2.1: `--color-bubble-mine` was set app-wide (the SHARED chat token, so real chats get it too — owner call: "the new bubble colour is the new primary theme colour") to **`#7e33ac`** (light); dark mode gets a PROPORTIONALLY-moved value **`#3d275f`** (the light-change ratio applied to the original dark, keeping dark's subdued character) — NOT the bright light-mode colour. The vocab underline (`--color-bubble-mine-underline`, `LIFE-BUBBLE-7`) was dialled BACK a notch for subtlety: `#c1a0d8` (light) / `#74569b` (dark). **Current-turn user bubbles get a brand-gradient BORDER with a GAP** (`life-bubble-current-user`): all on the element (so it scales cleanly with the drag-flip transform, no pseudo) — a 2px `border-box` gradient border, a `padding-box` mine fill, and an `inset 0 0 0 2px var(--color-bg)` box-shadow that lays the gap just inside the border. Out-from-fill: mine → 2px gap → 2px gradient border (the gap makes "new" read clearly — the earlier gap-less border sat flush). Previous-turn user bubbles are the flat mine fill. Never combine speakers inside a single bubble. **The bubble-vs-system-message taxonomy is set by `LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-1/2` — speech bubbles are exclusively for direct speech, system messages absorb everything else.** |
| **LIFE-BUBBLE-8** | The current-turn bubbles (those accumulated since the player's most recent action) render with a brand-gradient border. Previous-turn bubbles render with the standard flat border. The set transitions at every player action: CYOA pick → set cleared (filled by the next response); `Continue` from a practice → seeded with the just-resolved bubble; free-text submit → not seeded (free-text is intent, not direct speech — see LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-3). |
| **LIFE-BUBBLE-4** | Both visible items are independently tappable for view-mode toggle + ExplainModal long-press. The item's view mode is INDEPENDENT of the global default — tapping flips just that item. (Phase D wires the long-press handlers.) |
| **LIFE-BUBBLE-5** | Avatars use the existing `Avatar` component — initials circle with a deterministic palette pick keyed by the name in Phase A/B (so the same NPC keeps the same hue across turns). Phase C swaps in the Gemini-rendered SVG via the `svgUrl` prop without changing the component contract. |
| **LIFE-UI-ANIM** | **Visual changes are animated, reduced-motion aware** (polish pass, v2.1 2026-07). (1) **Option cards stagger IN** — each `ActionBar` chip mounts with `motion-safe:animate-in fade-in slide-in-from-bottom-2` + a per-index `animationDelay` (`i * 45ms`) + `fill-mode-both` (so it stays hidden until its turn); a fresh option set re-mounts on new chip ids, so new options cascade in. (2) **Answer / prompt cards slide + fade IN** on mount — the `PracticeFeedback` card, the `PracticePromptCard` `CardShell` (both graded variants), and the `CardMatchExercise` grid, each `motion-safe:animate-in fade-in slide-in-from-bottom-2 duration-200`. (3) **Bubble flip reveal** is the SYMMETRIC `grid-template-rows 0fr↔1fr` auto-height glide + the `scaleX` flip flourish (`LifeBubbleReveal` + `useFlipFlourish`, `LIFE-LANG-16`). (4) **Bottom-panel height glide** (`AnimateHeight`, wrapping `LifeScreenBottom` at the `LifeScreen` call site) — when the panel swaps phase (options → question → feedback → card-match) or the option grid gains/loses a row, the panel's height is animated (measured px height + 220ms `height` transition, driven by a `ResizeObserver` since iOS WKWebView has no reliable CSS `height:auto` transition), so the dialogue bubbles above (in the sibling `flex-1` scene area) move up/down GRADUALLY instead of the panel snapping and shoving them. **Disabled while the ✏️ composer is open** (`enabled={!composerOpen}`) — the soft keyboard drives that height and a 220ms transition would fight it — and under reduced motion; the first measure lands transition-less (auto→px can't interpolate) so it never animates up from zero on mount. All of (1)-(3)'s CSS-class animations are gated `motion-safe:` (skipped under `prefers-reduced-motion`), reusing the `tailwindcss-animate` utilities the chat bubble already uses; (4) checks `prefersReducedMotion()` directly. The loading/authoring skeletons keep their own `animate-pulse` (not combined with `animate-in`). |
| **LIFE-BUBBLE-6** | **Superseded by the node engine (`LIFE-NODE-*` / `LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-3`).** The pre-node design showed an optimistic user bubble with thinking-dots while `lifeStep` was in flight. Under the node engine the player's chip text NEVER renders as a bubble (`LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-3`) — the only user bubble is the fully-resolved line built at practice resolution (`buildResolvedBubble`), and a live turn shows the `live-loading` thinking BAR (turn phases), not an inline optimistic bubble. The `Bubble.pending` field + its dead thinking-dots render path were removed (rendering audit). |
| **LIFE-BUBBLE-7** | **Tappable vocab + save-as-flashcard.** All three bubble treatments (narrator system cue, NPC, user) render their primary line through the chat surface's `AnnotatedText`: when the primary line IS the beat's `practiceText`, its `complexWords` render underlined; tapping a word opens a `WordPopover` under the text with the word's native gloss (from `wordTranslations`, case-tolerant lookup) and a save toggle. **Chat parity (v2.1):** a complex word shows EITHER the underline (idle) OR the background HIGHLIGHT (when open) — never both, exactly like chat's `renderVocabLeaf` (`AnnotatedText` `activeWord` → `--color-underline-active-bg-*` replaces the underline classes for the open word). The popover opens/closes on the SAME `useExitTransition` + `grid-template-rows 0fr↔1fr` auto-height glide the chat `WordPopoverSlot` uses (so the bubble grows/shrinks smoothly, `cachedWord` keeps it populated through the close; reduced motion → instant). Annotations attach to `practiceText` ONLY — when the wurld's viewMode makes the native line the primary (`LIFE-LANG-3` `native-only`), that line renders plain (no underlines, no taps); practice-primary renders (`visible`, `hidden`, the `native-only` missing-gloss fallback, and the gate-held comprehension source) are the tappable surface. At most ONE popover is open across the whole surface (global `vocabStore`, keyed by bubble id). Save routes through the shared `addFlashCard` path (same write path as chat + practice, `PRACTICE-SAVE-3`) with the story context stamped on the card: `"<practiceText>" — <speaker>, <location>` (speaker = NPC name, the character's name for user bubbles, or a localized "Narrator"). A saved word's toggle deletes via `deleteFlashcard` using the card id from the live deck lookup — saved state derives from the deck (case-insensitive phrase match), never from per-session component state, so it survives remount/resume and syncs across devices. Annotations travel end-to-end: `ZStepBeat` → `Bubble` → beat event (`ZLifeBeatPayload.complexWords`/`wordTranslations`, written by `runLifeStep` AND by the practice-resolution bubble persist) → `resumeBubblesFromEvents`, so resumed bubbles stay tappable. Chips (`ZStepChoice`) MAY carry the same optional annotation fields but chips render PLAIN — a chip's tap already means "pick this option" and two competing tap actions on one chip are forbidden; on practice resolution the correct chip's annotations activate in the resolved user bubble (`buildResolvedBubble`). EXCLUDED surfaces: `PracticePromptCard` and the feedback overlay (a per-word native gloss on the question card would leak the graded answer), and the comprehension-source NPC bubble while its gloss is hidden (`hideNativeForUtterance` suppresses vocab taps too, until tap-to-reveal — same precedence as the gloss gate). Free-text input produces no immediate bubble (`LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-3`) and no annotation path of its own — the player's own typed words are never annotated; beats the model emits in response are ordinary beats and MAY carry annotations like any other. The new card emits a `flashcard_added` event on next session resume (`LIFE-FLOW-2`). |

---

## 9.5 Interaction contract — `LIFE-CONTRACT-*`

The load-bearing contract for what goes where and when. The contract supersedes the looser language in §9 and §10 — when those sections read ambiguously, the contract decides.

### 9.5.1 Two render surfaces, mutually exclusive

| Surface | Visual | Content |
|---|---|---|
| **Speech bubble** | Left-aligned w/ NPC avatar (`npc`) OR right-aligned w/ user avatar (`user`) | The literal, quotable words a character spoke. Nothing else. |
| **System message** | Centered, greyed, italic, no avatar | Everything that isn't direct speech. |

A beat goes in exactly one surface. There is no third option.

### 9.5.2 What counts as "direct speech"

A beat is direct speech **iff** it is a verbatim utterance a character spoke aloud — the content that would appear in quotation marks in a novel. Everything else (scene cues, sensory detail, internal feelings, player physical actions, transitions, body language, world events, narrated choices) is system-message content.

### 9.5.3 Invariants

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-1** | The dialogue area renders exactly two surfaces — speech bubble (NPC or user) and system message (centered). Speech bubbles are reserved for direct speech (verbatim spoken utterance). System messages absorb everything else: narration, scene cues, the player's own non-speech actions, internal feelings, transitions, body language. A beat belongs to exactly one surface; never both, never a hybrid. |
| **LIFE-EX-VOCAB-3** | **The line that LANDS AS A BUBBLE when a graded question resolves carries its own vocab** (`practicePrompt.lineVocab`), segmented and glossed exactly as a beat is (`LIFE-LANG-10`). Reported 2026-07-31: graded lines arrive as messages with nothing tappable. Comprehension carried **no** annotations at all — the `npcUtterance` is a full practice-language line and the densest thing in the turn, and it was the one line the learner could not look up. Fill-blank inherited the correct CHIP's annotations, which gloss the answer token (`voudrais`) while the bubble is the whole completed sentence (`Je voudrais déjeuner avec toi.`) — one word of a line. `lineVocab` is keyed to the RESOLVED line (completed sentence / verbatim utterance), takes precedence over the chip's gloss on the fill-blank path, and falls back to it when absent so responses authored before the field keep what they had. No leak: it rides the resolved bubble, which only exists after the answer is committed. |
| **LIFE-EX-VOCAB-4** | **`lineVocab` is a RETRY-ENFORCED requirement on the comprehension path, not a field the generator may forget.** `LIFE-EX-VOCAB-3` added the field and described what it is for; nothing made its absence cost anything, and a count over stored playthrough artefacts put **25% of comprehension questions on screen with no `lineVocab` at all** — the `npcUtterance` bubble landing untappable, which is the exact defect `LIFE-EX-VOCAB-3` exists to prevent. The generator satisfied the rule by doing nothing. `vocabViolations` now joins `leakViolations` / `directionViolations` in `runGradedQuestionGenerate`'s retry ladder: a `comprehension` result with a missing or empty `lineVocab` is a VIOLATION and buys one hinted retry naming the omission. Deliberately a violation rather than a schema requirement — the bubble is still usable without it (the learner keeps `nativeText`), so a hard schema reject would trade a tappable line for a dead turn, which is the worse failure (`LIFE-NODE-16`). Fill-blank is exempt: its resolved bubble legitimately falls back to the correct chip's annotations. |
| **LIFE-EX-8.2** | **The native anchor is shown on EVERY fill-blank. `disambiguation` no longer decides whether the player sees it.** It used to: `translation-anchor` showed `completedNativeText`, `context-anchor` hid it on the generator's own claim that the sentence discriminated. Owner-reported twice, most recently 2026-08-05 — *"any word could work and there was no target sentence"* — and the earlier report is already recorded in `LIFE-EX-8.1`, which fixed the same complaint for the type-to-fill variant ONLY. **Measured before changing anything**: 360 `context-anchor` fill-blanks from 17 stored runs, each wrong chip classified by the reason THE GENERATOR ITSELF gave for it. **91% were separated purely by MEANING, with no grammatical cue anywhere in the set** — precisely the case the design reserves for `translation-anchor`. Real examples, all declared `context-anchor`: *"War der ___ heute schon hier?"* against teacher / driver / visitor / **director**; *"Maestro, quiero ___ con el Codex"* against to study / to speak / to work. Every chip grammatical, every chip sensible, only the translation picks one. So this is not the generator judging badly at the margin — it is **asserting a discriminator that nothing verifies**, the same shape as every other self-declared field that turned out to be decorative. The reliable fix is to stop trusting the flag rather than to try to make the self-assessment accurate. **Cost is near zero**: `completedNativeText` is already REQUIRED on every fill-blank (`runGradedQuestionGenerate` rejects it as missing) and was present on **419 of 419** generated across those runs, so there is nothing to fall back to and no data risk. The ~9% of sets that genuinely turn on FORM (*"Je ___ un café"* → veux / veut / veulent) show a native that does not give the answer away, because the question there is the agreement, not the verb — redundant, not spoiling. `disambiguation` STAYS on the schema: it still shapes how the generator builds a set, it is simply no longer allowed to remove the anchor from the player. `PracticePromptCard`'s `production` prop is likewise retained but inert — it existed only to force the anchor on for type-to-fill. |
| **LIFE-LANG-17** | **"It means:" only appears when there IS a gloss.** The label was unconditional while the native text beside it was conditional, so a correct fill-blank — whose chip legitimately carries `nativeText: ''` (`LIFE-LANG-1`: a gloss on the chip would telegraph the answer) — rendered "It means: voudrais", asserting that the word means itself. The meaning of a fill-blank answer lives on the full-sentence row below (`LIFE-LANG-6`), which always renders. The WRONG-answer label ("The correct answer was:") makes no claim about meaning and is unaffected. |
| **LIFE-UI-11** | **The `live-loading` bar names what the STORY is doing, not what the app is doing.** It said "Thinking…" on every turn — the app narrating its own machinery mid-scene, on the element the player sees more often than any other. `waitKindForTurn` derives one of `opening / say / hear / act / write / resume / outcome` from the action that caused the wait, and the bar renders `life.wait.<kind>`. **Keyed, not randomised:** random rotation on a 2-3s element that fires every turn reads as noise and would need seeding to stay stable across a re-render mid-wait, whereas action-keyed copy is already varied in practice (the player's actions vary) AND meaningful — the line says what is being waited on. Precedence matters and is the whole behaviour: a practice outcome wins over a same-turn choice; empty free text is NOT a write and falls through; an undeclared `speechAct` floors at `act`, which the step SI documents as the default. Falls back to the old generic line if the kind is unknown. |
| **LIFE-DAY-1** | **The player-facing hierarchy is DAY → CHAPTER → STORY.** A resolved scene is a **day** in the character's life, several days make a chapter (`LIFE-WORLD-ARC-*`), and the saga is the story. The completion screen's eyebrow reads "Day complete". Two rejected namings, both instructive: **"Scene complete"** is internal jargon the player meets nowhere else; **"Story complete"** is worse, because story is the TOP level — it reads as the whole wurld ending. The day framing also carries the intended product shape: **one scene per day**, to bound token spend, with the screen inviting the player back rather than dead-ending them. **The "come back tomorrow" teaser is suppressed when `arcStage === 'concluding'`** — that scene also ends the chapter, so `WorldArcCompleteScreen` follows immediately with its own *Continue your wurld* CTA, and inviting the player back tomorrow one beat before offering to carry on now contradicts itself. **NOT YET ENFORCED:** the one-per-day limit is copy only at this point; nothing stops a second scene starting today. Until the gate lands, the teaser is an invitation, not a statement of fact. |
| **LIFE-DAY-2** | **The chapter screen is a NARRATIVE recap, not a second statistics screen.** It renders immediately after the day screen when `arcStage === 'concluding'`, and the two had become near-identical — eyebrow, title, outcome quote, stat grid, practised-words list — so the player met the same layout twice in a row. Worse, the day's practised words are a SUBSET of the chapter's, so the same rows appeared again with a second set of save buttons that knew nothing about the first. The chapter screen therefore DROPS the stat grid and the practised-words list (both already shown, per-day, where they are actionable) and instead recaps **the days the chapter was made of** — one line per resolved scene, from `arc.completedSceneMemories[]`, which is the one thing the day screen cannot show. It KEEPS the chapter's story vocabulary (`LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-3/4`, saveable) and the recurring cast, and adds a forward-looking teaser. **The teaser is deliberately NON-SPECIFIC:** the next chapter's arc is not generated until *Continue* is pressed, so naming its content here would be invention — the real teaser (`arc.playerTeaser`) shows on the resume screen once the arc exists. |
| **LIFE-DAY-3** | **The day summary offers a practice session built from what the player got WRONG.** Practice mode's summary has always offered "Review N mistakes"; the wurld summary showed you what you missed and then dead-ended. The gap was never data — `PractisedWord.correct` was already there. Routes through the EXISTING `/cards/practice?intent=…&count=…` contract rather than a wurld-specific session type: the planner already weights the USER INTENT block, and `PRACTICE-PLAN-38/39` is what makes an intent-driven session build around the intent instead of borrowing unrelated deck cards — a second session shape would have to re-earn all of that. **Only `correct === false` counts**: `null` is ungraded (met in the story, never tested) and quizzing it would invent a failure the player never had. The intent carries each word WITH its gloss ("tablier (apron)") because the bare surface form is ambiguous to the planner. `count` tracks the number of mistakes, so a two-mistake day is a two-question session rather than being padded with cards the player never got wrong. Capped at `MAX_MISSED_IN_QUIZ` (12) so the intent stays a prompt, not a corpus. **No mistakes ⇒ no CTA** — an empty "practise your mistakes" session is worse than no button. The screen builds the href and the caller owns routing, so the overlay stays presentational and testable without a Router. |
| **LIFE-ARC-PACE-4** | **The DAY has a turn budget, and the model can see it.** A chapter had `storyLog.turns` and a `<chapterPace>` block; the scene — which IS a day (`LIFE-DAY-1`) — had neither, so "bring this day to a close" was stated in a quantity the model could not observe, the exact defect `renderChapterPace` exists to fix one level up. Days therefore ended wherever a `completionTrigger` happened to fire: *"the day can end when the story has stated it's mid-morning"* (owner, 2026-08-01). `storyLog.sceneTurns` counts generated turns scoped to the scene, re-scoping when `storyLog.sceneId` changes rather than on resolve, so a resolve/start pair cannot leave a stale total behind; an **undefined** `sceneId` means "caller doesn't know" and leaves the count alone, since treating it as a change would zero the day on every such commit. `renderScenePace` emits `<dayPace turns stage ceiling>` at 6/9/12, escalating in-range → due → overdue, and the due tier asks the narration to move into the evening — which is also the wind-down the player asked to feel coming. **Emitted ALONGSIDE `<chapterPace>`, never instead of it**: they bound different units, and the model needs both to know whether it is closing a day or a chapter. `SCENE_OVERDUE < CHAPTER_IN_RANGE` is asserted, because the 2026-08-01 regression was exactly these two collapsing onto each other. |
| **LIFE-DAY-4** | **A day that also ends the chapter leads to the chapter summary, not home.** When the resolving scene carries `arcStage: 'concluding'`, the day-complete screen is the only thing between the player and the chapter summary — so "Return home" left the larger milestone unmarked and its summary somewhere the player had no reason to look. The primary button becomes "See chapter summary" and a celebration banner (`LifeScreen-SceneComplete-ChapterEnd`) says why it changed. An ordinary day is untouched: same copy, same destination, no banner. |
| **LIFE-DAY-5** | **Practice launched from a wurld returns to that wurld.** `SessionSummary` defaults its exit to `/cards`, which is right from the deck and wrong here — the player was mid-story, and finishing a missed-words drill dropped them on a screen they were not using with the world to find again by hand. `missedQuizHref` takes a `returnTo` and carries it as `from`; `PracticeScreen` reads it into `backHref`. **It rides the URL, not a store**, because practice is a full navigation and a store would not survive a reload or a cold open of the link. **Only same-origin absolute paths are honoured** (`/…` but not `//host` and not a protocol): `from` is attacker-supplied via any shared link, so anything looser is an open redirect. |
| **LIFE-DAY-6** | **The per-type stats are SCORES, not tallies.** The day grid showed `Practice questions: 10 · Accuracy: 70% · Fill-blank: 6 · Comprehension: 4` — one accuracy for the whole day, and two bare counts. So a player who aced every fill-blank and missed every comprehension read exactly the same grid as one who was evenly mediocre, and the request was for a *score* per exercise type (owner, 2026-08-01: *"just show total score across different exercise types"*). Each type cell now renders `correct/total` (`4/6`), from `fillBlankCorrect` / `comprehensionCorrect` on the completion snapshot. **The overall accuracy row stays** — the per-type fractions do not replace it, because two fractions do not read as a day's result at a glance. **A type with no questions renders `0/0`, not a hidden cell**: a day with no comprehension questions is a fact about the day worth seeing, and a grid whose cells come and go is harder to read than one that is always four. Counting is on the SAME pass as `gradedCount` (`snapshotSceneCompletion`), so a correction that carries no `kind` still lands in the day total without inflating either type — the two fractions are allowed not to sum to the total, and asserting that they must would be asserting the model always tags its kinds. The snapshot is in-memory only (React state, never a Firestore doc), so there is no old-shape back-compat to hold. |
| **LIFE-LANG-19** | **The correct answer's gloss is always GENERATED; it is only withheld from the tappable chips.** A fill-blank chip carries an EMPTY `nativeText` by mandate (`functions/src/prompts/lifeGradedQuestion.ts`, server-owned since SRV-PROMPT-8..13 batch 7) because a gloss on a tappable answer telegraphs it, and `LIFE-LANG-17` then suppressed the "It means:" label rather than print "It means: voudrais" — a word asserting it means itself.
