Both rules are right; together they lost something real, because after answering the learner should see what the word they just practised MEANS. **No new field:** `practicePrompt.lineVocab` (`LIFE-EX-VOCAB-3`) already segments the COMPLETED sentence, so the answer is already glossed there — it simply was not read. `glossForAnswer` resolves it exact-first, then falls back to the tightest chunk CONTAINING the answer (segmentation chunks by meaning, not by where the blank was: "tend" legitimately lives under "te tend"), word-boundary matched so "tend" never matches "attendre". Returns null rather than guessing, so `LIFE-LANG-17`'s suppression remains the floor. The generation side is now explicit too: the fill-blank rule REQUIRES one `lineVocab` entry covering the answer token. |
| **LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-4** | **Both vocabulary sections on the completion screens render as FLASHCARD ROWS with a save button, not chips** (`ChapterResultsList`, mirroring practice mode's `SessionSummary` row markup so the two summaries read as one product). `PractisedWord` has carried `correct` and `translation` since `LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-1`; the chip list threw both away, so a word the player nailed looked identical to one they missed and neither could be kept. **`correct: null` is a THIRD state, not a falsy second one** — it means met-in-the-story-but-never-graded, and gets a neutral marker rather than a cross. **Story vocab (`LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-3` key words) is saveable too:** whether a word happened to be quizzed has nothing to do with whether it is worth keeping, and rendering it reference-only made a whole chapter's vocabulary unsaveable. A word with NO gloss renders but carries no save button — a flashcard with an empty back is worse than none. **The 12-item cap and its "+N more" chip are REMOVED:** rows are saveable, so a word the player cannot reach is a word they cannot keep, and truncating a results list hides the thing a summary exists to show. The `LifeScreen-VocabRecap` testid is retained (the section is unchanged — only its rendering), so `life-flow.spec.ts` still selects it; rows are `-Row`. |
| **LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-5** | **The recap list holds VOCABULARY, because that is what its heading claims.** Reported 2026-08-01: the screen says *"Words you practised"* and shows whole messages. Both halves were working as built — a `correction` event carries `correctAnswer`, which for a fill-blank IS a word (`difficultés`) and for word-order / comprehension is the entire sentence — so one list held two kinds of thing under a heading that describes only one. `isVocabSized` gates entry on `MAX_RECAP_WORDS` (4) **and** the absence of terminal `.!?`: length alone is not the tell, since *"Je ne sais pas."* is four words and still a line. The threshold is deliberately generous because real vocabulary is routinely multi-word (`junto al río`, `por la tarde`, separable verbs, fixed expressions) and cutting at one token would discard most of what the player learned. **Nothing is lost from the player's RESULTS**: scene stats already score every exercise type separately, and the missed-words quiz rebuilds from the graded outcomes — what a sentence is excluded from is only the list that calls itself words. |
| **LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-6** | **The day's list is fed by the whole DAY, not only by what happened to be graded.** `LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-5` fixed the heading's honesty by dropping sentence-shaped entries, which left the other half of the trade-off: on a day with two graded questions the list is two rows, and the vocabulary the player actually READ across the day's beats is nowhere. Owner, 2026-08-01: *"the words and sentence trade off can be solved by generating a list of words from the whole day, if that's not too costly."* **It costs nothing** — `deriveKeyWords` is already zero-LLM and zero-read (it frequency-ranks `complexWords` off the beat payloads the chapter screen has always used), so the day simply runs it over its own window. **One list, not a second section.** The list already holds untested items — a flashcard saved mid-day renders with the neutral `correct: null` marker — so story words are the same kind of thing it was already showing, and a second section on the day screen would reproduce exactly the duplication `LIFE-DAY-2` removed from the chapter screen. **Graded words come FIRST and win the dedupe**, so a word the player was tested on keeps its ✓/✗ rather than being flattened to untested by a later story hit; matching is case-insensitive on the trimmed surface form, the same key `derivePractisedWords` uses internally. The story contribution is capped (`DAY_KEY_WORD_CAP`, 12 — the chapter's number); the graded contribution is NOT, since it is already bounded by the day's questions and `LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-4` removed caps from what the list renders. `isVocabSized` still gates entry, so this widens the list without reopening the sentences it closed. |
| **LIFE-UI-15** | **A full-screen life overlay is anchored to the VIEWPORT, not to the scene box.** All four — `WorldIntroScreen`, `WurldResumeScreen`, `SceneCompleteScreen`, `WorldArcCompleteScreen` — were `absolute inset-0` while rendering inside `LifeScreen`'s `relative flex-1` scene container, which spans neither the header above it nor the action bar below. So `items-center` centred them in a box the player cannot see the edges of, and the card sat visibly high with the blurred backdrop stopping mid-screen (owner screenshot, 2026-08-01: *"the modal is also not vertically centred"* — reported for the day summary, but it was one cause and four symptoms). They are now `fixed inset-0`, so the containing block is the viewport regardless of where they are mounted in the tree. **Going fixed makes the z-index global**, so the scoped `z-40` / `z-50` are replaced by the shared scale: completion screens take `--z-modal` (they are dialogs, and one may open over a resume recap) and the intro / resume take `--z-life-overlay` (1150) so that ordering survives. **Padding is the safe area, not a guess**: the shell carries `p-4 pb-safe-bottom pt-safe-top` and the card `max-h-full`, which resolves against the padded content box — replacing a `max-h-[calc(100dvh-2rem)]` that knew about neither inset. `LIFE-UI-14`'s rule (cap the card and scroll INSIDE it) is unchanged and is what keeps a long recap from overflowing both ways. |
| **LIFE-UI-16** | **The debug overlay renders through the shared `BottomSheet`, not a hand-rolled shell.** It was `fixed inset-0 flex items-end sm:items-center` around a `max-h-[80dvh]` card: bottom-docked on a phone with NO safe-area padding, so the card ran under the home indicator and its footer was unreachable. Reported twice — *"the debug modal no longer scrolls and goes off screen at the bottom"*, then *"still not centred and goes off the bottom on mobile"* — the second time with the fix attached: *"base it off the phone number region picked dialogue and it will work"* (owner, 2026-08-01). `CountryDialPicker` renders through `BottomSheet`, which already owns every part of this: safe-area padding via `.pb-keyboard`, the `.max-h-sheet` cap, drag-to-dismiss, inner-scroll ownership, and **centring on desktop rather than docking**. `keyboardInset="none"` because the overlay contains no input at all, so it must not spend height clearing a keyboard that never opens. `LifeScreen-DebugLog` moves onto the inner flex column, and the tab bodies keep their `flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-y-auto`. **The general rule this instances:** a full-screen surface in this app does not hand-roll its shell — the fixes for safe areas, keyboards and desktop centring are already made once, in the primitive. |
| **LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-7** | **A graded word carries its gloss, so the word you got WRONG is the one you can save.** The `correction` event wrote `correctAnswer` and nothing else, so `derivePractisedWords` set `translation: null` for every graded row — and `LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-4` gives no save button to a row with no gloss, because a flashcard with an empty back is worse than none. The result was exactly backwards: `voudrais`, marked ✗, was the one row on the day summary the player could not keep (owner, on the day-summary screenshot, 2026-08-01). **The gloss was already computed** — `glossForAnswer` (`LIFE-LANG-19`) resolves it out of `practicePrompt.lineVocab` for the feedback overlay's "It means:" line, at the same moment the outcome is built — so `PracticeOutcome` gains `correctAnswerNative` and carries it into the event rather than anything new being generated or requested. **Written only when non-empty**, since Firestore rejects `undefined` and a `null` gloss is what "unknown" already means. `localCorrectionEvent` mirrors it, or the optimistic row would show a save button that vanished when the listener's copy arrived. **Not added to the prompt**: `PracticeOutcomeForPrompt` is a separate, narrower type that renders explicit attributes, so the model's context is unchanged. Older events have no gloss and keep rendering read-only — this fixes new days, it does not backfill. |
| **LIFE-FAIL-10** | **The once-per-world and once-per-chapter generations get ONE retry.** From a real session log: `[LIFE/WORLD-ARC-FAILED] worldArc generateGemini threw — falling back { err: "TypeError: Load failed" }` at chapter 1. `TypeError: Load failed` is WebKit's wording for a transport failure, and the telemetry confirms it — there is no `life_world_arc` row in that session at all, while the saga and bio calls either side recorded normally. **One dropped socket cost the whole chapter its arc**, because an arc is generated exactly once and nothing later re-attempts it; `runWorldArcGenerate` caught, logged, and fell straight to `trivialArc`. The saga has the same shape and a larger blast radius (every downstream prompt reads it), so both now wrap their call in `generateOnceWithRetry`. **What is NOT retried:** an `AppError` carrying `auth/session-expired` or `network/unauthorized` — we reached a definite answer and a second attempt cannot change it, so retrying only delays the fallback the player is waiting on. Everything else (the transport `TypeError`, a 5xx, a rate-limit, the client-side timeout) gets one more attempt. **The SECOND error is the one that propagates**, since it is the one that decided the outcome. The retry emits a `lifeDebug` line, so a recovered failure is still visible — a retry that leaves no trace makes the failure rate unobservable. Precedent: `generateNpcAvatarWithRetry` (`LIFE-FAIL-3/5`) is the same one-retry-then-graceful-terminal shape. |
| **LIFE-FAIL-11** | **A complete JSON object followed by trailing prose is still a good object** (`lib/life/parseJsonLoose.ts`). Found 2026-08-03 while probing the saga generator: **2 of 6** real `life_world_saga` responses failed `JSON.parse` with *"Unexpected non-whitespace character AFTER JSON"* — a valid, complete object with junk appended. The cost is out of all proportion to the defect: `runWorldSagaGenerate` retries a THROWN call but **not** a parse failure, so a null parse fell straight through to `trivialSaga` and the player silently received the PLACEHOLDER world instead of the saga the model had written correctly. That is the same user-visible failure `LIFE-WORLD-SAGA-9` was written for (*"every realistic wurld was called 'A Life'"*), reached by a different route — and the saga is generated ONCE per world and feeds every chapter, scene and turn after it, which makes it the most expensive parse failure in the app. **Salvage, don't retry:** a retry costs a second full generation (the priciest call in the sim, ~5.5k output tokens on flash-preview) to re-obtain an object already in hand. **A bracket SCANNER, not a regex** — a regex cannot match balanced delimiters, and the saga body is full of braces inside strings and escaped quotes, so the scanner tracks string state and `\`-escapes and stops at the object's real end. **Deliberately lenient about nothing else:** no trailing-comma repair, no single-quote coercion, no comment stripping — those change what the model said, whereas this only ignores what it appended after it had finished saying it. **Truncation is NOT salvaged** (an unclosed object returns failure): half a saga would pass `LIFE-WORLD-SAGA-9`'s gap-patching and become the world's permanent through-line, which is worse than the placeholder. Signals failure as `undefined` rather than `null` so a model that legitimately returned `null` stays distinguishable. Currently wired into the SAGA path only; `generateStepOptions` and `runGradedQuestionGenerate` keep their own stricter local parsers — migrating them is a follow-up, and both already retry a bad parse, so they do not lose the response the way the saga did. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-SAGA-10** | **The saga call sends an ENFORCED `responseSchema`, so malformed output cannot be emitted rather than being repaired afterwards.** `LIFE-FAIL-11` salvages a complete object followed by trailing prose; this removes the failure at source — under a schema the decoder is constrained to the object, so a fence, a preamble or a trailing sentence is not producible. The plumbing already existed end-to-end (`GeminiGenerateRequest.responseSchema` → `geminiGenerate` → `generationConfig`) and the practice generators have used it for months; the saga simply never did. **Measured 2026-08-03, real Gemini, 6 seeds × 2 arms:** strict-`JSON.parse` failures **1/6 → 0/6**; teaser question marks 0/6 both arms (`LIFE-STORY-STAGE-5` holds under the schema); cliché `___ de ___` titles 3/6 → 1/6 (n=6, treat as noise, but it is certainly not worse). **The prose did not suffer, which was the risk worth measuring** — this is the most creative call in the app, and a constrained decoder could plausibly have flattened it. **`LIFE-FAIL-11` STAYS**: a schema is not enforced on the `MAX_TOKENS` path, where the object is genuinely truncated, and belt-and-braces on the one call that is generated once per world and feeds everything after it is cheap. **What is `required` is the whole design question.** `LIFE-WORLD-SAGA-9` is explicit that a MISSING field is not a bad saga — `antagonist`, `midpointTwist` and `climax` are heightened-genre furniture that a grounded world legitimately has none of, and requiring them would force a villain into every realistic story, re-creating the defect that invariant was written for. They are therefore declared and NOT required, and the gap-patching still bridges Zod (which does require them) to the schema (which does not). **But the same run exposed the cost of that choice:** under a schema the model omits a non-required field far more readily than in free JSON — a `wandering_bard` saga dropped `antagonist` entirely, which never happened in the unschema'd arm. `centralConflict` was therefore moved INTO `required`: a dramatic question is not genre furniture, every story has one including the conference-presentation world `LIFE-WORLD-SAGA-9` cites, so requiring it costs that invariant nothing and shrinks the surface the patching must cover. **Not yet applied to the other life-sim JSON calls** — `life_step`'s node is a discriminated union whose schema support is weaker, and both it and the graded generator already RETRY a bad parse, so neither loses the response the way the saga did. |
| **LIFE-BUBBLE-15** | **A beat pushed out of the two-bubble window FADES; it does not blink out.** `DialogueArea` renders `bubbles.slice(-2)`, so a third beat's arrival deleted the oldest in a single frame with no cue (owner, 2026-08-01: *"message bubbles should fade on their way out, as they're pushed off screen, rather than suddenly disappear"*). React unmounts synchronously and has no exit transition, so `useExitingBubbles` HOLDS the departed beat for `BEAT_EXIT_MS` (320ms) and hands it back to be rendered above `visible`, under `.life-beat-exit` (fade + a 12px drift upward — the direction it is being pushed). **Faster than the 380ms entrance on purpose**: an arrival is the thing to look at and a departure is the thing to stop looking at, so matching them would read as a swap rather than a hand-off. **Nothing below it moves while it plays** — the column is bottom-anchored and this is the top row — so the exit cannot disturb the beat the player is mid-sentence on. The ghost is `aria-hidden` and `pointer-events: none`: it is a picture of a bubble, not a bubble. **The hook keys on the ID LIST, not the array** — `slice(-2)` allocates every render, and an array-identity dependency would restart the fade on every frame — and it drops a ghost whose id reappears in `visible`, because a turn reset can re-mount the same ids and the bubble must not be painted twice. |
| **LIFE-STORY-LEXICON-1** | **The portentous-noun family is banned BY NAME, on every surface that names things.** Reported 2026-08-01, after `LIFE-STORY-VOICE-1` shipped: *"Still too many titles, chapters and prose with the words echo, shadow, etc."* `worldSaga` already forbade *"The Shadow of X"* as a TITLE — too narrow twice over: it named one construction rather than the vocabulary, and it governed one of the three surfaces that produce this language. One reported session yielded *Le Dernier Souffle de Verre*, *L'Ombre-sur-Seine*, the *Syndicate of Silence*, the *Archive of Unspoken Vows*, the *Echo-Heirs* and *The Final Broadcast* — saga title, place, factions and chapters, all from the same dozen words. `LEXICON_RULES` is therefore shared VERBATIM so the surfaces cannot drift into disagreeing. ⚠ **Two corrections, both from measurement.** (1) It is NOT in the step prompt: `LIFE-SI-LEAN-7` dropped it on measurement and `bannedLexicon.test.ts` now asserts its ABSENCE from `lifeStepSystemInstruction`. (2) Since `SRV-PROMPT-8..13` batch 5 it lives SERVER-side in `functions/src/prompts/lifeLexiconRules.ts`, shared by the saga and arc prompts; the client copy is gone. `src/lib/life/prompts/bannedLexicon.ts` still holds `BANNED_LEXICON` / `BANNED_IDIOMS`, which are the playthrough SCORERS and are NOT prompt text. ⚠ That split removed an assertion nobody had noticed was load-bearing: the client rule text was BUILT from the scorer arrays, so equality tied the scored words to the banned words as a side effect. `serverPromptParity.test.ts` now asserts containment directly — every scored word and idiom appears in the shipped server text — or a word could be added to a scorer and never to the prompt, and the benchmark would count violations of a rule the model was never given. **The ban is on the ABSTRACT use only** — a deck really is salted, a burnt house really has ash — because a blanket word-ban would break concrete nouns doing concrete work; what is forbidden is reaching for one to supply mood. **A list, not a plea**, for the same reason `LIFE-STORY-VOICE-1` is a count: "avoid cliché" is unobservable, since the median output is exactly what the model samples, whereas a word list is checkable against its own draft in one pass. Paired with a positive rule (name things after something the player can POINT AT — a street, a dish, a debt, a date) and a swap test the model can run itself: move the story to a different setting, and if the name still fits, rewrite it. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-15** | **Fantasy leads the mode toggle and is the default; realistic is second.** Owner, 2026-08-01: *"Fantasy should be the default story type, realistic second."* Realistic led because it is the safer-sounding option, but this is the screen where the player decides what KIND of thing they are about to make, and the fantastic row is what makes the product read as more than a chat partner — a learner who wants the grounded version recognises it instantly in the second segment, while one who never knew the fantastic option existed does not go looking. **`WORLD_MODES` is now the only order**: `DEFAULT_WORLD_MODE` is `WORLD_MODES[0].mode` rather than a literal, and `LifeStartScreen` maps over the array instead of carrying its own `['realistic','fantasy']` — that second copy disagreed with the array the instant the array changed, which is the whole failure mode of a duplicated ordering. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-17** | **The seed list is a PORTFOLIO — nine chips per mode, each legible from its own label, each promising something the brief must deliver.** Reworked wholesale 2026-08-05 with the owner; three rules came out of it, and they are the ones to apply when the list changes again. **(a) A chip must be legible without a key.** Owner on a draft label: *"the crew could mean a crew on a boat, and the description doesn't give much more information away. It's only the audience that says heist fans that told me what you actually meant."* The test is now: read the label and hint ALONE — do you know what kind of story you get? Renamed for it: `crew`→**The heist**, `inheritance`→**The haunting**, `season`→**The local team**, `work secondment`→**Sent abroad for work** (British corporate jargon). Two more collided on the word *country* — `country_life` meaning countryside beside `ancestral_roots` meaning nation — and became **Taking on the farm** / **Your family's country**. Enum keys were renamed to match rather than left to drift, the owner having released the migration constraint (*"we are not tied to this list or existing wurlds"*). **(b) A hint names what the universe makes POSSIBLE, not its furniture.** `LIFE-ONBOARD-14` forbids naming the PLOT; it says nothing against naming a standing tension, and the first pass over-corrected into inventory — *"halls of residence, lectures, friends on other courses"* — which sells nothing. Owner: *"Although they can't give actual plot, your descriptions can still give an idea of what's possible."* So: *forbidden shelves*, *a charter nobody agrees on*, *a crew with its own arrangements*, *a miracle nobody may doubt*. Each is a permanent fact about the world, none is an event already in motion, and all 18 still clear LIFE-ONBOARD-14's ≤90-char and hook-shape guards. **The realistic hints deliberately do NOT get the same treatment** — the scar in LIFE-ONBOARD-14 is a menacing backpacking hint, so possibility on that side means SOCIAL possibility (a term that ends, a return ticket, a surname already known), never threat. **(c) The promise must reach the generator, and the pairing is ASSERTED.** Richer hints introduce a failure the flat ones could not have had: the chip sells something `sagaOpen` was never told about, so a player picks the University of Magic for the forbidden shelves and gets a polite campus with an open library. `en.ts` and the saga prompt (since 2026-09-18 `functions/src/prompts/lifeSagaOpen.ts`) are separate files edited at separate times — the shape that drifts (same reasoning as the two `<alreadySpoken>` builders in `LIFE-SPEECH-6`). `seedBriefCoverage.test.ts` asserts every chip is named in the brief, every hint promise appears in it, and every key has a `PROMISES` entry so a new seed cannot opt out by omission. It caught a real leftover on its first run. **What the list is selected FOR, beyond audience:** a **recurring cast** (the canon ledger rewards people who come back — `wandering_bard`, `last_train` and `backpacker_hostels` were built on MOVEMENT and were cut for it); **one home place** (backdrops cache per location); **a clock** ("scene never resolved" was 4 of 4 harness failures in the 2026-08-05 run — seeds with something running out resolve, open-ended ones drift); and **transferable vocabulary**, this being a language app — `restaurant` teaches more usable words than any other seed in the set. `hostel` replaces `backpacker_hostels` precisely because staff RECUR while guests ROTATE, keeping the audience while feeding the cast machinery. `drowned_court` was cut on the owner's call and replaced by `outlaw_trail`; `frontier_colony`'s brief lost its "or western frontier" clause in the same move so the two cannot collide. |
| **LIFE-ONBOARD-18** | **`fantasy` means "a life you could not live TODAY" — which is not the same as "magic".** `outlaw_trail` sits in the fantasy row because the player cannot live in the 19th century, not because anything supernatural happens. Its first generated world came back a weird-west — *"your skin stinging from the mana-flecked wind"* — and the model was **obeying the instruction it was given**: the mode rule read *"fantasy → magic, other worlds, the far future, the supernatural … a 'fantasy' world that turns out to be a village with no magic in it is a bug"*, and `MODE WINS` on any disagreement. Owner: *"the cowboy scene should stay realistic without magic. only fantasy as it's set in the past."* Three edits, because one would not have held: the mode line now defines fantasy by what the player cannot LIVE rather than by what is impossible; a ⚠ line states outright that magic is not compulsory and points at the period case; and `MODE WINS` is narrowed to disagreements about REGISTER, so a seed that explicitly rules something out is the more specific instruction and stands. The `outlaw_trail` brief carries the prohibition in its OWN line as well as the preamble — a rule that lives only in a general section is one the model can satisfy by not looking back at it. `seedBriefCoverage.test.ts` asserts all of it, **including that the magical seeds are not weakened by the exemption** (`LIFE-ONBOARD-16`'s "if the seed says magic, the universe laws must be about magic" still has to bite) — loosening a rule to admit one exception is the ordinary way the other cases go quiet. |
| **LIFE-STORY-VOICE-1** | **Most lines are PLAIN. At most one line per turn may be figurative.** Reported 2026-08-01: *"the text in storylines is so overly written. Every line is delivered like a haunting finisher. It's too much"* — with `"Le Grapheur n'écrit pas avec de l'encre, il grave ses secrets dans la buée de ton existence même."` The session behind it had **four consecutive NPC lines on one frame**, the antithesis-reveal (*X is not Y, it is Z*), which is what collapses a cast into a single voice. The rules are deliberately phrased as things the model can **check in its own output** — a count ("at most one figurative line, count them before you emit"), a named construction with the four real failures quoted back, and a swap test ("if two NPCs could swap lines without anyone noticing, you have written one character twice"). This is the same discipline as `<chapterPace>` and `<dayPace>`: **a rule stated in terms the model cannot observe is not a rule**, and "don't be overwritten" is the purest example of one. The argument given to the model is **comprehensible input, not taste** — a learner cannot parse a metaphor built on vocabulary they are still acquiring, and "the Syndicate waits to devour your silence" teaches nothing reusable — because a pedagogical reason survives paraphrase in a way a stylistic preference does not. `GENTLE_INTRODUCTION_RULES` moved into the same module in this change (a pure move, asserted by the tests that check those strings still reach the SI), because adding the voice block took `step.ts` past the 600-line cap. ⚠ **AMENDED 2026-09-18 — read the next paragraph before citing the rest of this entry.** The module that held these rules, `prompts/stepVoice.ts`, no longer exists, and two of the mechanisms described above no longer ship. `LIFE-SI-LEAN-7` cut the step SI from ~73,000 characters to ~21,000 and **the figurative-line cap and the swap test were among the rules left behind — deliberately, on measurement, not by accident** (`prompts/step.ts:128` records the decision). What survives in the shipped SI, in ported form, is the gentle-introduction rule (`stepLean.ts:102`, the `PEOPLE:` block) and the plain-prose rule (`:104`, `PROSE:` — *"say the concrete thing, not the atmospheric one"*); `LIFE-STORY-LEXICON-1`'s banned nouns were also dropped here and `bannedLexicon.test.ts` now actively asserts their ABSENCE from the step SI. `stepVoice.ts` itself was finally deleted once it was clear nothing served it: its only importer was `prompts/stepTurn.ts`, which nothing imported either, so the constants this entry describes had stopped reaching any player some time before. **The reported behaviour is still governed** — by the shipped `PEOPLE:` / `PROSE:` rules — but by fewer mechanisms than this entry claims, and anyone reasoning from the cap or the swap test is reasoning about text the model never sees. |
| **LIFE-STORY-STAGE-1** | **A world's first chapter runs a COUNTED stage ladder, and each stage states what it may not contain.** Reported 2026-08-03: *"The storytelling is still a bit overcomplex, overly dramatic from the off, and written like a high school piece of creative writing. The wurld always drops you in to a high drama situation 'full of lies', where 'nothings what it seems'. Characters are never introduced, the universe is not laid up before seeds of story line start to take root. Tension never builds, characters don't develop, relationships don't form. It makes for tiring and repetitive reading."* Every existing lever aims at the SENTENCE — `LIFE-STORY-VOICE-1` caps figurative lines, `LIFE-STORY-LEXICON-1` bans the portentous nouns — and both worked; the prose came down. Neither touches WHEN in a world's life the plot may start, which is the actual complaint: turn one already contains the conspiracy. There was also no world-scale clock: `<chapterPace>` counts inside a chapter and `<scenePace>` inside a scene, but nothing ever said *"this world is four turns old and nobody has been introduced"* — so *"let it build"* was stated in a quantity the model cannot observe, the same failure `LIFE-ARC-PACE-1`, `LIFE-EX-CADENCE-1` and `LIFE-SCENE-25` each fixed one level down. `<storyStage>` (`prompts/storyStage.ts`) is that clock: **ARRIVAL** (turns 0-5 — know the place, meet ONE person properly), **SETTLING** (6-12 — ordinary life, a WANT not a threat, and someone already met must show one new specific thing), **STIRRING** (13-20 — ONE small concrete thing the player NOTICES, never a revelation and never explained), then the block disappears at 21. **The prohibitions are phrased as a COUNT the model can run on its own draft** — *"emit zero of: a secret, a lie, a warning, a threat, a summons, a stranger who knows too much, something that is not what it seems"* — for the same reason `LIFE-STORY-VOICE-1` is a count rather than a plea: *"don't be dramatic"* is unobservable, a list of things to find and delete is checkable in one pass. **The ladder governs the FIRST chapter ONLY** (`chapterNumber > 1` ⇒ `underway`): a returning player is not arriving anywhere, and re-running the held stages every chapter would make the saga a series of beginnings. **A CAST FLOOR rides in the same block** (1 / 2 / 3 by stage, compared against the live `<npcRoster>`): `GENTLE_INTRODUCTION_RULES` already governs HOW someone is introduced and nothing governed how MANY existed, so a world could run twenty turns on one NPC in one room and break no rule — which is *"characters are never introduced"* and *"relationships don't form"* exactly. Under the floor the block names the shortfall (*"you have 1, bring in 1 more"*) instead of asking for variety in the abstract. **Verified over a 22-turn playthrough** (real Gemini, production prompts, default `surprise`/`fantasy`, always picking option 1): the curve the report says never happens DID. Turns 1-6 (arrival) were harness straps, carabiners and a descent, with ONE person introduced by trade. Turns 7-13 (settling) brought a second face carrying a want in as many words — *"I am looking for pure amber to pay my rent"* — and five turns of ordinary exchange with them. Turns 14-22 (stirring) surfaced a black sap found under a walkway: a thing with edges, noticed rather than announced, plus a third face. Cast grew 1 -> 2 -> 3 across the stages. **One real defect the same run exposed, and the reason the `stirring` body now names it:** two turns after the good discovery, an NPC EXPLAINED it — *"it is the heart of the tree that is beginning to rot"*, *"the wood never cries this black ink without a mortal suffering"*. That hands the chapter's discovery to a player who has done nothing to earn it, in exactly the portentous register `LIFE-STORY-VOICE-1` exists to suppress. The stage now bans the explanation BY NAME, quotes those two lines back as the failure, and — because a ban with no alternative is not actionable — states what an NPC who knows may do instead: be uneasy, change the subject, refuse to look at it, say *"ask me another time"*, or be plainly wrong. |
| **LIFE-STORY-STAGE-2** | **While the stage says the story has not started, the saga's PILLARS are not in the prompt at all.** `renderSaga` shipped `premise` (documented in its own schema as *"INCLUDES spoilers"*), `centralConflict`, `antagonist`, `midpointTwist`, `climax` and `endStates` into every step prompt from turn one. A model handed the twist writes toward the twist — and no amount of *"don't foreshadow it yet"* survives that, because a rule is a request the draft can quietly ignore whereas an absent field is a guarantee. `renderSaga(saga, { pillars })` therefore withholds all five during `arrival` / `settling`, substitutes the SPOILER-SAFE `playerFacingTeaser` for `premise`, and renders only the FIRST `storyMovement` — the one actually in play. **What stays is deliberate:** title, tone, factions, keyArchetypes, openThreads, and the separately rendered `<universe>` (setting, era, laws, places, figures) — the world's TEXTURE, which is precisely what the early stages are supposed to be built out of, and the direct answer to *"the universe is not laid up before seeds of story line start to take root"*. **`LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-1` is unchanged:** the pillars are still the anchors the story rhymes toward; they arrive at `stirring` (turn 13), and are fully in play from turn 21 — well before a ~25-40-turn chapter has to resolve. **The trade, stated plainly:** the model cannot plant a seed of the twist in turns 0-12, because it cannot see the twist. That is the intent, and the cost is real — early-chapter seeds now come from `openThreads`, `factions` and the universe's laws instead. Revisit if chapter 2 starts reading as a non-sequitur from chapter 1. **One computation, two consumers:** `buildLifeStepPrompt` derives the stage ONCE and feeds both the `<storyStage>` block in the volatile tail and the saga render in the stable prefix, so the two can never disagree about whether the story has begun. |
| **LIFE-STORY-STAGE-3** | **Chapter 1 is an ESTABLISHING chapter, and `worldArc` says so ABOVE the rule it excepts.** The chapter generator's standing instruction is *"this chapter MUST advance the saga"* plus *"the chapter's themes should connect to the saga's `<centralConflict>` and `<antagonist>`"* — correct from chapter 2, and the single most efficient way to ruin chapter 1, because the chapter premise is what every scene in it is then generated from. The carve-out is placed BEFORE the saga block in the SI (asserted by index, not by presence) since an exception read after its rule is read too late. Chapter 1's dramatic question is PERSONAL and ORDINARY and answerable by living — hold the job, find the room, get the neighbour to talk, be forgiven for the first mistake. The opposing force does not appear and is not named; its world may exist as texture the player walks past. No twist, no secret revealed, no summons — *"a revelation costs nothing to someone with nothing invested yet, which is exactly why opening with one makes the whole saga feel weightless"*. Chapter 2 is where `centralConflict` properly begins. **Measured 2026-08-03, real Gemini, end-to-end** (`worldSaga` → `worldArc` chapter 1 → opening turn, 3 worlds at the app default `surprise`/`fantasy`): all 3 chapter-1 premises came back stating what the chapter is NOT about, unprompted by any example — *"This is not about the Sages or the Sun-Core, but rather about the physical labor of living on a floating rock and finding a place to sleep"*, *"explicitly avoiding any hints of the ink's origin"*, *"purposefully ignoring the deeper conspiracy of the vanishing salt supply"*. **This is the dominant lever of the four**, because the chapter premise is what every scene in the chapter is generated from; the openings it produced were ordinary in all 3 worlds (a workshop, an atelier, a mine entrance — each with one named person doing their job). **Honest limits of the same run:** with the chapter premise already establishing, the pillar-withholding of `LIFE-STORY-STAGE-2` changed the OPENING turn only marginally — it is insurance against the step model reaching past the premise later in the chapter, not the thing that fixed turn one. And `LIFE-STORY-STAGE-4` bound on 1 of 3 teasers: two still came back thriller-shaped (*"have you been told a lie to keep you from jumping?"*). That is tolerated rather than escalated, because the teaser is ALSO the player-facing hook on the intro card, where intrigue is wanted, and chapter 1's premise now explicitly overrules it. Revisit if openings drift back. |
| **LIFE-STORY-STAGE-6** | **Chapter 1 is THIS story at rest — establishing is not the same as unrelated.** Reported 2026-08-07 against a `heist` world whose front was an Observatory job with a hard date and whose bio hooks were iron keys, canal-lock timings and a crew: *"why does this first scene have nothing to do with a heist? It doesn't feel like a buildup, it feels entirely irrelevant, wrong atmosphere, wrong characters."* Chapter 1 came back about rent and a coffee downstairs, `recurringNpcIds: []`, and a premise stating its own refusal — *"rather than initiating the heist itself... not immediate criminal action."* **`LIFE-STORY-STAGE-3` caused it, and only half of it was wrong.** Its restraint on `<inMotion>` as PLOT is exactly what stops the in-medias-res opening it was built to fix and is kept verbatim; but *"texture they walk past"*, read as a description of the whole chapter rather than of its events, also removes the CAST and the SETTING — producing a well-made establishing chapter for a different story. So the restraint stays on the plot and comes off the people and the place: `<cast>` principals appear in chapter 1 and `recurringNpcIds` names them, the player's own `<life>` hooks are what the chapter's ordinary question is about, and the world's own materials are what the days are made of. **The line is drawn operationally, not as a mood** — the front's CLOCK does not move and nobody proposes the job — because *"low stakes"* is unobservable while a clock is a fact, the same argument `LIFE-STORY-VOICE-1` makes for a count over a plea. A player who chose a heist spends chapter 1 among the crew, on the ground the job will happen on, doing none of it. |
| **LIFE-STORY-STAGE-4** | **The saga must supply the ORDINARY WORLD its plot interrupts.** `worldSagaSystemInstruction` asked for a novel synopsis and got one; nothing asked it for the life the novel starts from, so chapter 1 had nothing grounded to be built out of and reached for the conflict instead. Two additions: (a) `playerFacingTeaser` is reframed as **the ordinary world plus ONE unanswered question, explicitly "not a thriller logline"** — load-bearing now in a way it was not before, because `LIFE-STORY-STAGE-2` makes it the ONLY saga field the early chapters are written from; (b) `universe.places` / `universe.figures` must include ORDINARY, usable entries — a bakery, a ferry, a night watchman, a stretch of road everyone complains about — since *"a saga whose only named places are the palace and the vault leaves the player nowhere to have breakfast"*. Closed with the line the whole cluster turns on: **the premise may open in the middle of things; the PLAYER never does.** |
| **LIFE-STORY-STAGE-5** | **The teaser contains NO QUESTION MARK — the previous rule was itself the bug.** `LIFE-STORY-STAGE-4` asked for *"the ORDINARY WORLD plus ONE unanswered question"* and the model complied literally: **4 of 4** measured teasers opened grounded and then closed on a rhetorical interrogative naming the plot — *"Can you find a way to relight the sky before the clouds swallow the city?"*, *"…what happens to the ink that keeps the bridges from dissolving?"*, *"Is there truly nothing beneath the mist but death, or have you been told a lie to keep you from jumping?"*, *"…but why is the bark beneath your feet beginning to feel so soft?"*. **A rule the model can satisfy by writing the very thing being banned is not a rule** — the same lesson as `LIFE-SCENE-24` and `LIFE-ARC-PACE-3`, arrived at from the opposite direction: not a request it could ignore, but a request whose wording named the failure. The ban is now a COUNT (*"read your draft back and count them; if there is a '?', rewrite that sentence as a statement"*), the four real failures are quoted back so the shape is recognisable rather than abstract, and — because a ban with no alternative is not actionable — *"unresolved"* is defined positively as **a plain FACT with a loose end still attached**, with worked statements (*"The room above the chandlery came cheap, and nobody will say who had it before."*) and a test the model can apply: *"things a neighbour would mention"*. The stakes clause is banned alongside it (*"before it is too late"*, *"nothing here is what it seems"*) with a genre check — back of a paperback, not a film trailer. **Measured 2026-08-03, real Gemini, 6 fresh sagas across 4 seed/mode combinations: 0/6 question marks, 0/6 stakes clauses**, against 4/4 failing before. The prose moved with the count, which is the part that matters: *"Your grandfather's name is still etched into the wood of the pier, though no one seems eager to talk about why he left."* · *"The neighbors say he spent his final days obsessing over a map that shouldn't exist."* · *"There is a stack of unopened mail on the floor, and the neighbor is watching from the fence with a scowl."* This matters more than its size suggests: `LIFE-STORY-STAGE-2` makes the teaser the ONLY saga field the early chapters are written from. |
| **LIFE-UI-10** | **The options row reserves ONE height across every phase of a turn** (`optionRowMetrics.ts`). It used to render at three: the `live-loading` thinking bar was a single centred line, the `options-loading` skeleton was `h-[3.25rem]` (~1.5 lines), and the real chips sized to content — routinely two lines on mobile. So the dialogue above jumped twice per turn, while the player was reading. The chips get a `min-h` FLOOR (not a fixed height — a three-line label must still be readable) and the skeleton gets the same number as a fixed height, so the swap is invisible; the thinking bar reserves the full grid (two chip rows + the `gap-2`) rather than collapsing. Sized for a two-line mobile chip: 2 × 0.875rem × 1.375 line-height + `py-2.5` × 2 + borders ≈ 3.75rem. Asserted against the SOURCE, not a render — jsdom does no layout, so a render test would pass while the heights disagreed. **Superseded in scope by `LIFE-UI-12`** — this invariant governed the GRID only, and the block above the grid still changed height on the swap. The numbers here are unchanged; `LIFE-UI-12` extends the same one-source-of-truth rule to the whole block. |
| **LIFE-UI-12** | **The loading options block IS the loaded options block with its content replaced** (`optionRowMetrics.ts`, `LifeScreenBottom.tsx`). Reported 2026-07-31, after `LIFE-UI-10` shipped: "the options area decreases and increases in height every time the UI flexes between generating and showing the options." `LIFE-UI-10` had given the GRID one height in both states, but not the row ABOVE it — the loaded state renders a frame row ("What do you do?", `LIFE-OPT-VOICE`) that the `live-loading` bar simply did not have, and the two states carried different outer paddings on top of that, so the block was still about a text row plus a padding delta shorter while generating. **The owner's design, adopted as specified:** the rotating wait phrase (`LIFE-UI-11`) takes the TITLE's slot, and a 2×2 grid of two-line skeleton boxes takes the grid. **The fix is structural, not arithmetic** — the frame wrapper, the frame slot, the bar padding and the grid are FOUR shared class constants (`OPTION_FRAME_CLASS`, `OPTION_FRAME_SLOT_CLASS`, `OPTION_BAR_CLASS`, `OPTION_GRID_CLASS`) rendered by BOTH states through ONE `OptionsFrame` component, so there is no second copy to drift and no measurement to keep in sync; the swap changes only the CONTENT of two identical boxes. The frame slot's height is FIXED (`h-4`) rather than content-sized, because its two occupants differ (an uppercase title vs. a phrase preceded by three pulsing dots) and a content-sized slot would let them differ by a pixel. **Skeleton count is 4** — `LIFE-OPT-VARIETY` asks for 2-3 options and branch turns add the ✏️ chip, so the common counts are 3 and 4, and BOTH occupy exactly two rows in a 2-column grid; the residual case (a 2-option continuation, one row) is absorbed by `OPTION_GRID_MIN_H` on the REAL grid (`ActionBar reserveGrid`, set on the CYOA path only — graded chips and the Continue ladder are not what the skeleton stood in for) rather than by shrinking the skeleton. Matching the typical turn beats matching every turn: 4→3 costs nothing (same two rows), 1→3 would reproduce the jump. **One loading surface:** `options-loading` renders the same block as `live-loading`, so the two loading phases cannot disagree with each other either. The skeleton box uses the `.skeleton-chip` shimmer (globals.css) — `animate-pulse` over `bg-elevated` is near-invisible in dark mode — and that utility deliberately sets NO height, since the height is `OPTION_CHIP_SKELETON_H`. Asserted two ways: on the CONSTANTS (the reserved grid height is derived from `grid-cols-2` + `gap-2` + the chip height, so re-styling the grid breaks the test) and on the RENDER (the frame, slot, bar and grid `className` strings of the two states must be byte-identical). Not asserted: the resulting pixel heights — jsdom does no layout, which is precisely why the measurements are shared rather than mirrored. **This invariant is why `LIFE-NODE-11` can hold the options back at all** — the placeholder now stays up for the whole paced sequence, so a placeholder that was the wrong height would be on screen longer, not less. Selectors: `LifeScreen-OptionsFrame` (the frame row, both states), `LifeScreen-OptionsEyebrow` / `LifeScreen-ThinkingBar` (the slot's two occupants), `LifeScreen-OptionsGrid` / `LifeScreen-SkeletonChips` (the grid's two occupants), `LifeScreen-ActionBar` / `LifeScreen-SkeletonBar` (the bar). |
| **LIFE-SPEECH-2** | **A `say` pick's spoken line rides into the call that authors the REPLY.** `saysLine` is rendered client-side as the player's bubble the instant they pick — but that happens on ARRIVAL, one call *after* the generation that writes what follows. So the generating call's entire view of a speech turn was the system-language menu LABEL (`<choice>Tell them I am lost</choice>`): it had been told the player said something, never what. The model therefore staged the speech itself, and because `LIFE-SPEECH-1`'s PLAYER-VOICE RULE bars it from emitting a `user` beat, the player's words landed **in an NPC's bubble** — reported 2026-07-31 as "my character says it, then the npc says it from their character". The rule was working; the information was missing. `LiveTurnChoice.saysLine` now carries the line, and `<turn kind="choice">` renders `<alreadySpoken>` plus an explicit instruction to start from the moment AFTER it was said. Absent on `hear`/`act` picks and on the graded path (whose line lands via `buildResolvedBubble` at resolution instead), and omitted entirely rather than emitted empty — an empty value would read as "the player said nothing". |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-2** | Speakers and their surfaces are pinned: `npc` beats render in NPC bubbles only; `narrator` beats render in system messages only. The `user` speaker is a CLIENT-CONSTRUCTED bubble — the model NEVER emits `user` beats (`ZStepBeat.speaker` is `narrator\|npc`). There are exactly TWO client sources: `buildResolvedBubble` (a graded line, produced post-practice) and an option's `saysLine` (a non-graded speech pick, `LIFE-SPEECH-1`). No other path to a user bubble exists. |
| **LIFE-SPEECH-1** | **Player speech is a first-class client-built bubble; graded direction is a machine-checked projection.** Every option declares `speechAct: 'say' \| 'hear' \| 'act'` (absent ⇒ `'act'`, the non-speech default), mirroring the chip label. A `say` option carries `saysLine` — the player's leveled `practiceText` + `nativeText` + optional vocab annotations, the SAME payload shape as a beat, authored alongside the option and hidden until the pick. On pick, `arriveAtNode` PREPENDS `{speaker:'user', …saysLine}` as the first bubble, then the node's beats (capped at ONE reaction beat — the player's line already fills a visible slot, `LIFE-LANG-13c`); it flows to persist + overlay + resume through the SAME `user`-beat chain `buildResolvedBubble` already uses. **Post-preview-removal (`LIFE-NODE-2`) `saysLine` earns its keep twice over: it is the ONLY thing on screen during the `live-loading` wait that a pick produces**, so the player sees their own line land immediately and the wait reads as the world responding rather than as dead air. **The direction projection is DELETED — superseded by `LIFE-EX-18`.** What survives of this row is the `saysLine` half, enforced by `optionSpeechActIssues` at the strict-parse seam: `saysLine` is valid ONLY on a `say` option and REQUIRED there (else the pick shows no player line — the reported bug). The *other* half — "if the pick declared `say`, a `question` node emitted this turn MUST be `fill-blank`; if `hear`, `comprehension`" — projected the graded kind from the PICK, repaired the mismatches it created, and is gone in its entirety along with `gradedPlanDirectionFix` and `repairGradedPlan`. **The pick was the wrong source.** A `hear` pick is what the model puts on ambient sound ("Listen for footsteps"), so on 2 of 4 `hear` picks the repair rewrote `sentenceContext` — the line the PLAYER was about to say — into an NPC's mouth (`LIFE-SPEECH-7`); and the `say` half was overriding an authoring choice `LIFE-SPEECH-4` explicitly permits. The kind is now derived from the plan's OWN fields at the parse seam, where it cannot be wrong. **Kept from the retired half, because they were measured and did not depend on it:** the unsaid-line rule and the after-`say` production route, both restated in `renderPracticeCadence` (see `LIFE-EX-18`). The model still never authors a `user` beat — `beatRepair` is unchanged (a novelistic `saysLine` with `« … », dis-tu` is de-fanged for free by the existing wrapper strip). **TRAP the SI now names explicitly (v2.1):** an "ask about X" option is a `say` (the player asks) → its question is a FILL-BLANK testing a word in the QUESTION the player asks — NOT `say`+comprehension (which the machine rejects). To test the player understanding the NPC's ANSWER, the option must be `hear` (the pick RECEIVES the reply) → comprehension. Pick one; never `say`→comprehension. |
| **LIFE-SPEECH-3** | **The outbound fill-blank is gated ON THE OPTION, not on the node after it.** Reported 2026-08-02: *"Questions seem to not be triggering for things I say, ie they are all comprehension questions so far."* They were structurally unreachable. `optionSpeechActIssues` makes `saysLine` REQUIRED on every `say` option, and `pick()` prepends that line as the player's bubble the instant they pick — so by the time `life_step` authors the node the pick leads to, the line has ALREADY been said and there is nothing left to intercept. A fill-blank is defined as *"INTERCEPT the line the player is about to SAY"* (`LIFE-LANG-13b`), and no unspoken line survives the pick. `hear` picks are locked to comprehension and `act` picks naturally produce it (an NPC speaks), so **every** graded moment came out comprehension. The code says so in its own doc comments: `ZPlayerSaysLine` is described as *"the player's spoken line for a NON-graded `say` option"* and `ZSpeechAct` as *"`say` = … via `saysLine` when non-graded **or a fill-blank question when graded**"* — a branch the validator forbids. **This regressed with `LIFE-NODE-2`**: before the lookahead was removed, a `say` option's `next` COULD BE the question node, so the option itself declared the interception; afterwards options carry no lookahead and the interception point vanished with it. **The fix restores exactly that, in the minimal form — one string, not an authored branch.** A `say` option MAY carry `gradeTarget`: the word or phrase inside its OWN `saysLine.practiceText` to blank out. When present, the pick is intercepted CLIENT-SIDE: no `user` bubble is prepended, the plan is built in code (`gradedSayPlan` → `{kind:'fill-blank', testTarget: gradeTarget, sentenceContext: saysLine.practiceText}`), and `arriveAtNode` is handed a synthesised `question` node — the same path a model-authored question node takes, so authoring, the card-match steal (`LIFE-EX-10`), grading, `buildResolvedBubble` and the post-outcome prefetch are all unchanged. **Why the option and not the node:** the model picks the target in the same breath as the line, which is the only moment it holds the level ceiling, `<recentlyPracticed>` and the cognate rule together; the plan is then built from already-validated fields, so there is no *"the model didn't make it a question"* failure mode to recover from. **Call cost is unchanged, not increased** — the node route was `life_step` (question + resolution) + generator; this is generator + a post-outcome `life_step` that is already prefetched behind the feedback overlay (`resolveGraded`), and it writes the reply KNOWING the line landed rather than predicting it. **Degrades, never fails:** if `gradeTarget` is absent, blank, not a verbatim substring of `saysLine.practiceText`, or already in `recentFillBlankTargets` (`LIFE-EX-PLAN` fresh-targets rule), `gradedSayPlan` returns null and the pick takes the ordinary non-graded `say` path — the line simply lands. A dropped grade costs one practice moment; a rejected turn would cost a retry and a visible wait. `saysLine` stays REQUIRED on every `say` option either way, so the original *"the pick surfaces no player bubble"* bug cannot return through this door. |
| **LIFE-SPEECH-4** | **The `say`→`fill-blank` direction lock projects only from a pick whose line has NOT landed.** `LIFE-SPEECH-1` states it unconditionally, and its stated purpose is that *"a 'say' pick can NEVER resolve to an NPC bubble"* — i.e. a line the player was PROMISED they would speak must not come out of someone else's mouth. Under `LIFE-SPEECH-3` that promise is discharged at the moment of the pick: a graded `say` routes to the fill-blank and never reaches `life_step` at all, and a non-graded `say` has already landed as the player's own bubble. So every `say` pick arriving at `life_step` has definitionally spoken, its natural graded follow-up is comprehension OF THE REPLY, and the unconditional lock rejects it. Worse, it rejected it to no effect: the repair would not fabricate a `testTarget`, so the mismatch was logged as `graded-direction-unrepairable` and the comprehension plan shipped anyway. **The carve-out this row describes is now moot: `LIFE-EX-18` deleted the lock entirely, in BOTH directions.** The `hear` half — the one this row left "UNCHANGED and still unconditional" — turned out to be the actively harmful one (`LIFE-SPEECH-7`: it flipped the player's own line into an NPC's mouth on half the `hear` picks it saw), so it went first. **What this row still asserts, and what makes it worth keeping:** a comprehension of the REPLY is the natural and PERMITTED follow-up to a `say` pick. Nothing may re-introduce a rule that treats it as a fault. |
| **LIFE-SPEECH-5** | **A graded `say` whose GENERATOR fails degrades to the ordinary `say` — not to a fresh options set with the player's line dropped.** `LIFE-SPEECH-3` says the pick *"degrades, never fails"*, and it does when `gradedSayPlan` DECLINES: the plan is never built and the line lands as an ungraded `say`. The gap was the failure one step later — a plan accepted, then `runGradedQuestionGenerate` exhausting its retries. That fell into the shared `LIFE-NODE-8` graceful demote, which authors fresh branch options for *"the node already on screen"*. On this path there is no such node: `arriveAtNode` cleared the board for a question that never appeared, and the line the player chose to SAY was never spoken. They pick *"Ask for the key"*, the dialogue area empties, and new chips arrive as though they had said nothing — while the committed `decision` event tells the model they did. `pick()` therefore ARMS the degrade (`gradedSayFallback`) immediately before the synthesised question node arrives, and `authorQuestion` CONSUMES it at entry — captured and cleared unconditionally, so a fallback can only ever apply to the question it was set for and can never leak into a model-authored one. On demote it runs the ordinary `say` live turn: the line prepends as the player's own bubble (`LIFE-SPEECH-1`), the pick's `speechAct` + `saysLine` ride into the call (`LIFE-SPEECH-2`), and the action is `{kind:'none'}` because the arrival that showed the question already committed the decision — a second one would double-log the pick. **The card-match steal is also barred on this path** (`LIFE-EX-10`): the player chose a line to speak, and replacing it with a deck review both drops the line and answers a question they did not ask. Model-authored question nodes carry no fallback, so they are stolen and demoted exactly as before. |
| **LIFE-SPEECH-6** | **A line already on screen is stated as already-spoken to the call that writes what follows — on EVERY branch that puts one there, and a beat that echoes it is dropped.** Reported 2026-08-05: *"occasionally when i get a question that's directed from ME saying something (e.g. fill blank) it shows in the events as both me and the NPC saying the line, one after the other."* Same mechanism as `LIFE-SPEECH-2`, one branch over. The `practiceOutcome` turn carried only the graded metadata (`picked` / `correctAnswer` / `promptSource`); the completed sentence the client had just committed as the player's bubble was never in the prompt. The model, told a fill-blank was answered and shown no line, staged the speech itself — and `LIFE-SPEECH-1`'s PLAYER-VOICE RULE bars a `user` beat, so the player's own words came out of an NPC. Two changes, because the prompt alone is a rule the model can satisfy by doing nothing. (a) `practiceOutcomeFromFeedback` resolves the bubble (line + speaker) and it rides into the next call as `<alreadySpoken>`; both branches that emit that block now share ONE builder (`renderAlreadySpoken`), since two call sites that must say the same thing is the shape that drifts — the `say`-pick branch had the note since `LIFE-SPEECH-2` and the graded branch, written separately, simply did not. (b) Belt and braces in `normalizeBeats`: a non-`user` beat whose text NORMALISES EQUAL to the already-spoken line (trim → lowercase → strip quotes → collapse whitespace → strip terminal punctuation) is dropped with a `life.beat.echoed-player-line-dropped` warn. Equality, never containment — dropping a real beat that merely quotes the phrase is worse than the duplicate — and `user` beats are never touched. |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-3** | Origins → surfaces: (a) LLM `npc` beat → NPC bubble; (b) LLM `narrator` beat → system message; (c) CYOA chip pick — speech action → triggers outbound practice → user bubble on resolution (chip text itself never renders); (d) CYOA chip pick — directive → captured by the model as a `narrator` beat in the next turn → system message (chip text itself never renders); (e) player free-text → submitted to the model as raw intent, NO immediate UI render — the model classifies it (directive ⇒ narrator beat next turn; speech intent ⇒ outbound practice → user bubble on resolution). Free-text is intent, not direct speech. |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-4** | **A speech bubble holds the VERBATIM utterance and nothing else.** Observed on beta (2026-07-21): a bubble rendered, under an NPC's avatar, `« Qu'est-ce que c'est que ce projet ? », demandes-tu.` — the PLAYER's own line (`demandes-tu` = "you ask"), wrapped in the quotation marks the UI already draws, carrying a narrator attribution tag, mis-attributed to the NPC. **Primary fix = the SI** (`LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-1`'s LINE TEST is now explicit: an npc beat is the text INSIDE the quotes and only that — no `« »`/`""`, no `…, dit-elle`/`demandes-tu` tag, and the player NEVER speaks in an npc beat). Measured with the strengthened SI: 0/10 authoring violations on the exact setup (a live probe, `tests/experiments/narrative-machinery/bubble-probe.mjs`), so the model now renders "you type a reply" as narration on its own. **Safeguard = a deterministic repair** (`src/lib/life/utterance.ts`, wired into `commitNode` + `buildResolvedBubble` + the graded generator's guard): `stripAttributionWrapper` removes a quote-and-attribution wrapper (fires only when the line opens/closes on a quote AND the residue reads as an attribution, so a line that legitimately CONTAINS a quotation is left intact); `attributionSpeaker` then decides whose line it is — by NAME match first, else by the attribution's SUBJECT PERSON (2nd-person "you ask"/"demandes-tu" ⇒ player, 3rd-person "dit-elle"/"she says" ⇒ npc, anything ambiguous ⇒ keep the model's choice). The subject-person fallback is what closes the beta case, whose attribution named nobody: de-wrapping alone would have left the player's words inside the NPC bubble. Wrapper detection uses a multilingual speech-verb / quotative signal (EN/FR/ES/DE/IT/PT + CJK), NOT a length heuristic — the old char-cap both over-stripped a legit line ending on a short quoted word and missed a long novelistic attribution. Subject detection requires the pronoun in SUBJECT position (clause-start or enclitic), so "she says to you" is correctly the NPC (the "you" is the addressee). The graded generator additionally REJECTS a comprehension `npcUtterance` written as narrated prose (`hasAttributionWrapper`), since it becomes an NPC bubble verbatim at resolution. **Applied at the ARRIVAL BOUNDARY, not just persist** (`LIFE-BEAT-REPAIR`): the repair + the mute→narrator convert (`LIFE-CANON-3`) + the speaker-name/id correction (`LIFE-CANON-4`) are the one function `normalizeBeat`, run in `arriveAtNode` before the beat becomes a bubble, the in-memory overlay, AND the persisted event — so all three agree. Doing it only in `commitNode` (persist) left the raw defect on-screen the whole session (fixed only by reload) and diverged the overlay from Firestore. `commitNode` still normalises idempotently for its other caller (practice resolution). |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-1** | Practice is a non-mutating observer of an UPCOMING speech event. Outbound: player intent → INTERCEPT → user bubble lands with the planned line. Inbound: NPC about to speak → INTERCEPT → NPC bubble lands with the verbatim line. The intercept inserts a teaching moment between the decision and the speech; it does not change what happens. |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-2** | Practice fires ONLY on upcoming speech: outbound fires when the player committed to a speech action (CYOA speech-action chip OR free-text classified as speech intent); inbound fires when the model has decided an NPC will speak content. Practice NEVER fires on narration, directives, or system cues. Practice is ALWAYS before the bubble for the line it tests. |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-3** | Practice is non-mutating. After resolution, the speech event happens identically regardless of correctness: outbound user bubble = the model's planned full line (the practice template with the `correctness: "correct"` chip substituted into the blank, **not** the player's pick); inbound NPC bubble = the verbatim `npcUtterance`. Both resolved bubbles carry their system-language gloss (`LIFE-LANG-8` — bilingual, viewMode-aware, like every other dialogue bubble): outbound `nativeText` = `completedNativeText`, inbound `nativeText` = `utteranceNativeText` — the SAME faithful full-sentence translation the feedback overlay showed (`LIFE-LANG-6` / `LIFE-LANG-12`), threaded via `FeedbackState.sentenceNative` → `buildResolvedBubble`. No leak (the answer is already committed); this replaced the earlier practice-only resolved render, which left the revealed comprehension line as the only npc bubble without a gloss. The `practiceOutcome { correct }` passed to the next lifeStep is for OPTIONAL soft acknowledgement only ("you stumbled but Sophie understood") — it MUST NOT fork the narrative. |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-4** | Never-repeat: the bubble for the practiced line lands during practice resolution, not as a beat in the following turn. The next turn after inbound MUST NOT contain an `npc` beat whose practiceText equals (or substantially overlaps) the prior `npcUtterance`. The next turn after outbound MUST NOT contain any beat that quotes the player's just-said line. The next turn emits only what happens AFTER the speech: the NPC's response, narrative consequence, scene transitions, new CYOA choices. |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-6** | **A question node may carry 0-2 LEAD-IN BEATS: the player's action landing, before the teaching moment.** Owner, 2026-08-06: *"I picked an option saying grab the letter by force, it went immediately to a comprehension question, testing me on the other NPC's response to me doing that, whilst never inserting an event of me doing that."* The question node was defined with **no beats at all** (`ZNodePreview`, and `nodeEngineActions` discarded them outright: `node.kind === 'question' ? [] : node.beats`) on the reading that the tested line's bubble lands at resolution. That is right about the SPEECH and wrong about the ACTION. `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-1` puts the intercept *"between the decision and the speech"* — so the decision's physical consequence belongs BEFORE it, and with no slot to put it in, a pick that triggered a question was silently deleted from the story. It is the same defect `LIFE-INTENT-1` exists to prevent (*"what the player just did HAPPENED"*), reached through a schema hole rather than a prose failure, which is why that invariant's rule could not catch it. **What the beats may contain:** what the player DID and the world's physical reaction to it (Rémy pulling the envelope back). **What they may not:** the line under test, from either side — an outbound fill-blank's `sentenceContext` is the player's own upcoming speech and an inbound comprehension's `npcUtterance` is the NPC's, and pre-empting either destroys the question. Enforced in CODE, not asked for: `leadInLeaksTestedLine` compares the normalised beats against the plan's tested line and the turn is retried, the same shape as the `LIFE-SPEECH-6` echo guard. `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-3` is untouched — there is still exactly ONE resolution and the narrative does not fork on correctness. |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-7** | **The lead-in is REQUIRED after an action, not requested — the same bullet pair proved it.** `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-6` gave the question node somewhere to put the player's action and wrote the rule as forcefully as prose allows: *"when the player's pick was an ACTION it MUST: land what they did, and the world's physical reaction to it, BEFORE the teaching moment."* **MEASURED over 65 archived runs / 712 question turns whose preceding pick was an action: 13.8% narrate nothing.** In the three chapter runs of 2026-08-06 it was 19% overall and **43%** in one world — three consecutive picks (*back away slowly · tug at the mast rope · help stir the pot*) each followed straight into an NPC line responding to something never shown. That is the owner's report reproduced exactly, one invariant after it was supposedly fixed. **The tell is in the same bullet pair.** The half about leaking the tested line ends *"This is CHECKED; a leak is rejected and you will be asked to redo the turn"* — and `leadInLeaksTestedLine` enforces it. The half about narrating the action says MUST and is enforced by nothing. The model obeys the checked one. A rule the model can satisfy by doing nothing is not a mechanism, and writing a third, firmer sentence is the move that already failed twice on `LIFE-BACKDROP-27`'s location label. So the narration half gets a check of its own: a `question` node with **zero beats**, on a turn whose pick carried `speechAct !== 'hear'`, is REJECTED and regenerated with a hint naming the action that went missing. `hear` picks are exempt by construction — the NPC speaking IS the event — and so is a pure speech pick, which the prompt already tells the model to answer with `"beats": []`. Cost: 13.8% of question turns, ~5% of all turns, well inside the repo's ~15% backstop ceiling. On the last attempt the turn ships anyway (`LIFE-NODE-8` — never strand the player behind a validator) and the miss is logged. |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-8** | **A leaked lead-in is REPAIRED, not retried — because the retry had nowhere to land.** Reported 2026-08-07 with a screenshot: Madame Mora's warning arrived as a full bubble WITH its native translation, the comprehension card directly beneath asked *"What is Madame Mora saying?"*, and answering emitted the same line a THIRD time as the resolution bubble. `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-6` already detected exactly this (`leadInLeaksTestedLine`) and spent a retry on it, so the defect is not a missing check — it is **two invariants pointing at each other**. `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-7`, which shipped the day before the report, rejects a question node with no lead-in and hints *"what they did, and the world reacting to it"*; on a comprehension the world reacting IS the utterance under test. With `MAX_ATTEMPTS = 2` the only available ladder is: attempt 0 omits the lead-in → rejected → attempt 1 obeys the hint → leaks → **no attempt 2, so it ships**. The owner's trace shows the pair firing twice in six minutes, leaking once. A retry cannot resolve a rule pair that contradicts itself, so the leak stops being a retry: `stripLeakingLeadIns` DELETES any beat whose normalised text equals the tested line, and does it BEFORE validation. Ordering is the invariant, not a detail — the repaired node is what EX-7 then judges, so a node whose only beat WAS the leak is told it narrated nothing (true, actionable) rather than told off for a leak it no longer contains. In the reported turn the player keeps climbing the stool and loses only the warning. **The duplicate is fixed at source by the same edit**: `buildResolvedBubble` re-emits the utterance at resolution and has no idea what is already on screen, so a leak was always going to double. Costs no extra Gemini call. Both prompt sites are reworded too — a hint that asks for a forbidden thing wastes the retry it is spent on — and `step.ts` now states the resolution explicitly, since the two bullets were a trap read together. **Observability, same cause:** `generateStepOptions` logged `beats: []` unconditionally for `question` nodes, a leftover from when they were beatless by construction, so the one field that would have named the fault was hard-coded to hide it; every node kind now logs what it carries. |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-9** | **The NODE MODEL and BEAT RULES sections may not state a question-node beat rule that `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-7` overrides 130 lines later — the condition travels WITH the definition.** Found 2026-08-09 by attributing the retry rate rather than assuming it. Across the 95 `life_step` calls of the 2026-08-07 three-world archive, **18 (18.9%) were REGENERATIONS**, and replaying every response through `ZRawNodeResponse` (`tests/playthrough/schemaFail.probe.test.ts`) showed **only ONE of the 95 failed the schema** — so the retries were almost entirely cross-turn VALIDATOR rejections. Attributed: **`question-omits-lead-in` 15, `unknown-entity-ref` 2, unexplained 1.** Fifteen of eighteen regenerations — **~16% of the app's single biggest cost line** (`LIFE-CTX-17`: `life_step` is 88% of life-sim spend) plus a doubled wait on roughly one turn in five — were the model emitting a question node with zero beats, **which is exactly what the SI told it to do**, twice, in absolute terms, in the two DEFINITIONAL sections: *"NO beats and NO top-level options"* (NODE MODEL) and *"Question nodes carry ZERO beats"* (BEAT RULES). `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-7`'s conditional exception sat ~130 lines below both, and the model anchored on the definition. **The same shape as `LIFE-EX-13.4` and `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-8`: a rule stated absolutely and EARLY silently pre-empts the conditional case stated later, and no retry ladder can repair a pair of rules that point at each other — the hint argues with the model while the SI still says ZERO.** Both lines now carry the condition inline (REQUIRED after an ACTION pick, `"beats": []` after a pure SPEECH pick); the *no top-level options* half of the NODE MODEL sentence was correct and is kept, since the graded generator authors the chips (`LIFE-EX-GEN-1`). **The change is output-NEUTRAL: in all 15 cases the retry produced a question WITH a lead-in beat — 15/15, none still empty, none demoted.** The player already saw the lead-in version every time. **BUT REMOVING THE CONTRADICTION DID NOT FIX THE RATE, and that was measured rather than assumed — the first claim written here said it would, and a harness run refuted it.** Fresh three-world run 2026-08-09 on the edited SI: retries **18/95 (18.9%) → 17/89 (19.1%)**, and `question-omits-lead-in` per question node went **37.5% → 47.2%**. Attribution re-run with the validator's real arming guard (`pickedSpeechAct` present and not `hear`, parsed from the rendered `<turn>` attribute — the prompt DOES carry it, an earlier reading that said otherwise was a regex that excluded every turn bearing the attribute) still puts **17 of 17** rejections on this one code. So the self-contradiction was real and is worth having gone, but it was not the cause. **Do not bank a saving against it.** The surviving hypothesis, untested at time of writing: the SI carries exactly ONE worked example of a question node, it is the `hear` case, and it ends *"no options, no beats"* — so the only imitable example shows the forbidden shape, which is the `EXEMPLAR-1/2` mechanism (a model follows the example over the rule) rather than a rules problem at all. **THE EXEMPLAR HYPOTHESIS WAS THEN TESTED AND ALSO FAILED.** A third run added a paired ACT-case worked example beside the `hear` one (verified on the wire: all 3 SIs in the archive carry it). Result: **16/88 retries (18.2%), `question-omits-lead-in` 36.4% of question nodes** — indistinguishable from the 37.5% baseline. The edit was REVERTED rather than left in the tree: it grew the SI for a refuted theory, and `LIFE-CTX-14(c)` says a prompt-size change can land on a worse cache block and make every call dearer, so unmeasured SI growth is not free. **Three runs, three different things the SI says about question beats, one number: 18.9% / 19.1% / 18.2% retries; 37.5% / 47.2% / 36.4% of question nodes. The between-arm spread is smaller than the run-to-run noise. This is not a wording problem, and a fourth rephrasing should not be attempted.** What IS kept from this invariant is the contradiction removal itself, justified on its own terms — an SI that says both "ZERO beats" and "it MUST land what they did" is a hazard whether or not it moves this metric — and `LIFE-NODE-15.1`'s honest rejection label. **No cost saving is banked against either.** **Sizing the residual, so the next attempt is aimed:** the retry costs a full ~22k-token regeneration, ~$0.0022 a time, ~18% of `life_step` calls, so ~16% of total life-sim spend, plus a doubled wait on roughly one turn in five. The model complies 100% of the time on attempt 2 — it fails first and succeeds on being TOLD, which is the signature of a rule that is not salient rather than one that is not understood. The untried lever is therefore per-turn INJECTION rather than general instruction: the client already knows the pick's `speechAct`, so it can render an explicit directive into the volatile tail ("this turn's pick was an ACTION — the question node MUST open with one narrator beat") for ~30 uncached tokens, instead of relying on a rule 200 lines up in the cached head. That is the same *code owns the decision* principle as the v2 branch's `composeSlots`, and it is testable with the same harness. |
| **LIFE-EX-13.2** | **The end-of-scene review is ARMED on resolve and OPENED when the turn settles — those are not the same moment.** Reported 2026-08-07, one fix after `LIFE-EX-13.1`: *"I'm still not getting card matching exercise before the scene summary."* `tryEnterSceneReview` opened the grid from inside `handleSignals`, which runs from the MIDDLE of `arriveAtNode` — so whatever phase that arrival went on to set overwrote it in the same tick. `LIFE-EX-13.1` read that as *"guard the writes"* and guarded the two `options-ready` release sites, which is why a scene ending on a BRANCH worked and was the only case tested. `arriveAtNode` has two more phase writes and neither was guarded: a `question` node goes to `question-authoring` SYNCHRONOUSLY, and the graded-demote path to `options-loading`. **A third guard was the wrong fix twice over** — it is the same whack-a-mole a fourth write reintroduces, and for the question case it is not even the behaviour wanted: a scene that ends on a graded question should keep BOTH, the player answering the question that ended the scene and getting the review afterwards, not the review eating the exercise. So `handleSignals` only ARMS, and `settlePhase` — the one place a turn stops being mid-flight — opens the grid, whatever route the turn took to reach it. Declining is explicit: below `CARD_MATCH_MIN_PAIRS` it disarms and logs, because an armed review that never fires would open a grid several turns later, after the summary, reading as a random interruption. Both decisions now emit `lifeDebug('EX', …)` so the reason is visible in the overlay rather than inferred. |
| **LIFE-EX-13.3** | **An OWED review holds the summary chip back and shows the ordinary question-loading shimmer.** Reported 2026-08-07, immediately after `LIFE-EX-13.2`: *"the 'view scene summary' briefly shows before the card matching. Instead, the general question loading ui should show."* A direct consequence of that fix: splitting ARMING (on resolve) from OPENING (when the turn settles) put a gap between them, `onSceneResolved` sets `sceneSummaryPending` at the arming end, and the gap is the beat glide — long enough to see. So the chip claimed the slot and the grid then snatched it away. `cardMatchPending` on the machine closes it: the flag joins `feedback` and an open grid as a THIRD `midActivity` case (the chip waits) and renders `QuestionAuthoringBlock` for the interval, which is what the moment honestly is — an exercise is being prepared, and the player has seen that exact block before every other graded question. **The block is EXTRACTED, not copied**, because two surfaces meaning "an exercise is being prepared" drifting into looking different is the failure `LIFE-UI-12` already records against the two loading phases; the test asserts the two render the same `className`. **Cleared on BOTH exits** — when the grid opens and when the review declines below `CARD_MATCH_MIN_PAIRS` — since a pending flag left set by a decline would suppress the summary for the rest of the scene, i.e. neither the review nor the summary would ever appear. |
