| **LIFE-EX-13.4** | **The owed review NEVER outranks a ready question. `LIFE-EX-13.3`'s shimmer branch is deleted.** Reported 2026-08-08, a day after 13.3 shipped and with it live on beta: *"The ui is hanging on preparing a question"*, and the trace shows the question generating successfully at 10:20:49 with nothing moving after it. `LIFE-EX-13.3` rendered `QuestionAuthoringBlock` from a branch placed ABOVE the phase list, so it also outranked `question-ready` — and a scene closing on the same turn as a question is ORDINARY, not an edge case (the owner's trace: question node 10:20:43, scene close 10:20:44). `cardMatchPending` stayed true, the shimmer covered an authored question, and the turn could never advance. **Suppressing the chip (`midActivity`) was the whole fix all along** — every phase already renders its own loading surface, and `question-authoring` renders the very block 13.3 duplicated, so the gap is covered without a second claimant to the slot. The lesson is the one this file keeps relearning in a new costume: a branch added ABOVE an existing decision list does not just handle its own case, it silently pre-empts every case below it. |
| **LIFE-NODE-16** | **After a graded answer the options wait for the slot to finish resizing.** Reported 2026-08-08: *"the options grid is resizing down from the large 'correct' dialogue while the options come in and the events come in… Hold the options back until the grid is settled to reduce ui churn."* The bottom slot collapses from the feedback panel to the options bar over `AnimateHeight`'s 220ms, and a turn whose resolution was PRE-AUTHORED (`LIFE-EX-PLAN-5`) has nothing to fetch — so it took the no-sequence path and released instantly, putting three motions on screen at once inside a box still resizing under them. `scheduleTurnArrival` gains a `settleFloorMs`: the terminal step never runs before that many ms from the start. It is a FLOOR, not a delay — a turn with events already settles well past it (`lead + n*gap + gap`), so it only bites the instant path it was written for. `SLOT_SETTLE_MS` (260 = 220 + a frame) mirrors `AnimateHeight`'s constant and is named for the same reason `GLIDE_ENTER_MS` is: the number lives in a component's style, the sequencing lives in the glide module, and a silent drift between them surfaces as churn nobody can attribute. The three-way choice (staggered / settle-only / none) moved into `scheduleArrival` so ONE place decides turn ordering. |
| **LIFE-STORY-EXEMPLAR-1** | **No world-creation prompt may demonstrate a specific ordinary PLACE.** Reported 2026-08-07: *"Why is this story taking me to a bakery? Every story involves a bakery."* Measured over the 56 distinct worlds in `.playthroughs/`, the bakery does NOT bleed across genres — `frontier_colony` 0/11, `outlaw_trail` 0/2, `noir_detective` 0/2, `sea_voyage` 0/1 — it SATURATES the seeds where it is plausible: **`country_life` 9/10 (90%)**, `restaurant` 2/3, `magic_apprentice` 5/8, `farm_life` 1/3. 90% is not a setting, it is a reflex, and `sagaOpen` — the FIRST call, whose `places` every later call reads — offered *"a bakery, a ferry, a night watchman"* as its worked example of an ordinary place, with two more bakery examples in its `laws` and `consequence` lines and further ones in `bio`, `worldArc`, `vocabRules` and (then) `stepVoice`. All are replaced by the ROLE the example fills (*"somewhere they eat, somewhere they cross, somewhere they wait, someone who is awake when everyone else is asleep"*) plus an instruction to draw instances from the seed's own setting rather than the generic European high street. `exemplarBleed.test.ts` asserts it over the RENDERED prompts, and asserts the replacement kept the original's point (a world needs *"somewhere to have breakfast"*) so this does not trade a recycled bakery for worlds with nowhere to eat. ⚠ **The `stepVoice` surface in that test became `lifeStep (shipped SI)` on 2026-09-18**, when `prompts/stepVoice.ts` was deleted: this guard was the last thing importing it, so it had been policing a prompt no player was ever served. It now runs against `lifeStepSystemInstruction`, whose `PEOPLE:` and `PROSE:` rules carry worked examples of ordinary people and places — the surface where a bakery could actually bleed into a story. The backdrop IMAGE prompt is deliberately out of scope: it receives an already-chosen `locationType`. |
| **LIFE-STORY-EXEMPLAR-2** | **Variety comes from a per-world CONTEXT, because per-world WORDING was measured not to work.** The same report asked the real question — *"What structurally is leading to lack in variation?"* — and the answer is not another rule. Grouping the 56 archived worlds' cast names by how the prompts treat each name: used as a worked EXAMPLE (Sophie 1.8%, Marc 10.7%, Sabine 12.5%, Marcel 17.9%), listed under `<doNotReachFor>` (Élodie 10.7%, Claire 16.1%, Luc 17.9%, Elena 17.9%), and **never mentioned in any prompt at all** (Elara 12.5%, Hana 12.5%, Paco 14.3%, Lucía 19.6%). Three treatments, one band — a name the prompts demonstrate constantly is RARER than names they explicitly forbid, and neither separates from names the prompts have never heard of. `LIFE-NPC-10` shipped BOTH of those levers and the numbers say neither moved anything; that is why the complaint keeps returning in a new costume (clichés → names → places), because each round added prompt language and prompt language is not the variable. **The actual cause: every world is generated from a context that is, for this purpose, identical** — same SI, same rules, same seed table — and the one per-world signal that exists, `<alreadyInThisWorld>`, is EMPTY at world creation, which is exactly the call that chooses the cast and the places. A model handed the same context returns the same distribution. So each world now draws a `<texture>`: one NAMING axis and one EVERYDAY-LIFE axis, indexed out of two pools of ten by a salted FNV-1a hash of the world id — the same shape as `voiceForSpeaker` (TTS-VOICE-2), and deterministic for the same reason (a world must read identically on every device and reload). The axes name **no person and no place** — handing over a list would relocate the recycling, not fix it — they move WHERE the model samples: a generation older, names that arrived with people who moved, by-names, a world that runs after dark, one organised around waiting. The model sees ONE row and never the pool, since a model shown all ten averages back to the median world. Phrased as a LEAN the seed overrules, so an axis cannot fight its seed; **succession is exempt**, or a world would rename its cast mid-life. |
| **LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-5** | CYOA chip classification is implicit in phrasing: speech actions ("Ask…", "Tell…", "Say…", "Order…", "Greet…", "Reply…", "Suggest…", "Agree" vocally) → outbound practice next turn; directives ("Walk over", "Sit down", "Leave", "Look at the menu", "Wait", "Nod silently") → regular narrative turn next (model emits a `narrator` beat that captures the directive). The model classifies; there is no client-side classifier. |

---

## 9.6 Language display system — `LIFE-LANG-*`

**One practice channel (`LIFE-LANG-7/8`, v2 — 2026-07).** A wurld now renders EVERYTHING the player reads in the story — **narration** (scene-setting, action, sensory detail, the player's feelings) AND **dialogue** (npc lines + the player's own lines) — in the **practice (target) language**, LEVELED to difficulty (`LIFE-LANG-9`, age-reader framing), each beat carrying a **system-language gloss** the player flips to (`LIFE-LANG-3`) plus rich tappable vocab. This deliberately REVERSES the earlier two-channel model (narration = system language, unconstrained, immersion-only): narration is now practice-first comprehensible input, not native-language prose — because narration in a language the player already speaks taught them nothing. `systemLanguage` is now purely the GLOSS / instruction language. Graded questions (`LIFE-LANG-1/2`) still show exactly ONE language per surface; their INSTRUCTION is system-language, their tested content practice.

On top of the two channels, normal gameplay shows the DIALOGUE channel per a **per-wurld 3-way view mode** (`LIFE-LANG-3`); graded turns show exactly **ONE language per surface** — the practice-mode `PRACTICE-Q-PROMPT-1` contract (system-language instruction / practice content, never collapsed) lifted into the sim. **`nativeText` is the legacy field name for "the reading / system-language text"** — usually the user's native language, hence the name; it is deliberately NOT renamed (too much churn), so read every `nativeText` below as "the system-language line".

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-LANG-1** | One-language graded chips. Graded chips render exactly ONE language, pinned per kind: **comprehension** chips render ONLY `nativeText` (a third-person native interpretation — REQUIRED non-empty and distinct on every chip; `practiceText` is retained on the record but never rendered); **fill-blank** chips render ONLY `practiceText` (the bare missing token(s)); their `nativeText` MUST be the empty string on every chip — any per-CHIP gloss telegraphs the answer. **Superseded in part by `LIFE-LANG-17` (2026-07):** this binds the CHIPS, not the sentence — a `translation-anchor` question shows the completed sentence's translation above the template, which gives away no single chip and is the only honest way to ask a question whose options are interchangeable without it. Enforced by hard `leakViolations` checks in `runGradedQuestionGenerate` (one retry with hint, then demote per LIFE-EX-GEN-6). |
| **LIFE-LANG-2** | Per-kind graded layout. Comprehension: native eyebrow ("What did they just say?") + native `practicePrompt.question` (REQUIRED non-empty, names the speaker) + the verbatim practice-language `npcUtterance` card + native-only chips (`chipDisplay='native'`). Fill-blank: native eyebrow ("Complete the sentence") + practice-language template card with `___` (no native scaffold rendered pre-answer; `practicePrompt.nativeText` still carries the full native translation with `___` preserved — schema-required and retained on the record, but not rendered pre-answer; `practicePrompt.completedNativeText` carries the same translation with the blank FILLED and IS consumed post-answer per `LIFE-LANG-6`) + practice-token chips (`chipDisplay='practice'`). The post-answer `PracticeFeedback` overlay is exempt — it may show both languages where present (the answer is already committed; feedback is the teaching moment), and per `LIFE-LANG-6` it ALWAYS shows the full sentence + native translation. |
| **LIFE-LANG-3** | **Per-wurld view settings — the SAME two-row model and the SAME sheet as chat (`MSG-VIEW-7`).** `ZLifeWorld.viewSettings?: { primary, secondRow }` (the `mixed` primary and its `mixedSecond` were retired 2026-09-24 — `MSG-VIEW-7.12`; a stored `mixed` still parses) — the ONLY stored representation (the legacy `ZLifeWorld.viewMode` echo was migrated out and RETIRED 2026-09-29, MSG-VIEW-7.2). **The life default stays practice-only**: an absent block reads as `LIFE_DEFAULT_VIEW_SETTINGS` (`primary: 'practice'`, `secondRow: false`, `lifeViewSettings.ts`) — the player reads the target language with the system-language gloss one flip away (`LIFE-LANG-16`). This is the "only in practice language by default" product stance (v2 2026-07) and it is the ONE place life diverges from chat's `DEFAULT_VIEW_SETTINGS`. **Header controls (`MSG-VIEW-7.7`)**: the 3-way Eye→EyeOff→Languages cycle button is RETIRED. In its place, `data-testid="LifeScreen-EyeButton"` toggles `secondRow` directly (Eye when on, EyeOff when off), and the existing `LifeScreen-MenuButton` (⋯, already to its right) now carries the shared `ViewSettingsRows` — the identical radio group + second-row switch chat renders — above the wurld actions. The cycle had to be tapped up to three times to reach a state and never said what the states were; the radio group names all three in the player's own language. A world whose stored block still says `primary: 'mixed'` reads as the LIFE default above (practice, no second row), not chat's — `LifeScreen` drops the retired block before `fromStored`. **Obeying surfaces**: DialogueArea **narrator / NPC / user bubbles** (resume bubbles inherit automatically). **CYOA options do NOT follow the view settings** (v2, `LIFE-LANG-15`) — they are native-only always. (Chips still render via `chipDisplay`; with a native primary a chip's MAIN label is its nativeText, falling back to practiceText when empty — no bubble or chip renders empty, LIFE-WIP-2.) Legacy narrator beats with an empty `practiceText` render their stored narration plain. **Still excluded**: the location ribbon, TTS buttons (always speak practiceText), graded turns (LIFE-LANG-1/2 pin their display), the PracticeFeedback overlay. **LIFE-LANG-4 still outranks everything**: the comprehension-source bubble shows practiceText with the gloss behind an explicit reveal, whatever the view settings say. |
| **LIFE-LANG-4** | Gate precedence. The comprehension-source gloss gate (`hideNativeForUtterance`) OUTRANKS viewMode: while a comprehension question is active, the source NPC bubble renders practiceText with the native line behind the explicit tap-to-reveal — in `'native-only'` mode showing the gloss as the main label would BE the answer. The explicit reveal works in every mode (a deliberate user action outranks the passive mode). |
| **LIFE-LANG-5** | Why? explain wiring. The `PracticeFeedback` Why? button opens the lazy `ExplainModal` in practice mode, fed by `buildLifeExplainQuestion(prompt, allChoices, displayLang)` (`src/screens/LifeScreen/buildLifeExplainQuestion.ts`, `questionType: 'multiple-choice'`, mirrors `src/screens/Practice/buildExplainQuestion.ts`). `displayLang` is the language the chips were RENDERED in (comprehension → `'native'`, fill-blank → `'practice'`); `options`, `correctAnswer` and `userAnswer` are all expressed in that language. The modal receives the world's `practiceLanguage`/`nativeLanguage`/`difficulty`. The graded prompt + full chip set are captured into the feedback state at pick time (the live chip list is cleared when feedback shows). |
| **LIFE-LANG-6** | Full-sentence reveal on graded feedback. After ANY graded answer (correct OR wrong), the `PracticeFeedback` overlay ALWAYS renders the full practice sentence AND its native translation (`data-testid="LifeScreen-PracticeFeedback-FullSentence"`) — in addition to the existing verdict, the "you picked" row (wrong only), the correct-answer/meaning row, Continue and Why?. This closes the fill-blank gap: fill-blank chips carry no native gloss (`LIFE-LANG-1`), so the answer token alone never shows the sentence's meaning. **Fill-blank** shows the COMPLETED sentence (the `___` template with the correct chip substituted, matching `buildResolvedBubble`'s outbound line) + `practicePrompt.completedNativeText`. **Comprehension** shows the verbatim `npcUtterance` + its FAITHFUL native translation (`practicePrompt.utteranceNativeText`, `LIFE-LANG-12`) — NOT the picked chip's third-person interpretation, which the player already saw as the answer options. The `FeedbackState.sentencePractice` / `sentenceNative` pair is derived in `gradePick` and threaded to `PracticeFeedback`. `practicePrompt.completedNativeText` (fill-blank only) is the full native translation with the blank FILLED IN — distinct from `nativeText` (which keeps the `___`); it is authored by the `LIFE-EX-GEN-*` generator and enforced non-empty + `___`-free by the leak-guard (`LIFE-EX-GEN-3`). This is the one place the `LIFE-LANG-1`/`LIFE-LANG-2` one-language-per-surface rule is deliberately relaxed — the answer is already committed, so revealing both languages is the teaching moment, not a leak. |
| **LIFE-LANG-7** | **System language + capture + setup warning.** `ZLifeWorld.systemLanguage?: z.string()` (the language NAME, e.g. "English") is the **GLOSS / instruction language** — the language beat glosses (`nativeText`) and graded-question instructions are written in (v2: it was the NARRATION language, but narration moved to the practice language, `LIFE-LANG-8`) — FROZEN at world creation. Captured in `LifeStartScreen` from the ACTIVE i18n UI locale (`useI18nStore().locale`) via `resolveSystemLanguage({ activeLocale, nativeLanguage, practiceLanguage })` (`src/lib/life/systemLanguage.ts`): it PREFERS the user's own `nativeLanguage` / `practiceLanguage` NAME when the active locale maps to it (`chatLanguageToUILocale`), so downstream `=== nativeLanguage` / `=== practiceLanguage` string comparisons are EXACT; else the locale's English exonym (`findLocale(code).englishName`); else `nativeLanguage`. Written on the world doc by `createLifeWorld` (only when present). Resolved EVERYWHERE as `world.systemLanguage ?? world.nativeLanguage` (legacy worlds → native). **The setup warning is RETIRED (2026-07-31).** `LifeStartScreen` used to warn when the resolved system language ≠ `nativeLanguage` ("This wurld's narration will be in {systemLanguage} — challenging unless you change your app language"). That copy described the **v1 two-channel model**, where `systemLanguage` WAS the narration language and narration was unconstrained prose. Under v2 (`LIFE-LANG-8`, above) narration is written in the PRACTICE language and `systemLanguage` is purely the gloss / instruction language — so the warning was telling the player something that had stopped being true. The i18n key `life.onboard.systemLangWarning` and the `LifeStart-SystemLangWarning` testid are both gone. **Only the warning was removed — the VALUE is untouched**: still resolved from the active locale, still frozen onto the world doc, still read by every downstream gloss. `LifeStartScreen.test.tsx` pins that split with a pair of tests (no warning in any locale that used to raise one; `systemLanguage` still present in the `createLifeWorld` call) so the two cannot be confused later. |
| **LIFE-LANG-8** | **ONE practice channel — field semantics** (v2; `ZStepBeat` shape UNCHANGED). **EVERY beat (narrator AND npc)**: `practiceText` = the LEVELED practice-language text — narration OR the spoken line, NEVER empty; `nativeText` = its faithful system-language gloss; RICH `complexWords`/`wordTranslations` on `practiceText` (`LIFE-LANG-10`). The narrator/npc split is now about WHO is "speaking" (narrator = scene / action / feeling, nothing in quotes; npc = a character's quoted words), NOT language — both are practice language. This deliberately REVERSES the former two-channel rule (narrator = system-language narration in `nativeText`, `practiceText` empty, vocab only in the immersion case). **user beat**: unchanged (client-built). **CYOA chips** (`ZStepChoice`): still `practiceText` + `nativeText` label per viewMode (`LIFE-LANG-3`), NEVER vocab (`LIFE-BUBBLE-7`) — options going native-only is a separate later change. **graded** (`LIFE-EX-GEN-*`): unchanged — instruction/question + comprehension interpretations are system-language, tested content (fill-blank template/tokens, npcUtterance) is practice. `systemLanguage` is now purely the GLOSS / instruction language, no longer a narration channel. **Legacy** narrator beats (empty `practiceText`) render their stored `nativeText` as a fallback so old worlds never blank. |
| **LIFE-LANG-9** | **The level scopes to ALL practice text — narration AND dialogue AND questions** (v2). Since narration is now the practice language (`LIFE-LANG-8`), the difficulty calibration (`LIFE-CTX-3`) applies to the WHOLE story the player reads, not just dialogue. **Age-reader framing** (how the SI hits the level): a level maps to the READING AGE a learner at that level parses like — 1-2 ≈ an early reader (~ages 4-6), 3-4 ≈ a confident young reader, 5-7 ≈ teen/adult — governing ONLY the LANGUAGE (sentence length, vocab frequency, grammatical complexity), NEVER the STORY. Content, theme, tone, tension, danger, and maturity are fixed by the `<saga>` + scene, NOT the level: a noir murder or a wartime betrayal at level 1 stays dark, adult, and gripping — simply TOLD in the short plain sentences a beginner can follow. The SI explicitly FORBIDS sanitising / softening / "kid-ifying" the content to match the simple language (and models a DARK beginner-level example to anchor it). Effective difficulty resolves via `useEffectiveWorld` (`LIFE-SETTINGS-7`), unchanged. |
| **LIFE-LANG-10** | **Rendering + richer vocab** (v2). `DialogueArea`: **ALL beats** — narrator AND npc/user — render `practiceText` primary + the `nativeText` gloss per viewMode (`LIFE-LANG-3`), with RICH tappable vocab on `practiceText`. Narrator beats ALWAYS get vocab now (narration is practice language), not just the old `systemLanguage === practiceLanguage` immersion case; the narrator keeps its centered/greyed treatment but is otherwise a practice-first bubble like npc (legacy empty-`practiceText` beats fall back to the stored `nativeText`, plain). The practice channel is annotated as richly as chat: GAP-RULE density + must-tag / skippable / group / literal-gloss guidance (`functions/src/vocabTags.ts` `buildVocabRules`), restated in `src/lib/life/prompts/vocabRules.ts` (`lifeVocabAnnotationRules`) — no "1-3 hardest" cap. **v2.1:** the recurring defect is UNDER-tagging (the model under-fills the structured `complexWords[]`/`wordTranslations{}` arrays vs. the pipeline's inline `<vocab>` tags). The fix REFRAMES the task from SELECTION to **SEGMENTATION**: "break the WHOLE line into consecutive meaningful units and gloss every one" (implies complete coverage) rather than "surface the hardest words" (implies a sparse subset → 1-2 tags). A line that came back with 1-2 tags was selected, not segmented — the rule says redo it; only bare function words sit between chunks. A worked fully-segmented example is included. (If still insufficient, the robust fix is to switch life beats to the pipeline's inline-tag-then-parse mechanism — future work.) |
| **LIFE-STORY-RHYTHM-1** | **A scene breathes ESTABLISH → CONVERSE → BRIDGE** (step SI). The complaint this fixes: the world dropped the player straight into dialogue with no sense of who/where/what. (1) **ESTABLISH** — a MEDIUM narrator beat (2-4 sentences, leveled practice language) at the THREE transition moments: the OPENING, every SCENE CHANGE, and a clear TOPIC/TIME shift. It grounds the player (where, who is here, what is happening, the mood) — proper comprehensible-input prose, not a one-liner. (2) **CONVERSE** — short npc beats + the player's chosen/graded lines, mid-scene once the stage is set; DIALOGUE DOMINATES here (the learning moments), narration shrinks to bridges. (3) **BRIDGE** — a SHORT one-line narrator beat between exchanges (a reaction, a small action, time passing). The OPENING turn (`<turn kind="opening">`) explicitly leads with the establishing beat and MUST NOT open on an NPC line; on a scene change the arriving node leads with establishing prose too (SCENE RULES). Length is deliberate: establishing beats are medium, bridge + npc beats are one line. Verified live: the opening leads with a ≥2-sentence establishing narrator beat 8/8 (`tests/experiments/narrative-machinery/opening-probe.mjs`). This REFINES `LIFE-LANG-11` (dialogue-dominant mid-scene) rather than replacing it — narration expands at transitions, shrinks between them. |
| **LIFE-STORY-MOVE** | **A scene must not stall into an endless Q&A** (step SI, playtest 2026-07-22 — a real orchard wurld ran ~25 turns with ONE NPC in ONE place, no new characters, no scene changes). A living world MOVES, even a warm low-key one — and the model drives it through the beats it writes AND the options it offers: change LOCATION (→ ESTABLISH the new place), bring in a NEW FACE (neighbour / family / passer-by — GENTLE INTRODUCTION), let TIME pass, or hand the player a concrete THING TO DO. Movement is **TEXTURE, NOT escalation** — a grounded slice-of-life still introduces new people + places across a session (that is what makes it lived-in), while keeping the register warm and the stakes low. The SI names the failure explicitly: reading `<storySoFar>`/`<recentEvents>`, if the last several turns are one person talking in one spot, the NEXT node MUST break the loop (a beat that moves or a new face, and options that let the player LEAVE the conversation). Companion to `LIFE-OPT-VARIETY` (the options are the lever) and `LIFE-STORY-RHYTHM-1` (the scene arc). Prompt-first; a deterministic scene-staleness nudge (client-counted turns since the last `backdrop_visited` / `npc_introduced`) is the reserved safeguard if empirical play shows the SI alone insufficient. |
| **LIFE-OPT-VARIETY** | **An options set spreads across action TYPES, never three flavours of "ask"** (step SI, same playtest — options were ~90% "Ask about X", turning the scene into an interrogation). Across a branch's 2-3 options the model mixes: ASK · DO (a physical act) · MOVE (go somewhere) · OBSERVE · DECIDE/OFFER · LEAVE — and **at least ONE option per branch set must let the player DO or GO (act on the world), not ask another question.** Asking as ONE option is fine; asking as ALL is the failure. This is the option-level lever behind `LIFE-STORY-MOVE`; it composes with `LIFE-OPT-VOICE` (option format) unchanged. Also (2026-07-22): the wrong-graded-outcome acknowledgement was DE-SEEDED — the SI previously handed a verbatim example ("Tu butes sur les mots, mais Sophie comprend.") which the model parroted every slip (3× near-verbatim in the playtest, the same SI-example-seeding failure as `LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-7`); it now instructs a RARE, freshly-invented, in-world touch with NO stock phrasing, preferring to add nothing. |
| **LIFE-LANG-11** | **Scene rhythm — dialogue-first.** A tunable step-SI directive (`SCENE RHYTHM`) sits alongside the QUESTION + CONTINUATION cadences (`LIFE-NODE-7`): when PEOPLE are in the scene, DIALOGUE DOMINATES — most nodes carry spoken practice-language (npc lines + the player's chosen/practised lines); system-language narration shrinks to scene-setting on arrival, brief colour between exchanges, and speaker-to-speaker handoffs. Runs of consecutive narrator beats are reserved for peopleless stretches (empty place, travel, a solo moment). The SI names the failure mode explicitly: a peopled scene told mostly through narration with sparse dialogue. Balance skewed toward dialogue, not a hard client counter. See `LIFE-LANG-13` for the backbone/intercept/event-budget mechanics. |
| **LIFE-LANG-13** | **Dialogue backbone + question interception** (playtest refinement, step SI). **(a) BACKBONE** — content-bearing NPC lines flow as plain `npc` beats (leveled practice-language + gloss + tappable vocab; comprehensible input). They do NOT require a comprehension question to be spoken — this relaxes the earlier SI "every content line is gated" directive (the spec's §LIFE-EX "gate on whether testing is genuinely useful" stance always allowed it; the SI now matches, skewed toward dialogue). **(b) INTERCEPT** — a graded question intercepts a SUBSET of upcoming lines (~1 node in 3, `LIFE-NODE-7`): outbound fill-blank = a line the player is about to SAY, inbound comprehension = a line the player is about to HEAR or READ (a letter/note/sign). The intercepted line is the NEXT natural thing said, so answering reveals it and the scene continues from exactly it. **(c) EVENT BUDGET** — the revealed line (landed at resolution via `buildResolvedBubble`) counts as one of the turn's ~2 visible beat slots, so the post-`practiceOutcome` live node adds AT MOST ONE more beat (prefer zero — the other speaker's reply or a short scene beat) and continues FROM the revealed line, never pivoting to a different line or opening with two fresh beats that scroll it out of view. **PLAYER-VOICE unchanged** (`LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-2`): the model never emits `user` beats; the player's spoken lines reach the screen only via an outbound fill-blank or free-text (client-built bubble at resolution). |
| **LIFE-LANG-13.1** | **EVERY client-built player bubble costs a visible slot — not just the graded one.** `LIFE-LANG-13(c)` charges the revealed practised line against the turn's ~2 visible slots, and stops there. But `LIFE-CONTRACT-BUBBLE-2` names THREE client sources of a `user` bubble, and the other two — a `say` option's `saysLine` and free text — land on screen BEFORE the `life_step` call that writes the reply, and nothing charged them. So the model was free to answer a speech pick with two fresh beats on top of the player's own line: three bubbles competing for two slots, and the overflow scrolls into the NEXT turn's window. Reported 2026-08-02 as "I choose to say something, then on the turn it shows 2 other events land, and those count into the next turn's 2-event quota". **Stated AND enforced**, in that order: the step SI's `<alreadySpoken>` note and the free-text `<note>` now say the bubble has spent a slot and this node carries AT MOST ONE more beat, and `generateStepOptions` SLICES the beats to one when the player spoke this turn. A stated budget is a request; a slice is a guarantee — the same lesson `LIFE-ARC-PACE-*` keeps relearning about quantities the model cannot observe. The FIRST beat survives (beats are in narrative order, so it is the reply that follows the player's line; the tail is what would have scrolled their own words away). Question nodes are exempt — they carry no beats at all. An empty/whitespace `saysLine` is not speech and does not trigger the clamp. |
| **LIFE-LANG-14** | **Bilingual saga title.** The saga `title` is the evocative in-world name in the PRACTICE language; `ZWorldSaga.titleNative` is that same title translated into the DISPLAY (system) language (`world.systemLanguage ?? world.nativeLanguage`). Both are authored in ONE `worldSaga` gen call — `worldSagaSystemInstruction` is parameterised on `systemLanguage` and carries a TITLE LANGUAGE directive + a `titleNative` OUTPUT-SHAPE field — so there is NO render-time translate call. `WorldIntroScreen` renders `titleTranslation` (= `saga.titleNative`) as a muted line directly under the practice-language `<h2>` title (`data-testid="LifeScreen-WorldIntro-TitleTranslation"`), HIDDEN when empty (legacy sagas — `titleNative` defaults to `''`) or identical to the title (immersion users where system == practice, so the line would just repeat). This fixes both the "title showed no translation" ask AND the latent bug that the title's language was previously UNPINNED (model-dependent — the SI gave no language directive). The `trivialSaga` fallback sets `titleNative` to its own title. |
| **LIFE-LANG-15** | **Options are native-only** (v2). CYOA option chips render in the system / native language ALWAYS — `optionChipDisplay` is the constant `'native'` (no longer viewMode-derived) — carry NO tappable vocab (`LIFE-BUBBLE-7` plain-chip rule), and NEVER show a practice-language form. Rationale: options are the player's META-choices ("what do I do next?"), not practice content — and a practice-language option label would PREVIEW the answer to the fill-blank question that a speech-option leads to ("Greet Sophie" → picking it opens "how do you say the greeting?"; the French form must NOT already be on the chip). The step SI (`OPTION VOICE`, LIFE-OPT-VOICE) authors the `systemLanguage` action label in `nativeText` ONLY (v2.1 — it used to duplicate it into `practiceText`, pure output-token waste + "why is practiceText here?" confusion); a `ZStepOption` preprocess (`fillOptionLabel`) fills `practiceText` from `nativeText` before validation so the flashcard-key lookup + popover keep reading `practiceText`. Scoped to options — graded chips keep their distinct practice-language `practiceText`. Also v2.1: the fill-blank `practicePrompt.nativeText` (the with-blank native template) is dropped — it was never rendered (`completedNativeText` carries the shown meaning). The `ActionBar` renders `nativeText` (falling back to `practiceText`). GRADED chips are UNAFFECTED — they keep their per-kind one-language display (comprehension → native, fill-blank → practice; `LIFE-LANG-1`). This also frees the following graded question to test the very phrase the option implied, without redundancy. |
| **LIFE-LANG-16** | **Per-bubble flip + one-time swipe hint** (v2 — "flippable like message bubbles, with swipe hints"). Every dialogue bubble (narrator + npc + user) flips its per-bubble reveal via `useLifeBubbleFlip`, reusing the chat `flippedBubbleStore` (`useIsFlipped`, MSG-VIEW-3). **TRUE chat parity (v2.1, 2026-07): the gloss flips on SWIPE-LEFT ONLY (`useSwipeActions` `onSwipeLeft`, MSG-REPLY-5, `enableRight:false` since the sim has no reply) — NOT on tap.** Tap used to flip too, but a tap does nothing in chat and made the gloss too easy to reveal by accident on a reading surface; swipe-only matches chat exactly (the tap-flip `onClick` + its `justSwiped` trailing-click guard were removed). The gloss reveal is ANIMATED, and the whole card "flips" (v2.1, 2026-07). **Two independent layers:** (1) **Height glide** (`LifeBubbleReveal`) — the native row glides open AND SHUT on a `grid-template-rows` `0fr↔1fr` 200ms auto-height transition (the chat `BubbleRows` technique). It is SYMMETRIC: `useExitTransition(show, 200)` keeps the gloss mounted through the close (rendered from a `cached` copy, since the parent passes null the instant it un-flips) so the row COLLAPSES gradually instead of snapping — this is what makes the bubbles stacked ABOVE it glide up/down (`LIFE-UI-ANIM`) rather than jump. Under reduced motion `durationMs:0` collapses to a synchronous mount/unmount (no lingering DOM). (2) **The flip FOLLOWS THE FINGER** (v2.1, UNIFIED with chat) — while dragging left the bubble collapses live 1:1 (`transform: scaleX(1 - flipProgress)`, `flipTransformStyle`, `transition:none`); on release it springs back over `transform 180ms` and, past the threshold, commits (the gloss reveals via the height glide as the card re-opens). This is now IDENTICAL to the chat bubble's drag (MSG-REPLY-5) — a wurlds-only WAAPI "max flip speed" commit animation was tried and REMOVED (it didn't reliably cap a fast flick and risked leaving the bubble at `scaleX 0`); the height glide's 260ms already gives the grow/shrink time, and the scaleX is deliberately NOT speed-capped. `enableRight:false` keeps `dragX` at 0. Under reduced motion the flip is instant. **STUCK-INVISIBLE FIX (v2.1, `useSwipeActions`, shared with chat):** when the bubble collapses to 0 width the pointer leaves it and `setPointerCapture` can silently fail, so the `pointerup` never reaches the element handler and the gesture never resets — leaving the bubble at `scaleX 0` FOREVER (reported in both surfaces). A **window-level `pointerup`/`pointercancel` fallback** (attached while a gesture is `active`) now guarantees the gesture always ends and resets, deduped against the element handler via an `ended` ref. The `nativeText` gloss shows when the viewMode already shows it (`'visible'`) OR the bubble is flipped: in the DEFAULT `'hidden'` (`LIFE-LANG-3`) and in `'native-only'` the gesture REVEALS it; in `'visible'` it's already shown (gesture is a no-op). Vocab word taps `stopPropagation` (AnnotatedText) so tapping a word opens its popover without also flipping. There is **NO persistent "reveal translation" button** — the bubble flips EXACTLY like a chat message bubble (the native gloss row appears/disappears), matching the chat `showNative` model. **Discoverability is a one-time animated-finger coach-mark** (`LifeFlipHint`, reusing the tutor tutorial's `setup-pointer-swipe` — reduced-motion aware) that hovers over the NEWEST currently-withheld bubble (`[data-life-flip-target]`, set when `flippable && !secondary`) on the FIRST FEW occasions only: the `lifeFlipHint` budget (localStorage-backed) retires it once the player has flipped `MAX_FLIPS` (=2) times OR it has appeared across `MAX_SHOWS` (=3) sessions, whichever first. The hint renders ONLY while the dialogue is the active surface (`dialogueSurfaceActive` — never behind the intro/recap/completion overlays, and never during a graded question, since flipping the gated comprehension source would leak the answer). The comprehension gloss-gate (`hideNative`, `LIFE-LANG-4`) still outranks — its source bubble stays practice-only until the deliberate flip re-enables vocab taps. Flips are session-only (no persistence), cleared on chat entry (`flippedBubbleStore.close()`). |
| **LIFE-OPT-VOICE** | **Option voice + speaker framing** (perspective-clarity, step SI + UI — fixes the reported 1st/3rd-person confusion in the "what do you do next" options). **Prompt:** every option's label is an ACTION THE PLAYER takes, from the player's side, in each language's natural menu form (system-language imperative "Order a coffee"; practice-language infinitive/imperative) — NEVER 1st person ("I…" / "Je…"), NEVER 3rd person ("She offers…"), NEVER an NPC line. A speech-action option names the ACT ("Greet Sophie" / "Saluer Sophie"), never the words (authored at the question node, `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-5`). Resolves the prior contradiction between the "player is the camera" anchor and "options are the player's side of the conversation" — the latter now reads: options are the player's chosen ACTIONS, not first-person quotes. **UI:** (a) a per-turn person-frame eyebrow ("What do you do?", `life.options.eyebrow`, `data-testid="LifeScreen-OptionsEyebrow"`) renders above the CYOA chip grid on branch/continuation turns, so EVERY turn now carries an explicit frame (graded turns already had "Complete the sentence" / "What did they just say?"); hidden on the idle shell and while the composer is open. (b) The comprehension `PracticePromptCard` renders the speaker explicitly ("{speakerName} said:", `life.practice.saidBy`, `data-testid="LifeScreen-PracticePrompt-Speaker"`) from `practicePrompt.speakerName` rather than relying on the model weaving the name into the question string. |
| **LIFE-INTRO-OPT** | **Options obey gentle introductions** (step SI, playtest fix). An option label must never name a person, place, faction, or thing the player has not yet met — offering "Show the book to Marc" before Marc has appeared presents a choice that presumes knowledge the player lacks (the narrative then scrambles to introduce him only after the pick — bad flow). The SI directs the model to check `<recentEvents>` / `<npcRoster>` and name only already-introduced entities; to point at the unknown it describes what the player can PERCEIVE ("the man behind the counter", "the stranger who just walked in", "the address on the card"), and the beat that plays on pick introduces them by name. **The rule binds OBJECTS / ITEMS / FACTS just as strongly** (playtest leak this fixes — the original wording said "or thing" but every example was a person/place, so props still slipped through): an option may only reference a prop, possession, document, or fact a beat has ALREADY put on screen — "Show her the secret key" / "Give him the letter" / "Mention the contract" are BROKEN when no key, letter, or contract has appeared (the player owns nothing they weren't shown acquiring). To act with something not yet established, the SI directs a perceivable action instead ("Look through your bag", "Search the desk", "Check your pockets") and lets the beat on pick reveal what's found. Extends the SI's GENTLE INTRODUCTIONS block (now enumerating `object` alongside character/place/faction/concept, so props can't leak into beats either), which previously governed beats only. |
| **LIFE-LANG-12** | **Comprehension full-utterance translation** (the comprehension sibling of `LIFE-LANG-6`'s `completedNativeText`). The comprehension `practicePrompt` carries `utteranceNativeText?: string` — a FAITHFUL system-language translation of the WHOLE `npcUtterance` ("It's only the wind, James. Don't be afraid."), distinct from the chips' third-person interpretations ("She is telling him not to fear"). It is authored by the `LIFE-EX-GEN-*` generator (SI: REQUIRED, non-empty, a direct translation not a paraphrase) and enforced non-empty by the leak-guard. It is consumed ONLY in the post-answer `PracticeFeedback` "full sentence" row as `sentenceNative` (`gradePick`); revealing it pre-answer would be the answer. `gradePick` falls back to the correct chip's `nativeText` only for legacy prompts authored before the field existed. Fixes the bug where the "full sentence" line echoed the picked interpretation instead of translating what was actually said. |

---

## 10. Embedded exercises — `LIFE-EX-*`

### 10.1 The two-beat dialogue rhythm

Every linguistic exchange between the player and an NPC unfolds across TWO turns. The direction of the exchange decides which turn comes first:

**Player → NPC (outbound):**
1. **CYOA decision** — concrete narrative options. The player picks WHAT they want to say/do ("baguette", "croissant", "leave").
2. **Outbound practice** — graded fill-blank. The player practises HOW to say it. "Je ___ une baguette" with verb-form options.
3. (Next turn) NPC responds in narrative.

**NPC → player (inbound):**
1. NPC speaks a beat in the practice language.
2. **Inbound practice** — graded comprehension. The player picks WHAT the NPC meant. Choices are interpretations / glosses, one correct.
3. **CYOA decision** — concrete narrative options for the response.

| Turn kind | Trigger | Chip content | Correctness |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CYOA decision** | Default after narrative beats. Or after an inbound practice. | CONCRETE options — items, places, people, navigational actions, OR what to say next ("baguette", "leave"). EVERY option is a valid story branch. Chip display follows the wurld's view mode (LIFE-LANG-3). | None. `graded: false`. |
| **Outbound practice** | IMMEDIATELY after a CYOA pick that committed the player to a LINGUISTIC action (order, ask, reply, greet). | Same intent in N=3–4 language forms. Each chip carries ONLY the practice-language token(s) — `nativeText: ""` (LIFE-LANG-1). Exactly one correct; wrong options are plausibly-wrong (wrong verb form, wrong article, wrong register). `practicePrompt.kind = "fill-blank"` with the sentence + `___` blank. | `graded: true`. |
| **Inbound practice** | IMMEDIATELY after an NPC beat that materially advances the dialogue (a question to the player, a key revelation, a request) and the model decides comprehension is worth testing. | Same NPC utterance in N=3–4 interpretation chips. Each chip RENDERS only the native-language interpretation; the practice-language near-paraphrase is retained on the record but never rendered (LIFE-LANG-1). One correct; wrong options are plausibly-wrong (similar words but wrong meaning, wrong tense, wrong subject). `practicePrompt.kind = "comprehension"` with the NPC's verbatim utterance + "What did they say?" prompt. | `graded: true`. |

CYOA choices that are PHYSICAL / NAVIGATIONAL (leave, walk, sit down, look around) skip the outbound-practice turn entirely. Trivial / phatic NPC beats ("hi", "yes please") skip the inbound-practice turn — the model gates on whether testing comprehension is genuinely useful at this beat.

### 10.2 Invariants

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-EX-1** | v1 supports two graded variants, both delivered through the same chip-grid UI with a `practicePrompt` header: `kind: 'fill-blank'` (outbound — player practises HOW to say something) and `kind: 'comprehension'` (inbound — player picks WHAT the NPC meant). Both are multi-choice from the chip's perspective; the header differs. |
| **LIFE-EX-2** | The CYOA / practice distinction is HARD. A turn is EITHER a CYOA decision OR a graded practice, never a mix. CYOA turns NEVER carry `correctness`; graded turns ALWAYS carry it on every choice. A graded turn always has 3–4 options that test the SAME utterance (outbound) or the SAME comprehension (inbound). |
| **LIFE-EX-3** | Practice resolution writes a `correction` event (not a `decision`); a feedback overlay shows correct/wrong + the correct answer before the player taps Continue. The bubble for the practiced line lands during resolution per `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-3` (outbound = the planned correct-form line; inbound = the verbatim `npcUtterance`). The NEXT lifeStep receives a `practiceOutcome` payload and must obey `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-3` (non-mutating — no narrative fork) and `LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-4` (never re-emit the just-practiced line). The empty `beats: []` array is legal on the turn immediately after an inbound comprehension answer — the previous NPC line is still the "current" line and the next turn just issues the response CYOA. |
| **LIFE-EX-4** | A graded turn's `practicePrompt` is REQUIRED on the API response surfaced to the UI. For `kind: 'fill-blank'` it carries `{ practiceText, nativeText }` of the sentence template with `___` for the blank. For `kind: 'comprehension'` it carries `{ npcUtterance, question }` — the NPC's verbatim practice-language line + a native-language "what did they say?" prompt. **NB**: the `lifeStep` Gemini call itself no longer emits `practicePrompt` on graded turns — it emits a `gradedPlan` instead (`LIFE-EX-PLAN-*`), and the orchestrator runs a focused `gradedQuestionGenerator` call (`LIFE-EX-GEN-*`) to fill in `practicePrompt` + `choices` before returning. The UI contract is unchanged. |
| **LIFE-EX-5** | Free-text input is impossible on graded turns by construction: the ✏️ "write your own" chip (`LIFE-NODE-4`) is injected on branch nodes only — question and continuation nodes never render it, and the composer only opens from that chip. |
| **LIFE-EX-6** | The exercise SCHEMA is forward-compatible. v1 carries multi-choice via `graded: true` + `practicePrompt.kind` ∈ {fill-blank, comprehension} + `choices[].correctness`. Future v1.1+ extensions add explicit text input (true fill-blank with typing), sentence-construction (word-bank reordering), and listening (TTS playback + MC). New kinds need new ActionBar states. Spec must be updated when each new kind lands. |
| **LIFE-EX-7** | Per-question save-concept (the `PRACTICE-SAVE-*` pattern) is available on graded turns where the underlying concept is genuinely new vocabulary. The chip carries an optional `conceptToSave` field; if set, a small "Save this" affordance appears next to the chip after answering. (Phase D wires the UI.) |
| **LIFE-EX-8** | **Type-to-fill** (new exercise input). A fill-blank question at UPPER-intermediate+ level (`shouldTypeFill`: `kind === 'fill-blank' && difficulty >= TYPE_FILL_MIN_LEVEL` = 4) renders a TEXT INPUT (`TypeToFillInput`, `data-testid="LifeScreen-TypeToFill"`) in place of the chip grid — the player TYPES the missing word (production, not recognition). It's a CLIENT-side rendering variant: the graded generator still authors the fill-blank chips (no schema/SI change), and the correct chip is the answer key. On submit, `gradeTyped` compares the typed string to the correct chip's `practiceText` via `typedAnswerCorrect` (normalised — trim, lowercase, collapse whitespace, strip edge punctuation/quotes; **accents KEPT**, they're load-bearing) and routes through the SAME feedback + post-outcome prefetch as a chip pick (`resolveGraded`; the typed answer becomes a synthetic `__typed__` picked choice). Comprehension is NEVER type-to-fill (you can't type an interpretation); levels 1-3 keep the chip scaffold. Feedback overlay + SRS credit (`LIFE-EX-SRS`) unchanged. |
| **LIFE-EX-9** | **Listen-and-fill** (new exercise presentation). A fill-blank question sometimes renders as a LISTENING exercise: the COMPLETED practice sentence (the template with the correct chip substituted, `completedFillBlank` — matching `resolveGraded`'s `/_{2,}/` fill) is SPOKEN via `TTSButton autoPlay preload` (`ListenFillCard`, `data-testid="LifeScreen-ListenFill"`), and the player picks the word they HEARD from the normal practice-language chips (the visible template keeps its `___` — no answer leak in the TEXT; the answer is only in the audio). It's a pure CLIENT render variant of a fill-blank (same one authored `life_graded_question`, same chips, same `resolveGraded` grading) — the only additions are the spoken sentence + the "listen" framing. **Variant selection** (`chooseFillBlankVariant(difficulty, seed)` in `exerciseVariant.ts`): a fill-blank picks `'chips' | 'type' | 'listen'` deterministically from `difficulty` + a per-question `seed` (= count of `correction` events already in the wurld, so it's stable within a question and rotates across them — varied in play, predictable in tests, NO `Math.random`). Listen wins its slot (`seed % 3 === 2`, `difficulty >= LISTEN_FILL_MIN_LEVEL` = 2) OVER type-to-fill so an intermediate player meets all three forms; else type-to-fill (`LIFE-EX-8`, level ≥ 4); else chips. Comprehension is NEVER listen-and-fill (no blank). **TTS lazy-load**: `autoPlay` loads + speaks the sentence the instant the exercise MOUNTS — never before (honouring `TTS-SURFACE-4`: no global TTS pre-warm; the listen exercise is the one surface that auto-plays). |
| **LIFE-EX-10** | **Card-matching review** (new exercise; repurposes a graded slot). An occasional graded-question node becomes a two-column tap-to-match REVISION of the KEY VOCAB from the recent questions — NO `life_graded_question` gen (free, client-built). **Source** (`buildReviewPairs(events)` in `cardMatch.ts`, v2 2026-07): harvest practice→native pairs from the `wordTranslations` of the RECENT beat events — newest-first, bounded to the last `REVIEW_WINDOW`(5) questions (walk stops after that many `correction` events), deduped by practice phrase (newer gloss wins), cap `CARD_MATCH_MAX_PAIRS`(5). This is "revise the words you just met" — it VARIES as the story moves (the v1 saved-deck source repeated identically). **Cadence** (`arriveAtNode` → `nodeEngineCardMatch.tryEnterCardMatch` → `shouldReview(count, lastReview, pairCount)`): fire when ≥ `REVIEW_EVERY`(4) questions resolved since the last review (`count`−`lastReview` ≥ 4, `count` = `correction` events) AND ≥ `CARD_MATCH_MIN_PAIRS`(3) recent pairs. `lastReview` is an engine-local counter that ADVANCES on each review (init 0, reset per mount) — this is the fix for the "same card-match every turn" LOOP: a review writes no `correction`, so the old `seed % 3` trigger stayed true forever. On re-entry the first review lands at the first question once ≥4 have accrued (a natural "welcome-back" review, never a loop). When chosen, `authorQuestion` is SKIPPED (`activePlanRef` cleared), phase → `'card-match'`, the continuation is PREFETCHED (instant completion). **UI** (`CardMatchExercise`, `data-testid="LifeScreen-CardMatch"`): TWO columns — practice (`LifeScreen-CardMatch-Practice`) · native (`LifeScreen-CardMatch-Native`), each independently LCG-shuffled by `seed` (no `Math.random`, distinct column seeds so sides don't line up). Tap a tile then its partner in the OTHER column; a correct pair (`isMatch`) locks in the **practice-mode SUCCESS green** (`border-(--color-success) bg-(--color-success)/10 text-(--color-success)` — the SAME 'matched' style as practice mode's `MatchingQuestion` cell, v2.1; was the brand gradient) and SORTS TO THE TOP of both columns (solved rows align, remaining work shrinks; a wrong pair now flashes in the `--color-danger` token too); re-picking within a column switches selection; a wrong pair flashes ~650ms and clears; the last match fires `onComplete`. **Advance** (`completeCardMatch`): a review has NO narrative outcome (`LIFE-CONTRACT-EX-3`) → a NEUTRAL live turn (`runLiveTurn action:'none'`) consuming the prefetch, `cardMatch` cleared. Kill mid-review is safe (events log is source of truth). No coach-mark (`LifeFlipHint`) shows during a review (`dialogueSurfaceActive` excludes `'card-match'`). |
| **LIFE-EX-11** | **Word-order** (new exercise; build the sentence you're about to say). A fill-blank sometimes renders as a WORD-BANK builder (`WordOrderInput`, `data-testid="LifeScreen-WordOrder"`): the COMPLETED practice sentence (`completedFillBlank`) is split into word tiles, shuffled (`buildWordBank`, deterministic LCG by `seed`, no `Math.random`; each tile keeps its ORIGINAL index so duplicate words stay individually addressable), and shown as a bank below a sentence tray — cued by the native meaning (`completedNativeText ?? nativeText`, `LifeScreen-WordOrder-Cue`). Tap a bank word → it appends to the tray (`LifeScreen-WordOrder-Placed-<i>`); tap a tray word → it returns to the bank; Submit (enabled once EVERY word is placed) sends the assembled string to the engine's `submitOrder`. **Grading** (`gradeOrder` in `nodeEngineExercises`, extracted with `gradeTyped` for the LOC cap): `isOrderCorrect(built, completedFillBlank(...))` — normalise (trim, collapse whitespace, lowercase; punctuation + accents load-bearing) then compare; routes through the SAME `resolveGraded` feedback + prefetch as a chip pick (synthetic `__ordered__` picked choice). It's production practice — assemble the whole line, not fill one gap. **Variant selection** (`chooseFillBlankVariant`, LIFE-EX-9): a 3-slot rotation over the fill-blank forms — `seed % 3 === 2` → listen (level ≥ 2), `seed % 3 === 1` → order (level ≥ `WORD_ORDER_MIN_LEVEL` = 3), else type-to-fill (level ≥ 4) else chips — so the follow-up questions meet every form. Comprehension is never word-order. |
| **LIFE-EX-VOCAB-1** | When a chip's `practiceText` matches a phrase the player has saved as a flashcard (case-insensitive trimmed exact match against `flashcard.phrase`), the chip renders with a vocab-card affordance: a `BookOpen` icon + a gradient ring distinguish it from a plain chip. **Tap behaviour swaps**: a single tap opens a card-preview popover (showing phrase + native translation + saved-context if any) — it does NOT fire the chip. The popover exposes a "Use this answer" button that fires the chip's `onPick` (or `onFreeText` for the `__free__` chip — but that chip never matches anyway). Closing the popover (backdrop tap or close button) does not fire the choice. Match is computed client-side from the user's `users/{uid}/flashcards/{cardId}` collection — already loaded by `useFlashcards`. **Graded turns suppress this affordance**: on a `question-ready` turn the chips are answer options (LIFE-LANG-1), so `flashcardLookup` is NOT passed to their `ActionBar` — a chip whose text matches a saved card still taps to answer and never opens the popover. This keeps a graded chip a single-tap answer even when its text happens to match a saved card. (Historically this also mattered for card-backed questions; those were removed with flashcard threading, §10.47.) |

---

## 10.45 Two-stage graded-question generation — `LIFE-EX-PLAN-*` / `LIFE-EX-GEN-*`

A single overloaded prompt that did narrative + chip authoring + pedagogy produced systematically broken graded turns (CYOA-shaped chips, recycled targets, answer-leaking natives). Adopting the pattern from the standalone practice-mode generator: SPLIT the work. `lifeStep` decides WHAT to test; a focused second call AUTHORS the question.

> **Phase F note (`LIFE-NODE-*`):** the planner's output travels as a `question` NODE (`gradedPlan` on `ZNodePreview` — the node shape; previews themselves were removed 2026-07, `LIFE-NODE-2`), and the generator fires when the node is SHOWN (phase `question-authoring`), not merged into a `StepResponse` before the response returns. `LIFE-EX-PLAN-1/2/3` (plan shape + curator rules) and all of `LIFE-EX-GEN-*` are unchanged; only `LIFE-EX-PLAN-4`'s "the orchestrator immediately fires the generator, merges its result, and presents the combined response" mechanics are superseded by `LIFE-NODE-1`.

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-EX-PLAN-1** | On a graded turn (`graded: true`), the `lifeStep` response carries a `gradedPlan` object INSTEAD OF the `practicePrompt` + chip set. The plan tells the question generator everything it needs to author the chips. CYOA turns (`graded: false`) still author chips inline in `lifeStep` — the split only applies to graded. |
| **LIFE-EX-PLAN-2** | The `gradedPlan` shape names WHAT to test as plain prose, not structured fields. For `fill-blank`: `{ kind: 'fill-blank', testTarget: string, sentenceContext: string, rationale? }` — `testTarget` is the phrase or word being tested ("quand même", "voudrais"); `sentenceContext` is the full sentence where it appears in context ("ils vont tous abattre les humains quand même"). For `comprehension`: `{ kind: 'comprehension', npcUtterance: string, speakerName?, npcId?, rationale? }` — the verbatim NPC line + speaker labels. The lifeStep commits to WHAT to test and the CONTEXT; the generator (LIFE-EX-GEN-*) picks distractors, places languages, and authors the chip set. **The `testTarget` obeys DIFFICULTY (LIFE-CTX-3 / LIFE-LANG-9) — it IS the graded thing, so the step SI gates its selection on the live `${difficulty}`: at level 1-2 the target MUST be a high-frequency concrete word or a basic PRESENT-tense form, NEVER a conditional / subjunctive / past-tense conjugation (those are level 5-7). "voudrais" (conditional) is a level-5-7 example, not a default — the step SI's two prior worked examples (`step.ts` QUESTION PLANS + OUTPUT SCHEMA) that modeled it at every level were de-seeded (they seeded the "difficulty 1 still asks tense conjugations" bug — a level-1 target is now "veux" / a concrete noun).** No structured `disambiguation` / `targetForm` / `targetLemma` / `completedLine` fields — those forced premature commitments. |
| **LIFE-EX-PLAN-3** | The `gradedPlan.testTarget` (for fill-blank) MUST NOT appear in `<recentlyPracticed>`. The lifeStep SI re-states this as a HARD CONSTRAINT: pick a phrase / verb / card that has NOT been tested this chapter. If nothing fresh fits the moment, emit CYOA (graded:false) instead of recycling. The lifeStep is the CURATOR — it knows the story, the player's vocab, and the recently-tested list. Generator obeys rules; planner curates. |
| **LIFE-EX-PLAN-4** | `lifeStep` no longer emits `practicePrompt` or chip `correctness` fields on graded turns. The graded-turn chip set on the lifeStep response is empty; the orchestrator immediately fires the generator (LIFE-EX-GEN-*), merges its result, and presents the combined response to the UI. From the UI's perspective the API is unchanged — `StepResponse.practicePrompt` and `StepResponse.choices` are populated by the time it sees the response. |
| **LIFE-EX-CADENCE-1** | **The practice cadence applies PRESSURE, not position — and a demoted question SPENDS its slot.** Two defects, found 2026-07-30 by counting node kinds across a 25-turn run. **(a) The directive forced the moment.** `<practiceCadence>` fired at `cyoaStreak >= 2` and said *"Make THIS node a question"* — an imperative tied to a counter, landing the graded moment on whatever node happened to be Nth whether or not anything was being said there. It also contradicted the SI's own baseline rule ("put one WHEREVER it naturally fits"), and its only escape hatch was scoped to target EXHAUSTION, not narrative fit — so with a fresh target available the model was compelled to bolt a question onto a scene change or a mid-action beat, which reads as a pop quiz and teaches less than a question the story earned. It is now two tiers: **DUE** (`CADENCE_DUE` = 2) tells the model a graded moment is due and to place it at the next node that genuinely fits, explicitly permitting deferral; **OVERDUE** (`CADENCE_OVERDUE` = 4) closes the window and tells it to use the first available speech moment, or to steer the scene into one for the next node. The DUE threshold is unchanged at 2, so this splits an existing trigger into two tiers rather than moving it. **(b) A demoted question re-forced itself.** `cyoaStreak` resets on `correction` — an ANSWERED question — but the `LIFE-NODE-8` graceful demote wrote no event at all, only a `log.warn`. So a question whose generator rejected the plan left the streak elevated, the directive re-fired on the very next node, and the demote could repeat: **three calls per round** (the question's `life_step`, the failed `life_graded_question`, and the replacement options fetch) and a visible run of forced questions. A `question_demoted` event (`src/types/lifeEvents.ts`) is now written locally and to Firestore, and `cyoaStreak` treats it as a spent slot. It is NEVER rendered into the prompt (`LIFE-CTX-16`'s rule): telling the model a question was planned here and rejected invites it to explain or retry the failure in-narrative. **Measured, 25-turn runs:** question rate **36% → 29%** (the SI's stated target is "roughly 1 node in 3"), longest consecutive-question run **3 → 1**, and spacing changed from rigid `branch, question, branch, question` alternation to a variable 2-3 branches between questions — the model visibly choosing its moments. Fewer questions also means proportionally fewer `life_graded_question` calls (13 → 10 per 25 turns). **Note the measurement trap:** a question turn produces TWO nodes (the question, then the post-feedback node), so a census that counts one node per turn overstates the question rate by ~50% — an earlier pass reported 48% from exactly that error. |
| **LIFE-EX-12** | **Listen-and-answer: COMPREHENSION gets a render variant too, and listening starts at level 1.** Owner, 2026-08-06: *"the practice questions seem to also always be comprehension or fill the blank … make sure even as a beginner I'm exposed to listening and fill exercises as I think those are slightly easier."* **Why the variety was invisible where it mattered.** `LIFE-EX-8/9/11` gave the fill-blank four presentations (chips · type · listen · order) and comprehension exactly one — `exerciseVariant.ts` said so outright: *"Comprehension is never a listen-and-fill … a listening COMPREHENSION would be a separate exercise."* Flagged, never built. **MEASURED over 829 graded questions in 61 archived runs: the direction split is 49.6% inbound / 50.4% outbound**, so a player meets comprehension on roughly half of all questions — and every one of those looked identical. Worse in real play than in the benchmark: an outbound fill-blank is gated on the player PICKING a `say` option carrying `gradeTarget` (`LIFE-SPEECH-3`), so a scene the player takes physically — `reach for the letter`, `duck behind the fountain`, `scan the arcades` — can only ever produce comprehension. The benchmark picks evenly across option types and therefore never reproduced the owner's *"they're always comprehension"*. **`chooseComprehensionVariant(difficulty, seed)`** rotates `read` / `listen` on the same deterministic seed the fill-blank rotation uses (no `Math.random`, no extra gen — the authored question is untouched, only its presentation changes). On `listen`, the NPC's `npcUtterance` is SPOKEN (TTS `autoPlay`) and **withheld in print until the player answers** — hearing it is the exercise, and showing the transcript would make it the read variant with a speaker attached. The post-answer bubble and `utteranceNativeText` reveal are unchanged (`LIFE-LANG-12`). **`LISTEN_FILL_MIN_LEVEL` drops 2 → 1.** The gate was a guess that listening needs footing; the owner reports the opposite — that listening and fill are the EASIER forms for them — and a level-1 player under the old gate met exactly one presentation, chips, for the entire wurld. |
| **LIFE-EX-14** | **The listening LADDER: fill-only at 1-2, whole sentences from 3, and more native as the level climbs.** Owner, 2026-08-06: *"At levels 1-2 listen should only be listen and fill. Not listen for the whole sentence. Level 3 should be listen to whole sentence but not too hard. 4-7 should be harder and more and more native style."* **This AMENDS `LIFE-EX-12`**, which is the honest way to record it: that invariant dropped `LISTEN_FILL_MIN_LEVEL` to 1 AND made comprehension-listening available at every level, on the reasoning that *"listening is available at every level"* and that a level-1 player otherwise met one presentation for the whole wurld. The first half was right and stands — **a beginner still meets listening from level 1**. The second half over-shot: listen-and-FILL gives a beginner a blank to aim at and the rest of the sentence in print, whereas whole-sentence comprehension-listening gives them nothing but the audio, and those are not the same exercise wearing different clothes. `COMPREHENSION_LISTEN_MIN_LEVEL` = 3, so `chooseComprehensionVariant` returns `read` below it — the parameter it has accepted and pointedly ignored since it was written (*"a future level gate goes here and nowhere else"*) finally does something. **Levels 1-2 therefore keep 3 presentations** (fill-chips, fill-listen, comprehension-read) rather than 4, and every one of them still includes audio. The CONTENT half rides on the graded generator's existing difficulty band: level 3 is a whole sentence pitched plainly, and 4-7 climb toward how a native would actually say it — contractions, elision, idiom — which is a property of the LINE, not of the playback (there is no rate parameter; `TTS-STYLE-3`). |
| **LIFE-EX-13** | **The card-match review moves to the END OF THE SCENE, and it is built from what the player GOT WRONG first.** Owner, 2026-08-06: *"save card matching for the last question in a scene, make it intercept the scene summary so that it's able to give top vocabulary matches across the scene, prioritising questions I got wrong, and then other key vocabulary that appeared in the narration."* **This supersedes `LIFE-EX-10`'s cadence and source, not its mechanics** — the grid, the shuffle, the match/lock behaviour and the free client-built construction are all unchanged. What changes is WHEN and FROM WHAT. **When:** once per scene, on the resolve, ahead of the `SceneCompleteScreen` — so the last thing a scene asks is "did any of this stick?", which is the question a summary screen is about to answer anyway. `REVIEW_EVERY`(4)-questions mid-scene firing is retired: it interrupted a scene at an arbitrary point and drew on an arbitrary window. **From what:** `buildSceneReviewPairs` walks back to the scene's own `scene_started` marker (the same boundary `buildSceneLog` uses) and fills the grid in THREE TIERS — (1) **MISSED**: `correction` events with `correct: false`, paired from `correctAnswer` + `correctAnswerNative`; (2) **PRACTISED**: corrections the player got right; (3) **NARRATION**: `wordTranslations` off the scene's beats, most-frequent first, which is the "other key vocabulary that appeared in the narration" half. Deduped by practice phrase across all three, capped at `CARD_MATCH_MAX_PAIRS`(5). **Tier 1 is the point.** A review that draws uniformly from recent vocab spends most of its slots on words the player already knew; sorting the misses to the front makes the one guaranteed-useful thing also the first thing. **Frequency, not recency, orders tier 3:** a word the scene used four times is what the scene was ABOUT, while the newest word is an accident of where the scene stopped. |
| **LIFE-EX-13.1** | **The scene review must SURVIVE the options release — it was being built and trampled on the same arrival.** `LIFE-EX-13` was verified twice at the unit level (`buildSceneReviewPairs` builds the right pairs; `tryEnterSceneReview` transitions to `card-match`) and was broken end to end the whole time. `handleSignals` ends a resolving scene and opens the grid; the turn's options then release on the beat tail, and BOTH release sites set `phase: 'options-ready'` unconditionally. `LifeScreenBottom` renders the grid on `phase === 'card-match'`, so the grid was constructed — pairs and all — lived for one tick, and **the player never saw it**. Nothing caught it because each half was tested against its own contract and nothing tested the two ORDERINGS together; the engine test that finally did was written to answer *"does card matching intercept ALL scene ends?"* and printed `PHASE= options-ready cardMatch= {pairs:[…3 pairs…]}` — the mechanism firing and being discarded, in one line. Both sites now route through `keepPhase()`, which returns no `phase` key at all while a review is open, so a third release site cannot reintroduce it. The options are stored either way; `completeCardMatch` moves to `options-ready` when the grid is solved and finds them waiting. **The rest of the flow was already right and is now pinned by test:** the review is attempted on EVERY resolve (one `onSceneResolved` call site, with `tryEnterSceneReview` immediately after it, so no scene-end path bypasses it); `runSceneClose` is already in flight BEFORE the grid opens, so the scene's record loads in the background while the player matches; and a scene with fewer than `CARD_MATCH_MIN_PAIRS`(3) pairs declines the review and proceeds straight to its summary rather than stranding it. |
| **LIFE-GRID-6** | **The DANGLED GOAL — the story may not keep offering what it will not give — and `gradeOptionSet` is finally CALLED.** Owner, 2026-08-06: *"the story kept pushing in the direction of taking the letter, but every time I tried the NPC denied it. What was the point in leading me down these roads only to shut my options down?"* **MEASURED across 61 archived runs / 237 scenes / 2,016 picks: 12.6% of picks are a goal RE-OFFERED after the player already took it, 3.8% are dangled two or more further times, worst case SEVEN offers of one goal in a single scene.** So this is an engine behaviour with a number, not a bad roll. **The rule already existed and was never enforced.** `<recentActionsRule>` has said *"Do NOT offer them again … a different verb on the same target is still a repeat"* since LIFE-GRID-4, and `gradeOptionSet` — the "REJECT as a backstop" half this module's own header describes — **had no caller anywhere in `src`**. Written, unit-tested, never wired. A rule the model can satisfy by doing nothing is not a mechanism; a backstop nothing invokes is not a backstop. **Two halves now.** (a) `<recentActions>` gains the COUNT (`do:letter ×3`) and, when any shape repeats, a `<dangledGoal>` block that forces a CHOICE: let it happen this turn, or have someone close it off for good and make the options about what follows — *"a refusal that costs nothing and changes nothing is not a story beat, it is a wall."* A flat list could never distinguish one ask from three refusals. (b) `gradeOptionSet` is called from the retry ladder with `takenShapeCounts(events)` and REJECTS a grid offering a shape the player has already taken `DANGLE_TAKES`(2) times. **The threshold was measured, not guessed,** against this module's own ceiling (*"above ~15% the rule is wrong, not the model"*): rejecting ANY re-offer of a taken shape fires on **16.5%** of grids — over the line; rejecting at two-takes fires on **2.2%**. The counts are UNWINDOWED, unlike the 8-row prompt block, because a goal dangled across a long scene is precisely the case the window drops. On the last attempt the grid ships anyway (`LIFE-NODE-8` — never strand the player behind a validator) and the miss is logged as a warning. **Wiring it surfaced a second copy of itself.** `gradeOptionSet`'s other two rules — all-one-type and nothing-active — had meanwhile been re-implemented, more strictly, in `optionSpreadIssues` (`stepSchema.ts`), which DOES run, at the request-shape stage. Measured over the same 1,980 grids, `gradeOptionSet`'s versions fire on **0.0%** of turns: not because grids are clean, but because the live path already rejected those upstream. Two functions answering *"is this grid OK?"* is how a guard silently stops being the guard, so they are now one each — `optionSpreadIssues` owns the SPREAD (needs only the options), `gradeOptionSet` owns what needs the EVENT LOG. **Known gap, deliberately not papered over:** the third old rule, *same shape as the previous turn*, measured **2.3%** — a real defect — and is DELETED rather than kept behind a permanently-null argument, because the app cannot enforce it: only the player's PICK is persisted, never the set they were offered. Reinstating it means recording the offered shapes on the `decision` event payload; until then the prevention half (`<recentActions>`) is all there is for it. Keeping a rule that cannot fire would have recreated the exact defect this invariant exists to fix. **AMENDED SAME DAY — A GOAL IS A THING, NOT A VERB.** The first implementation shipped with the rule measured against one key and enforced against another, and a real playthrough caught it within the hour. The 2.2% measurement keyed a targetless option by its LABEL (near-unique, so targetless picks essentially never collided); the code used `shapeKey`, which collapses a targetless option to its bare verb. Two targetless `ask` picks — nothing at all in a conversation game — and every later grid containing any targetless `ask` was rejected as a dangled goal. Re-measured over all **2,026 archived grids**: the shipped bare-verb key fires on **75.7%** of grids, five times this module's own ~15% ceiling; keyed to targeted options only it is **7.1%** at two takes (2.5% at three, 19.5% at one). At 75.7% it was not a backstop but a tax — three quarters of turns paying a second generation to be told *"option 'ask' is a goal the player has already taken 8 times"*, which is not a sentence about anything. `goalKey` now returns `null` for an option that names no entity, and a null key is never counted (`takenShapeCounts` skips it), never checked (`gradeOptionSet` skips it), and never named in `<dangledGoal>`. `shapeKey` survives as the DISPLAY key for `<recentActions>`, where a bare verb is fine and informative. **The lesson is not "be careful":** the unit test written alongside the bug asserted that the count map keys a targetless pick *the same way `shapeKey` displays it* — so the guard and its test agreed with each other and both were wrong. What caught it was playing the game and counting. A rule chosen by measurement has to be RE-measured against the key it was actually built with. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-36** | **A scene closes ONCE, and only when it resolves — the 5-turn tick may not close a running scene.** PRODUCTION, 2026-08-06: FOUR scene closes inside six minutes, with summaries that contradicted each other. `runSceneClose` was gated on `plan.shouldReplan || resolvingNow`, and `shouldReplan` is a **5-turn tick** (plus the two pivot signals) — so a 12-turn scene closed at turn 5, at turn 10, and again on resolve, each call writing a *"what happened in this scene"* history line from a partial view of a scene that had not finished. **The tick is a leftover from `sceneReplan`**, which refreshed a RUNNING scene's plan; `LIFE-ARCH-4` replaced replan with close, and close writes STATE — one history line, clock deltas, cast deltas, new canon — about a scene that is over. It has nothing to say about one still in progress. **It also broke a stated contract with a second-order cost.** `LIFE-ARCH-5`: *"`sagaState.history` is an array of one-line entries appended at each `sceneClose`"* — and `canonDormancy()` derives dormancy from that history assuming ONE LINE PER CLOSED SCENE (*"the index of the newest line naming an entity IS its dormancy"*). Extra lines per scene make every entity read as more recent than it is, in proportion to how long the scene ran — which is exactly the design's own watch-list signal 4, *"always the most recent entity → dormancy not working"*. So the visible symptom (contradictory summaries) and the invisible one (reuse quietly starved) had one cause. The gate is now `resolvingNow` alone. The tick keeps its bookkeeping — `plan.patch` still resets `turnsSinceReplan`, a hard pivot still bumps the nonce — it simply no longer decides that a scene has ended. `LIFE-SCENE-32` fixed the other half of this gate (a resolving scene must close even when the tick has not come round) and left the tick in place; this is the same rule finished. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-10** | **The arc self-heal yields to the *Continue your wurld* CTA — one chapter, one generation.** PRODUCTION, 2026-08-06: chapter 2 was generated TWICE. `archiveActiveArc` sets `currentArc: null` on chapter close by design, which makes `LIFE-WORLD-ARC-9`'s precondition (*no arc, `completedArcCount > 0`*) true the instant the chapter-complete screen appears — while `continueToNextChapter` is about to generate the SAME chapter number from the CTA the player is looking at. Two billed generations for one chapter, last write wins, and **the chapter the player was shown is not necessarily the one that got stored**. This is the existing `completedArcCount <= 0` guard with a different owner: that one keeps chapter 1 for `LifeStartScreen` (*"firing here would race LifeStartScreen and write a second one"*), this one keeps chapter N+1 for the CTA. The distinguishing question is the same — WHOSE JOB IS IT — and it is answered by **whether the arc was missing on arrival or vanished while we were watching**. Missing on the first snapshot ⇒ the world was stranded before this session and nothing else is coming ⇒ heal. Present and then gone ⇒ a chapter just completed here ⇒ the CTA owns what follows. Latched per MOUNT, so *Pause for now* → re-enter still heals a genuine strand, which is the case `LIFE-WORLD-ARC-9` exists for. |
| **LIFE-BACKDROP-27** | **The location label is normalised in CODE, because two prompt rules were not enough.** `step.ts` says *"locationLabel is … A PLACE ONLY — no time of day"*; `playerKnowledge.ts` says *"do NOT append the hour to it ('Ton appartement · matin')"*; a production label still arrived as `Ton appartement · nuit` on 2026-08-06. Writing a THIRD sentence saying the same thing is the move that has already failed twice. **It costs more than a cosmetic slip: the label IS the backdrop cache key** (`LIFE-BACKDROP-2`), so a time baked into it regenerates the same place once per hour of story time — a fresh SVG, a fresh bill — and then the ribbon contradicts the light on screen the moment the clock moves. `normaliseLocationLabel` runs in a `z.preprocess` on `ZBackdropIntent`, the ONE seam every consumer passes through (the world patch, the `backdrop_visited` event, the cache key, the ribbon, `LIFE-BACKDROP-26`'s native twin, and the graded prompt's `<location>` block) — same technique and same reasoning as the `actionType` preprocess beside it: fix the value where it costs nothing rather than at the seam where it costs a turn. **Deliberately narrow.** It cuts everything from the first `·` (the documented failure shape; MEASURED over 145 distinct archived labels, zero legitimate uses) and a terminal full stop (a name does not end in one; 2.8% of archived labels were descriptions rather than names, e.g. *"Pedro's Hearth, a bakery that stays warm even in the dead of winter."*). It does NOT cut at a comma even though that is where those descriptions begin, because a comma tail is often a real sub-location (*"Pedro's Hearth, in the cellar"*) — a DIFFERENT backdrop that must keep its own cache key, and merging those would be worse than the bug being fixed. It never returns empty from a non-empty input: a label that is only a time would cut to `''`, collapsing every timeless place onto one cache entry. |
| **LIFE-EX-GEN-LOG** | **A rejection is logged under the guard that actually fired.** Three independent guards share one branch in `runGradedQuestionGenerate` — `leakViolations` (`LIFE-EX-GEN-3`), `directionViolations` (`LIFE-EX-DIR-3`) and `vocabViolations` (`LIFE-EX-VOCAB-4`) — and every one of them was reported to the overlay as `graded-question rejected: answer-leak`. Seen 2026-08-06 in the owner's production log: the real fault was a missing `lineVocab` (the model simply omitted the annotations) and it read as the model having spoiled the answer. That is the one mislabel that sends the reader to the wrong prompt to fix the wrong rule. The summary now names the families that fired (`answer-leak` · `direction` · `line-vocab`, joined when more than one), and the `question_demoted` reason keeps carrying the full violation text as before. |
