INTENT's procedure defines REDIRECTED as a pick that changed *WHERE the scene went, WHO was in it, WHAT was at stake, or what the player now knows* — and then band 2 capped *"an incidental redirect (a different room, the same conversation)"* at the DEFAULT band. A change of room satisfies "changed WHERE" as written, so the scheme scored its own definition down a band, and every genuine redirect landed at 2. Band 2 now states the negative condition explicitly (NO pick met the test) and band 3 states that a change of place or of who the player is with COUNTS; the procedure carries the bread worked example so the ACKNOWLEDGED/REDIRECTED line is drawn on a case rather than an adjective. **Measured on the four stored transcripts, rejudged with no new runs**: INTENT 1.281 → 1.719 (**+0.438** against a 0.25 noise floor, `LIFE-BENCH-1`), moving in all four. Of the other 19 judged dimensions, 16-18 per transcript held EXACTLY and none moved above its own floor — including OPT-FREEDOM (+0.063), which is the control: it is genuinely low and the rejudge did not inflate it. The standing lesson: before reporting a dimension as a product failure, re-read the band that produced the number. Scores below what the surface deserves are as likely to be a scheme defect as a product one, and a rejudge costs cents against a run's dollars. |
| **LIFE-BENCH-3** | **`PLAY_SEEDS=restaurant,heist` smoke-tests seeds without editing the benchmark.** Verifying six new seeds meant hand-editing `WORLDS` in `run.play.ts`, running, and remembering to revert — and the revert is the part that goes wrong. Left in, it silently replaces the benchmark's 8/8/2/2 mix with whatever the smoke test needed, so every composite measured afterwards sits on a different baseline and **nothing says so**. That is worse than the inconvenience it saves and it hides for weeks. One SHORT world per named seed; name, language and level rotate so a multi-seed run varies the learner instead of testing one language four times. Mode comes from `WORLD_MODES` — the same table the chip grid renders from — so a seed cannot be smoke-tested in a register the UI would never offer it in, and an unknown key **THROWS at load** rather than falling through to the defaults (a typo that quietly ran the full benchmark would be read as a smoke-test result). Lives in `playSeeds.ts`, not in `run.play.ts`, because `run.play.ts` IS a vitest file: importing it to test it would run the benchmark, so anything asserted there would have to be a COPY of the logic — a test that passes forever and catches nothing. The first version of this test was exactly that copy and was thrown away. |
| **LIFE-BENCH-4** | **The anomaly sweep — `npm run test:playthrough:sweep -- <stamp>` — reads one run's artefacts and reports what the rubric does not.** The judge scores the STORY. Nothing scored the ENGINE, and the engine's real costs live in the CALL LOG: the retry column, the latency spread, the responses that failed to parse. Written 2026-08-05; it found four defects in one sitting that no existing signal would ever have shown (`LIFE-EX-PLAN-6`, `LIFE-EX-PLAN-7`, `LIFE-NODE-16e`, `LIFE-NODE-19`). Every check reports a NUMBER first and a judgement second, and **a check that fires zero is as informative as one that fires** — "0 player lines echoed by an NPC" is the standing evidence that `LIFE-SPEECH-6` works, so the quiet checks are not noise to be deleted. **It cannot prove a defect on its own — every number is a lead.** Two of its own checks were WRONG on first use and reported real-looking problems that did not exist: orphan option targets read 18.5% because the check compared `targetId` against the CAST only (every hit was a `plac-` slug, and an option may legitimately target a place — now 0), and "scenePace is ignored" came from substring-matching the word `overdue` anywhere in the prompt (parsing the actual tag showed compliance climbing 2% → 6% → 57% by stage). Both were caught by reading the underlying rows. Do that before reporting anything from here as a bug. |
| **LIFE-BENCH-5** | **`PLAY_FREETEXT=3` makes the harness PUSH the story instead of only picking from its menu.** Two reasons. (a) The route had **never been run**: `freeText` was hard-coded `null` on every turn since the harness was written, so "Write your own" — a first-class user-facing input — had zero coverage, the same gap the code already records for options (*"because the harness never had options, it had never once run"*). (b) It is the only way to stress the SCENE BOUNDARY: options are authored by the same call that authors the scene, so they mostly point where the scene already intends to go, while a player writing *"I leave and go somewhere else entirely"* pushes AGAINST the outline — which is what forces a replan or a resolve, and therefore what exercises `LIFE-SCENE-33`. The push lines are deliberately blunt; this is a stress test, not a model of typical play, and its numbers must be read that way. On a push turn `choice` goes null — sending a pick AND free text would describe a turn that cannot happen. Never fires on turn 0. |
| **LIFE-BENCH-6** | **The transcript records what the player was SHOWN, chip by chip — the whole `practicePrompt` object and every chip with its verdict.** It used to record `prompt: JSON.stringify(q.practicePrompt).slice(0, 400)` and `choices: string[]`, which loses three things that turn out to be exactly what the questions get asked about: WHICH chip was correct, WHY each wrong one is wrong (`whyWrong` — a required field the generator is validated on, per `LIFE-EX-9`), and any prompt field past the 400th character (`completedNativeText` and `disambiguation` both sort late in `JSON.stringify` order, and `completedNativeText` is the native anchor `LIFE-EX-8.2` turns on). The `LIFE-EX-8.2` analysis therefore had to be reconstructed from the raw call log — the transcript, which exists so that reconstruction is unnecessary, could not answer *"was a native target shown?"* about its own run. **Same class as `LIFE-BENCH-5`'s `freeText`: a field the harness holds and does not write down.** `graded.prompt` becomes the parsed object (`PracticePrompt`, not a truncated string) and `graded.choices` becomes `{text, correctness, whyWrong}[]`. Kept as their post-`runGradedQuestionGenerate` values — after `applyGradedDirection` and `shuffleChoices` — because what is being recorded is what a player would have seen, not what the model first emitted. |

### Design decisions — the two-axis beat entrance (`LIFE-NODE-12`)

**The brief was "chat already does this, reuse it". Chat does not do this — not any more.** The chat bubble's horizontal component was DELETED on 2026-07-27 (commit `a2702c3`, TYPING-5): an incoming bubble used to enter `slide-in-from-bottom-1 slide-in-from-left-1` over a separate 250ms `THEIRS_ENTER_MS`, and unifying the two senders' entrances removed the shorter duration and the leftward slide together. `MSG-BUBBLE-8.1` ([message-pipeline.md](./message-pipeline.md)) now specifies ONE entrance for both senders, vertical only — `--tw-enter-translate-y: 100%` with X left at the plugin default of 0. **What almost certainly made it look implemented is a stale comment** above the class list in `MessageBubble.tsx`, which still says *"theirs slides up-and-from-the-left so it reads as 'incoming from the conversation side'"* about motion that no longer exists.

So there was no horizontal constant to import. What IS reused is everything that still exists:

- **the direction semantics** — a bubble enters from the side it is aligned to, which is exactly what chat's deleted `slide-in-from-left-1` meant for a left-aligned incoming bubble, generalised to the right-aligned case the sim needs;
- **the delivery mechanism** — an inline CSS custom property driven from a named TS constant, the same shape by which `MINE_ENTER_TRAVEL` reaches `--tw-enter-translate-y`, with the tests asserting the resolved property rather than the class name (`MSG-BUBBLE-8.1`).

**What was deliberately NOT done: putting the travel in `bubbleMotion.ts` for both surfaces to import.** Two reasons. (1) The two entrances are not the same animation and never were — chat's bubble travels a FULL bubble height (`100%`) into a grid track opening underneath it (`MSG-BUBBLE-8.2`), while a life beat translates a flat 20px with no gap-grow at all; a single shared "travel" constant would be one name over two unrelated distances, in different units, and the first person to tune one would silently move the other. (2) A constant that only ONE surface uses is precisely the `THEIRS_ENTER_MS` shape TYPING-5 deleted rather than deprecated — chat applies no X travel today, so exporting one from `bubbleMotion.ts` would be a "shared home" with a single consumer.

**The trigger for revisiting:** if chat re-adopts a horizontal entrance, the magnitude AND the mine/theirs sign belong in `bubbleMotion.ts` at that point, and `beatEnterOffsetX` should import them instead of owning the `20`. That is a two-line change on this side. It is deliberately not made pre-emptively.

**What the tests do NOT prove.** jsdom does no layout and runs no animations, so nothing in the unit tier observes the bubble actually moving. What is asserted is the full chain that decides the movement: the per-speaker sign on the rendered DOM, the shared magnitude, the keyframe's two-axis `from` reading that property, the duration still equalling `GLIDE_ENTER_MS` (which `LIFE-NODE-11`'s stagger floor depends on), and the reduced-motion rule still dropping transforms entirely. The motion itself remains a screenshot-review concern.

**Turn phases (client state machine, `useNodeEngine`):** `live-loading` (thinking bar — now the state EVERY pick passes through, `LIFE-NODE-2`) · `options-loading` (node beats visible, 3 skeleton chips — reached only via the graded-demote path now that live results arrive with their options) · `options-ready` (chips) · `question-authoring` (shimmer header — held a `MIN_AUTHORING_MS` cosmetic floor so it never flashes, `LIFE-NODE-10`; ~1.5s typical real-generator latency) · `question-ready` (prompt card + graded chips) · `feedback` (overlay; holds the prefetched post-feedback live turn) · `options-failed` (note + Continue chip).

### Design decisions (Phase F)

- **Why previews were REMOVED (2026-07, supersedes the two entries below).** The original rationale — quoted verbatim below because it is a good example of a defensible argument resting on one unmeasured premise — was: *"a second 'prefetch next node' call would double per-turn calls and re-send the full context. Bundling previews into the same structured output costs +450–650 output tokens/turn (~2× output, output is the cheap side at flash-lite pricing) and zero extra input/calls. Revisit if the Telemetry tab shows output cost dominating."*

  It was revisited, with measurement, and three of its premises turned out to be wrong:

  1. **Previews never saved a call.** The comparison was against a hypothetical *second prefetch call*, but the real alternative was one live call returning node + options — the same single call an unpreviewed pick already made. Previewed and unpreviewed picks were always 1:1 on calls. The "doubles per-turn calls" risk never existed.
  2. **Output is not the cheap side.** At the measured working point output was ~49% of `life_step` spend. Input looks larger in tokens (15.8k vs 0.8k) but ~77% of it is served from the prefix cache at a tenth of the rate, while every output token bills at 6× the input rate. Two of every three previews were thrown away unread.
  3. **The revisit trigger fired and nobody was watching.** "Revisit if the Telemetry tab shows output cost dominating" — the Telemetry tab was excluding thinking tokens from output (`LIFE-COST-2`), so the signal this decision was waiting on was under-reported by construction on every thinking call.

  What previews genuinely bought was **latency**: a pick rendered instantly instead of after ~2-3s. That cost is now paid visibly in `live-loading` rather than invisibly on every turn's bill, and `saysLine` (`LIFE-SPEECH-1`) fills the wait on speech picks. *Revisit if:* play testing shows the wait breaks flow badly enough to be worth ~15% of `life_step` — in which case previews on the 2 most-likely options (not all 3) is the cheaper middle ground, and the measurement to beat is in `docs/archive/model-migration-2026-07.md` §28.3.4.

- ~~**Why the cache is memory-only**~~ / ~~**Why the nonce only bumps on free-text + hardPivot**~~ — both retired with the preview cache. The nonce survives (`LIFE-NODE-6`) but now guards only in-flight results, and its bump conditions are unchanged for that narrower job.
- **Why the options call re-reads the events log as node context:** the picked node's beats + the pick decision are committed at pick time (and mirrored in-memory), so `<turn kind="options">` needs no bespoke payload — the "current node" is simply the newest events, which keeps the prompt assembly identical to every other turn (cache-friendly, one code path).

## 10.47 Flashcard weaving + SRS credit — `LIFE-EX-SRS-*` — **REMOVED 2026-07**

**This whole layer was removed.** The wurld no longer threads the player's saved
deck into the story, and story graded answers no longer credit SM-2. Rationale
(owner directive): the `<flashcards>` deck block cost ~1-2k input tokens on
every single turn, and forcing graded targets toward whatever card was due
(a) made turns feel contrived around a learning point rather than the scene,
and (b) fought the difficulty setting (a due card can be any level). The loop
is now cleaner and one-directional: the **story CAPTURES vocabulary**
(save-as-flashcard from any bubble, `LIFE-BUBBLE-7` — unchanged) and the
**dedicated practice session** (an existing feature) is where those cards are
reviewed and SM-2 is credited. Graded targets in the story are chosen purely
for scene fit + difficulty (`LIFE-EX-PLAN-2`).

Removed: the `<flashcards>` prompt block + `world.deckSnapshot` + `deckSnapshot.ts`
+ `useChapterDeck`; `gradedPlan.flashcardId`; the story-side `creditSrsAttempt`
(`srsCredit.ts`) and the `flashcardId` thread through `FeedbackState` /
`PracticeOutcome` / the `correction` payload. Kept: `flashcard_added` events
(they record genuine player captures and still appear as memories in
`<recentEvents>`), and `<recentlyPracticed>` (anti-repeat — never was
flashcard-specific). The `correction` event still records misses for the
future wurld-mistakes-practice backlog (§19).

The original invariants `LIFE-EX-SRS-1..4` are struck through below for history.

The player's saved deck is the seed vocabulary the wurld weaves into its questions (Product requirements: "Vocabulary woven into narrative"). The planner LLM is the curator — it sees the deck, the story, and the recently-tested list in one context and picks the card; no extra selector call. When a card-backed question resolves, the SAME SM-2 write path practice mode uses credits the review, so a wurld session advances the deck exactly like a practice session. **[SUPERSEDED — see the removal note above.]**

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| ~~**LIFE-EX-SRS-1**~~ (REMOVED) | Every `life_step` prompt carries a `<flashcards>` block at LIFE-CTX-1 position (4.2) — after `<backdropIndex>`, before `<scene>` — rendered from `world.deckSnapshot`: one `<card id="{cardId}">{practice phrase}</card>` row per card, id + practice phrase ONLY (no translations — the model needs the surface form; glosses would bloat the stable prefix and leak nothing useful to a planner). Order inside the block is the snapshot's stored order: due cards first (`nextReviewAt <= now`; a card with NO `nextReviewAt` counts as due), then not-yet-due, each group sorted by `cardPriorityScore` descending (ties keep the deck's `createdAt desc` query order — the sort is stable). Capped at 100 cards (`DECK_SNAPSHOT_CAP`). No snapshot / empty snapshot renders the self-closing `<flashcards/>` so the section count is stable. |
| ~~**LIFE-EX-SRS-2**~~ (REMOVED) | `gradedPlan.flashcardId` threads unchanged through the client: plan (`activePlanRef`) → `FeedbackState.flashcardId` (set at graded-chip pick) → `PracticeOutcome.flashcardId` (both build sites: the feedback prefetch and feedback-Continue) → the `correction` event payload (`commitNode` and the `mergeLocalEvents` overlay mirror; key OMITTED when null). On feedback-Continue, when the id resolves against the LIVE loaded deck, `creditSrsAttempt` fires `recordAttempt({uid, cardId, correct, card})` with the card's current SM-2 state (`interval`/`ease`/`reps`/`lapses`/`consecutiveCorrectDays`/`lastReviewedAt`) — the identical write practice mode performs (SM-2 schedule + correct/incorrect counters + the CARDS-ARCHIVE-2 auto-archive streak). Fire-and-forget: a write failure logs a warn and never blocks the narrative; an id that does NOT resolve (model hallucination, deleted card) logs a warn and attempts NO write. `reviewMode`/`nearMiss` are practice-mode concepts and stay at their defaults. |
| ~~**LIFE-EX-SRS-3**~~ (REMOVED) | The deck snapshot is CHAPTER-FROZEN. It is written at most once per chapter by the client (`useChapterDeck`): when `world.currentArc` is present AND (`world.deckSnapshot` is missing OR `deckSnapshot.chapterNumber !== currentArc.chapterNumber`), and ONLY from a server-confirmed deck read (`loading === false && fromCache === false` — a Firestore cache emit can be partial and must never be frozen). Mid-chapter deck changes (saves, archives, deletes) do NOT rewrite it — new cards enter the block at the next chapter, and still reach the model mid-chapter as `flashcard_added` events (LIFE-CTX-2/8). Between chapters (`currentArc: null`) the previous snapshot is kept as-is. The snapshot lives on the world doc so it syncs across devices, survives resume, and rides the existing `LifeStepInput.world` with zero new prompt plumbing. A world whose snapshot write hasn't landed yet (first turn of a fresh world) renders `<flashcards/>` — absence is always tolerated. |
| ~~**LIFE-EX-SRS-4**~~ (REMOVED) | Narrative re-exposure is an SI-only nudge: a few nodes after a word/phrase was tested (it appears in `<recentlyPracticed>`), NPC dialogue is encouraged to RE-USE it naturally — the player meets the word again as comprehensible input, not a quiz. No client mechanics, no counter, no schema field. |

### Design decisions (Phase G)

- **Why the snapshot is materialised on the world doc, not recomputed per turn:** "frozen per chapter" requires an as-of-chapter-start capture — recomputing from the live deck would shift the block's bytes on every mid-chapter save and defeat the LIFE-CTX-1 prefix cache, which is the whole point of freezing. The world doc is already in every prompt input, syncs across devices, and survives resume. This also matches the §19 explicit-cache plan ("the flashcard set is frozen for the chapter; new cards added mid-chapter wait for the next chapter").
- **Why id + phrase only:** 100 cards ≈ 1–2k tokens. Adding translations roughly doubles that for zero planner value — the graded generator (LIFE-EX-GEN-*) authors its own glosses, and the planner only needs to recognise the surface form in a scene.
- **Why the credit resolves against the LIVE deck, not the snapshot:** the resolve-guard exists solely to prevent `updateDoc` on a missing doc. A card saved mid-chapter (present live, absent from the frozen block) can still be tagged by the model via its `flashcard_added` event exposure — that review should still credit.
- **Why the planner picks the card (no selector call):** the plan's cost stance — the LLM already holds story + deck + `<recentlyPracticed>` in one context; a separate selection call would add latency and spend for a worse decision.
- **Why duplicates aren't deduped in the block:** two cards can share a phrase (different translations); each is its own SRS row, and the model tagging either id credits a real card. Deduping would silently orphan one card's schedule.

## 10.48 Story shape — `LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-*`

The original layered model (saga → chapterPlan → plotBeats → phase progression) gave us narrative coherence at the cost of player agency. The model was reading every layer as a checklist and pushing the player toward the next prescribed beat, regardless of what the player actually did. The relaxed shape preserves DIRECTION (the saga's pillars) without prescribing the PATH.

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-1** | The saga's pillars (`midpointTwist`, `climax`, `endStates`, `antagonist`, `factions`, `loveInterestFate`) are ANCHORS — they say "the story rhymes toward these". The saga's `storyMovements` (3-4 broad arcs like "discovery and stakes" / "alliances and the truth" / "the choice" / "aftermath") replace the old `chapterPlan` rail (which named 5-8 specific chapters with synopses). Number-of-chapters becomes EMERGENT, not declared — a saga can resolve in 4 chapters or 12 depending on how the player traverses it. |
| **LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-2** | Chapter generation reads the saga's pillars + storyMovements + the completed-chapter memories — NOT a pre-written chapter synopsis. The model designs a fresh chapter `premise` that picks up where the player actually is, gestures toward the saga's pillars, and stays open enough to absorb whatever the player does next. A chapter resolves when its dramatic question is answered, regardless of how many scenes / turns it took. |
| **LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-3** | Scenes drop the `plotBeats[]{status}` checklist. Outlines now carry `openBranches` (where the player COULD go) + `candidateEndings` (where this rhymes to) + `completionTriggers` (model-checked exit conditions). The scene is a SCENARIO, not a script. `currentPhase` is a gentle pacing hint — "we're roughly in the rising action" — not a waypoint the model must hit. |
| **LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-4** | `sceneReplan` fires aggressively. The old triggers (`replanRequested`, `hardPivot`, soft tick at 10 turns) stay, BUT the soft tick is now 4-6 turns (was 10) and the model is encouraged to flag divergence sooner. A replan re-reads the player's last 12 events + current outline + chapter context and produces a fresh outline — possibly with new candidateEndings, possibly with a different premise. The scene isn't precious. |
| **LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-5** | At chapter boundary (scene emits `arcStage: 'concluding'`), the next chapter's generator gets: saga (anchors + storyMovements) + completed-chapter memories + the just-completed chapter's `outcomeLine`. NOT a pre-allocated chapterPlan entry. The new chapter's premise is shaped by what actually happened, not what was planned. |
| **LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-6** | The model is the authority on whether the story is rhyming toward the saga's pillars. The client does NO consistency scoring. The model can ALSO emit `scene.replanRequested = true` (existing) when the player has drifted enough that the current outline is unsalvageable — the replan absorbs it. |
| **LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-7** | **SUPERSEDED (LIFE-ARCH-8 / `sagaOpen`) — kept for the measurement, not as a live contract.** `worldSagaSystemInstruction` and its probe (`tests/experiments/narrative-machinery/saga-probe.mjs`) were both deleted when `sagaOpen` replaced `worldSaga`, so the baseline quoted at the end of this row is no longer reproducible and nothing asserts it. Of the five rules below, only (d) carried across: the concrete-noun title rule now lives in `functions/src/prompts/lifeSagaOpen.ts` (server-owned since 2026-09-18) under **LIFE-LANG-14**. Rules (a) `VARIETY IS THE JOB`, (b) the OPPOSING FORCE reframing, (c) cross-genre worked examples and (e) the stock-name ban are NOT present in `sagaOpen` — re-establish them there with a fresh measurement before treating anti-collapse as covered. Original text: `worldSagaSystemInstruction` must produce sagas as varied as real books — the full tonal palette (comedy, romance, wonder, warmth, mischief, melancholy, awe, suspense) matched to the seed, NOT a single dark-conspiracy house style. Enforced in the SI, not by a deterministic check: (a) a `VARIETY IS THE JOB` block leads the instruction; (b) the `antagonist` field is reframed as **the OPPOSING FORCE** — a person, institution, rivalry, circumstance, scarcity, secret, or the player's own flaw — NAMED only when it is a person (schema unchanged: still a required `z.string()`); (c) worked examples in the DIRECTION + storyMovements sections deliberately span genres (a resistance saga AND a warm city romance sharing the same four-beat shape) so structural beats are not seeded to war; (d) titles must grow from a CONCRETE in-world noun (place/object/dish/person/phrase) — the `The ___ of ___` / mood-noun template (shadow/silence/whisper/legacy/secret) is named as the tell of a lazy draft; (e) names are cast to place+era, never stock literary/fantasy villain names (Valmont, Vane, Valerius, Blackwood, Thorne…). Baseline measurement (real Gemini, `tests/experiments/narrative-machinery/saga-probe.mjs`): 87% `___ of ___` titles, 100% dark tone / 0% warm, ~60% `Va-` villain surnames. |

---

## 10.5 Scenes — `LIFE-SCENE-*`

A scene is a multi-session story arc with a known endpoint. Between scenes the world runs in idle mode; while a scene is active, the lifeStep engine pulls from a scene outline so the narrative has direction.

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-SCENE-1** | At most ONE scene is active at a time. The active scene lives at `users/{uid}/lifeScene/active` (reserved doc id). Completed/abandoned scenes archive into the same collection under generated ids with `status !== 'active'`. `useLifeScene` subscribes to the `active` doc; null = idle mode. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-2** | The scene doc carries `{ id, status, outline, currentPhase, turnsSinceReplan, chosenEndingKey?, acceptedAt, resolvedAt? }`. The outline is `{ title, premise, tone, practiceFocus[], keyNpcs[], openBranches[], candidateEndings[], completionTriggers[], estimatedPhaseCount }`. **NB**: the old `plotBeats[]{id,summary,status}` checklist was removed on 2026-06-01 (LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-*) — the model was treating plotBeats as a rail it had to complete, dragging players toward the next beat. The scene now leans on `openBranches` (where the player COULD go) and `candidateEndings` (where this rhymes to) instead of a forced beat sequence. `currentPhase` stays as a gentle pacing hint, not a waypoint. Outline is read by every active-mode lifeStep prompt; rewritten by `sceneReplan` more aggressively than before. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-3** | Hooks ride inside `lifeStep` responses via the `scene.hook = { hookId, pitch, acceptChipId }` envelope field. The named chip's id is the "accept"; any other chip on that turn is implicitly a decline. The UI logs a `scene_hook_offered` event on offer and a `scene_hook_declined` event on decline; the SI's recent-events filter suppresses re-offering the same hookId for 5 turns. Hooks fire opportunistically (the SI suggests ~every 5-10 idle turns when it fits) and are NOT counter-driven on the client. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-4** | Replan triggers from EITHER (a) the model emitting `scene.replanRequested === true`, (b) the model emitting `scene.hardPivot === true` (immediate), OR (c) a soft client-side tick when `outline.turnsSinceReplan >= 10`. NO divergence-scoring on the client — the model is the authority. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-5** | `sceneReplan` runs as a BACKGROUND promise (stored in a ref) kicked off after the lifeStep response that requested it; the next runLifeStep awaits the ref so it reads the refreshed outline. The replan never blocks chip rendering — its wait is absorbed by the player's read / practice time on the just-arrived turn. Graded turns are the canonical case (long read time); on non-graded turns the wait happens between the player's next pick and the next response. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-6** | `sceneGenerate` is a one-shot Gemini call on hook accept (model: `gemini-3.1-flash`, high temperature). It writes the active scene doc + pre-promotes `keyNpcs[]` into the persistent NPC roster with scene scope. On generation failure a TRIVIAL outline (just the hook pitch + a single pending beat) lands so the player isn't blocked — the failure logs at `warn` with the `[LIFE/SCENE-GEN-FAILED]` prefix. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-7** | **RETIRED (LIFE-SCENE-36 / `sceneClose`).** `sceneReplan` was deleted, not replaced in kind — `story-architecture.md` §"sceneClose replaces sceneReplan" records the call count going 4-6 → 0 per scene, and `src/lib/life/runSceneReplan.ts` / `src/lib/life/prompts/sceneReplan.ts` no longer exist. The outline is now revised only at scene close. Original text: `sceneReplan` runs on `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` (cheap, runs more often). Input: `(world, outline, currentPhase, recentEvents, reason)`. Output: same-shape outline with completed beats marked, new beats added if the player diverged, narrowed candidate endings, refreshed open branches. On failure: the existing outline is preserved unchanged; the failure logs at `warn` with the `[LIFE/SCENE-REPLAN-FAILED]` prefix. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-8** | NPCs carry `sceneId: string | null`. World NPCs (`null`) recur everywhere. Scene-scoped NPCs (`<sceneId>`) only surface in the prompt's `<npcRoster>` block while THAT scene is active. On scene archive they remain in the `lifeNpcs` collection but disappear from the active roster (their `sceneId` stays set — the filter is at read time, not write). `promoteOrTouchNpc` accepts an optional `sceneId` that is sticky (not cleared on re-touch). |
| **LIFE-SCENE-9** | Scene themes MUST fit the world seed. The SI references `worldSeedKey` explicitly: practical seeds (`city_life`, `studying_abroad`, `country_life`, `just_moved_here`) get grounded everyday arcs; fantastical seeds (`magic_apprentice`, `wandering_bard`, `frontier_colony`, `noir_detective`) get genre-appropriate arcs. The sceneGenerate SI carries this rule too — a fantasy-world player should not be offered a "submit art to the gallery" arc. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-10** | A scene completes when the model emits `scene.resolved = { endingKey, outcomeLine }` in a turn response. On completion: a `SceneCompleteScreen` overlay shows title + **the world's record (LIFE-SCENE-31)** + graded-question stats (computed from `correction` events between `acceptedAt → now`) + a "Return home" CTA. The CTA fires `closeSceneFlow` which (a) appends a templated `<completedScene>` line to `world.bio` so the memory persists across future turns — **capped to the last N entries (LIFE-SCENE-13)** — (b) moves the active doc to history via `archiveScene(uid, 'completed')`, (c) writes a `scene_completed` event to the log. After close, the player returns to idle mode and the next lifeStep fires. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-11** | Temperature is HIGH (0.95) across all life-sim Gemini calls — `lifeStep`, `sceneGenerate`, `sceneReplan`, `generateBio`. Creative variety + low repetition is the dominant concern; the structured-JSON schema gates correctness. Compaction stays at default temperature (faithfulness matters more than variety there). |
| **LIFE-SCENE-12** | The dev console logs every Gemini round-trip with a `[LIFE/...]` prefix via `lib/life/debugLog.ts`. Successful turns log at `info` (`[LIFE/STEP]`, `[LIFE/SCENE-GEN]`, `[LIFE/SCENE-REPLAN]`); failures log at `warn` with the `-FAILED` suffix. Payloads include parsed response bodies so a developer can see at a glance when a replan fires + what the new storyline is. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-13** | **Bio scene-memory de-accretion.** `world.bio` is the frozen `<bio>…</bio>` character sheet followed by an accreting tail of `\n<completedScene …>…</completedScene>` lines (one appended per scene resolve by LIFE-SCENE-10). Because the bio is embedded VERBATIM in the stable prefix of every step prompt (`prompts/step.ts`, LIFE-CTX-1), an unbounded tail bloats every turn's token bill AND trends the world doc toward Firestore's 1 MiB ceiling. The append (`appendSceneMemory` in `lib/life/bioMemory.ts`) therefore CAPS the tail to the last `MAX_BIO_SCENE_MEMORIES` (= 8) entries — oldest dropped, base `<bio>` block always preserved, format byte-identical below the cap. Nothing is lost permanently: every scene outcome is also recorded as a superset (with `keyMomentsForArc`) in its arc's `completedSceneMemories[]` (LIFE-WORLD-ARC-2), archived to `completedArcs/{arcId}` on chapter close. The bio tail is just the recent-history window the prompt sees; older continuity rides the `storyLog` spine (`LIFE-STORY-LOG-2`) + archived arc memories. The cap is self-healing — an over-cap bio (e.g. a pre-existing world) trims to N on its next close. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-31** | **The completion screens lead with what the WORLD recorded, not the last beat.** `resolved.outcomeLine` is the resolution BEAT, and `LIFE-SCENE-30` already parks both overlays behind a "View scene summary" chip so the player reads that beat IN THE STORY first — so showing it again on the card spent the summary slot on a sentence they had finished one screen earlier. The scene card now leads with the line `runSceneClose` wrote (`historyLine`), which is the compacted record every later turn reads back as history, so the card and the story cannot disagree about what happened; it is system-language by construction (the close reasons in the system language), so the `LIFE-SCENE-28` gloss is unnecessary. It arrives via a new `onSceneSummary` engine callback, LATER than `onSceneResolved` — the close is a separate generation kicked off by the resolving turn — so the card renders a pulsing placeholder holding the slot's height rather than blocking on it. The chapter card gets the same treatment: its `LIFE-DAY-2` day list (one line per resolved scene, from `arc.completedSceneMemories`) moves up to lead, and its `outcomeLine` is dropped. **Addendum 2026-08-10 — THIS INVARIANT SHIPPED WITH ITS E2E LEFT ASSERTING THE DELETED ELEMENTS, and stayed red unnoticed for four days.** `0a810a7` removed `LifeScreen-SceneComplete-Outcome` / `-OutcomeNative` and `LifeScreen-WorldArcComplete-Outcome` / `-OutcomeNative`; two specs in `life-flow.spec.ts` (`LIFE-VOCAB-RECAP-2` and `@standard LIFE-SCENE-29/30`) went on asserting all four and failed with *element(s) not found* on every run. The unit tests DID move — `SceneCompleteScreen.test.tsx` and `WorldArcCompleteScreen.test.tsx` both assert the elements are absent — which is why nothing local went red. **The reason it hid so well is worth naming: the CONTENT never changed.** `absorbSceneIntoArc` stores the resolution beat as the day's summary, so the exact sentences those assertions look for are still on both cards; only the elements carrying them moved. A stale-selector failure that reads *"element not found"* on text that is plainly on screen is easy to file as harness flake. Fixed with the `LIFE-SCENE-31.1` work: both specs now assert the leads this invariant actually created — `-Summary` on the scene card, `-Day` / `-DayNative` on the chapter card. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-23** | **The 2026-08 `campaign` → `scene` rename.** A "campaign" is now a SCENE: one subplot around one subject, several to a chapter. Renamed throughout — `LIFE-CAMPAIGN-*` → `LIFE-SCENE-*`, `LifeCampaign` → `LifeScene`, `campaignLifecycle` → `sceneLifecycle`, `CampaignCompleteScreen` → `SceneCompleteScreen`, the four `campaign_*` event kinds → `scene_*`, the `life.campaignComplete.*` i18n keys → `life.sceneComplete.*` (moved IN PLACE across all 80 locales, so each translation and its hash sidecar rode across and nothing was re-bought), and the per-world `lifeWorlds/{worldId}/campaigns` subcollection → `scenes`. **NO back-compat, by decision** — same call as `LIFE-BACKDROP-22`. The first cut had `useLifeScene` subscribe to both paths, `updateActiveScene` copy the live scene forward on its next write, and `archiveScene` look in both; that mattered because unlike a backdrop cache the `active` doc is the player's live story, and reading only the new path presents a mid-scene player as idle. With beta data disposable, none of it is worth the standing complexity — an existing world simply starts fresh, which `LIFE-SCENE-24` now does on its own. `scene_hook_offered` / `scene_hook_declined` were dropped from `ZLifeEventKind` entirely at the same time (they were already dead under `LIFE-SCENE-24`, and were only being kept to render pre-rename history as nothing). |
| **LIFE-SCENE-24** | **Hooks are DELETED. A scene starts because the player has no scene, not because they accepted a pitch.** The hook was a two-step handshake: the step model emitted `scene.hook` with a one-line pitch and nominated an `acceptChipId`, the player picked it, and only then did `sceneGenerate` write an outline. `LIFE-ARC-PACE-3` measured what that cost — **zero hooks offered across two harness runs (25 turns and 12), `<scene status="idle"/>` on every single turn** — so no chapter could ever end, because a chapter ends only when a scene resolves. The fix at the time was `<hookPressure>`, which made the idle count observable and nagged at 6 and 10 turns. That is the third instance of the same defect class, and the lesson `LIFE-LANG-13.1` and `LIFE-SPEECH-3` both land on: **a stated request the model can satisfy by doing nothing is not a mechanism.** So the handshake goes rather than being nagged harder. **What replaces it:** when a world has no active scene and is not loading one, the engine starts one — no pitch, no accept chip, no decline path. `startScene` (was `acceptSceneHook`) calls `runSceneGenerate` with the world, the saga and the recent events, and the generator invents the premise itself. It is strictly better grounded than the pitch was: the pitch was one line written by the step model mid-turn, whereas the generator holds `storyMovements`, the chapter's intent, the NPC roster and the open threads. **Deleted with it:** `scene.hook` from `StepSceneSignal`, the `scene_hook_offered` / `scene_hook_declined` event kinds, `hookIdleStreak`, `renderHookPressure` + `SCENE_DUE`/`SCENE_OVERDUE`, `pendingHookRef` + `acceptHookPick`, and the SI's whole hook block. The two event kinds stay in `ZLifeEventKind` as read-only history — a world that ran under the old flow still has them in its log, and `stepRender` must not throw on a kind it can no longer produce; they simply render nothing. **Idle is now a transient, not a state.** `<scene status="idle"/>` can still appear for the turn during which generation is in flight, but nothing asks the model to do anything about it, and it cannot persist across turns. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-25** | **A scene is ONE subject, 6-12 turns, and the model states how far through it is on every turn.** The restructure's shape: several scenes make a chapter, so a player can finish something in a sitting instead of grinding at a day that never ends (owner: *"Days are too long… I've been playing for ages and the day seems nowhere near complete"*). Three pieces. **(a) A DECLARED SUBJECT.** `SceneOutline.subject` is the ONE thing the scene revolves around, in a handful of words — distinct from `premise` (the situation) and `title` (the label). It is the test a beat has to pass to belong here: a scene brings several people in and out, but a beat that serves a different subject IS the next scene. Optional in the schema so pre-2026-08 docs parse; the pace block falls back to the premise. **(b) `<scenePace>`, reframed from DAY to SCENE.** Same 6/9/12 thresholds, kept deliberately (owner: *"Keep 6/9/12 for a scene to play out"*). What changed is the ask: it used to say *"move the narration into the evening"*, because a scene WAS a day (`LIFE-DAY-1`). A player may now run three scenes in one sitting, so reaching for dusk at turn 9 of the first would break the clock rather than the scene. It now asks for the SUBJECT to land — and names it, so "bring it to an ending" points at a specific thing. It also publishes the floor. **(c) `scene.sceneProgress` (0-100), emitted EVERY turn.** The owner's ask, verbatim: *"You could ask the model to OUTPUT scene completeness so it is even more conscious of moving things along."* That is what it is for — a forcing function first, a signal second. `<scenePace>` says where the model is in the BUDGET; this makes it say where it is in the STORY, and having just written "30" at turn 9 it has to reconcile the two itself. **Deliberately advisory** — nothing gates on the number, because a model that learns a value ends the scene simply stops emitting high ones. The budget and the floor are what bound the length. The pre-existing volatile `<sceneProgress phase=… turnsSinceReplan=…/>` tag was renamed `<scenePhase>` to free the name; two different quantities under one tag would be unreadable to the model and to us. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-25.1** | **The scene turn counter restarts when the scene id changes, and at no other time.** Owner, verbatim: *"The turn counter should never spell into the next scene's quota. The counter should restart when and only when the scene changes."* Both halves are load-bearing and each fails silently in a different direction. **Never resetting** makes every scene after the first look overdue from its first turn — a scene that overran to 15 hands the next one a debt instead of a budget. **Resetting too eagerly** means a scene never reaches its window, so it can run forever. `commitNode` keys the reset on `storyLog.sceneId !== input.sceneId`, and an UNDEFINED `sceneId` means *"don't know"* rather than *"no scene"* — treating it as a change would zero the count on every commit that omits it. Reset on the id CHANGING rather than on resolve, so a resolve/start pair cannot leave a stale total behind. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-26** | **A resolve below `SCENE_FLOOR` (4 turns) is REFUSED in code.** The owner named two failure modes — scenes that run too long AND *"too small a scene should be discouraged"* — and only the first was ever guarded. A model that resolves on turn 2 because a `completionTrigger` happened to match hands the player a completion screen for something they barely played. Refused rather than requested, for the reason this spec keeps relearning (`LIFE-LANG-13.1`, `LIFE-SPEECH-3`, `LIFE-SCENE-24`): the SI already said "don't end it too early", and a rule the model can satisfy by doing nothing is not a rule. On refusal the scene simply CONTINUES — the model gets another turn and `<scenePace>` keeps counting; nothing is thrown and no error surfaces to the player. It is a FLOOR, not a minimum length: resolving at 4 or 5 turns is allowed, because a subject that genuinely lands early should be allowed to. The floor sits well below `SCENE_IN_RANGE` so it can never refuse a resolve inside the window `<scenePace>` is asking for. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-27** | **Every scene ends on the scene-complete screen; the chapter screen follows only when the chapter ends.** Owner: *"Keep the scene complete screen. After all scenes in a chapter are done, show chapter complete screen as it is now."* The MACHINERY was already right — `onSceneResolved` renders `SceneCompleteScreen` on every resolve, and `arcStage: 'concluding'` additionally renders `WorldArcCompleteScreen`, with `chapterAlsoEnding` suppressing the scene screen's continuation teaser so the two do not contradict each other. What was wrong was the WORDS. The screen said **"Day complete"** and teased **"Come back tomorrow to see where the story takes you"**, because a scene WAS a day (`LIFE-DAY-1`). Under `LIFE-SCENE-25` a player may finish three scenes in one sitting, so that fired three times an evening and invited them back tomorrow while they were plainly still playing — the copy actively fighting the restructure's whole point, which was to let someone *finish something* in a short session. It now reads "Scene complete" and "The story picks up from here whenever you are ready", which is true whether they stop or carry straight on. The chapter screen is unchanged: it is still the bigger, rarer milestone, and `life.sceneComplete.seeChapterSummary` still routes there instead of home when both land on the same turn (`LIFE-DAY-4`). |
| **LIFE-SCENE-28** | **The outcome line renders in BOTH languages, on the scene screen AND the chapter screen.** Owner: *"the last event shows at the top showing how the scene finished, make sure it is shown in practice AND native, not just one."* `scene.resolved.outcomeLine` was practice-language only, and it is the ONLY piece of story on either completion card — so a learner who cannot yet read the practice line was told how their own scene ended in the language they are still learning, with no gloss and no flip to reach for. Every other narrative surface in the sim already pairs the two rows (`LIFE-LANG-*`); this one screen never did. **The model now emits `outcomeLineNative` — the SAME sentence in the system language — as a required field of `scene.resolved`**, rather than the client translating it after the fact: a second Gemini round-trip to translate one sentence would put a spinner on the completion screen, and the step model is already holding both languages for every other line it writes this turn. Optional in the Zod schema so a scene resolved before this shipped still parses; `snapshotSceneCompletion` defaults it to `''`, and BOTH screens simply omit the muted second row when it is blank (`.trim()`, so a whitespace-only value counts as absent) rather than rendering an empty line. The practice line LEADS on both — it is the one worth reading — with the twin under it, muted and smaller. **The chapter screen was fixed in the same pass** even though the report named only the scene one: `WorldArcCompleteScreen` renders the resolving scene's outcome as its own blockquote and had the identical defect one screen over. It reads the twin from the in-memory snapshot, NOT from `arc.outcomeLine` — the `completedArcs/{arcId}` doc is a persistence record and stays single-language by design, so nothing about this is a migration. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-29** | **Two CTAs, each saying what it actually does.** Owner: *"the input button at the bottom is return home which actually return back to the story."* The single button was labelled `life.sceneComplete.returnHome` — "Return home" — and called `continueTurn()`: it resumed the story, which is the opposite of what it promised. A player who wanted to stop had no way out of the wurld except the system back gesture, and a player who wanted to carry on had to press a button that said they were leaving. The screen now offers **"Continue the story"** (primary, `onClose` → `continueTurn()`) and **"Return to Wurlds home"** (secondary, `onClose` → `pauseToOverview()`), and the `returnHome` key is DELETED from `en.ts` and all 80 locales rather than left orphaned — a key that names a lie is worse than a missing one, because the next screen to need "return home" would silently inherit it. **The chapter-ending path keeps ONE button** (`chapterAlsoEnding`): `WorldArcCompleteScreen` follows immediately and owns the exit, so offering "return to Wurlds" here would skip the larger of the two milestones — the same reasoning as `LIFE-DAY-4`, which is why that branch still reads "See chapter summary". Both buttons go inert together while the archive write is in flight. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-35** | **The chapter recap's day list is BILINGUAL — `LIFE-SCENE-31` regressed `LIFE-SCENE-28` one screen over, and nothing noticed.** Owner, 2026-08-06: *"the chapter summary, how the chapter went section, is also only in practice and needs to be either only in native or in both."* `LIFE-SCENE-28` had made both completion cards bilingual and closed with the reasoning that `completedArcs/{arcId}` *"is a persistence record and stays single-language by design"*. `LIFE-SCENE-31` then promoted that record to the chapter card's LEAD content — the `LIFE-DAY-2` day list, one line per resolved scene read from `arc.completedSceneMemories[].summary` — and dropped the bilingual blockquote that had sat above it. The premise of `LIFE-SCENE-28`'s carve-out ("nobody reads the persistence record") stopped being true in the same commit that made it the thing the player reads. **The two invariants never disagreed on any line of text, which is why no test caught it:** `LIFE-SCENE-31`'s claim that "the chapter card gets the same treatment" was false in exactly one respect — the scene card's new lead (`historyLine`) is system-language by construction, while the chapter card's new lead is `snapshot.outcomeLine`, the practice-language resolution BEAT. Same sentence, opposite language, one word of spec apart. **Fix:** `ZWorldArcSceneMemory.summaryNative` carries `snapshot.outcomeLineNative` (already computed, already required of the model by `LIFE-SCENE-28`, and until now simply dropped on the floor at `absorbSceneIntoArc`), and each day row renders the practice line over a muted native twin — the same pairing every other narrative surface uses. Optional + `.trim()`-guarded, so a chapter recorded before this shipped renders exactly as it does today rather than showing an empty second row. **Not backfilled:** the native twin was never stored, and re-deriving it would mean translating every archived scene outcome. |
| **LIFE-SCENE-30** | **The resolution beat plays out on screen BEFORE the summary; the player opens it.** Owner: *"delay the modal so it plays out on screen and then shows in the modal. Maybe a view scene summary button shows where the options would have been so the user is in control of when to move to the modal."* The modal used to mount the instant `scene.resolved` arrived — covering the very line that explained how the scene ended, which is also the line `LIFE-SCENE-28` had just made readable. **A timer was the obvious fix and is the wrong one**: how long the beat takes to read depends on its length, the player's reading speed and their language level, so any constant is wrong for someone — and the same lesson this spec keeps relearning applies (`LIFE-SCENE-24`, `LIFE-LANG-13.1`) in that a delay the player cannot see or control is not an affordance. So the snapshot lands in `pendingSceneComplete` instead of `completedScene`, and a single **"View scene summary"** chip takes the place of the options; tapping it promotes pending → completed and the modal mounts. **The chip replaces the options rather than sitting beside them**: the resolving turn still produced options, and rendering them invites the player to walk on past an ending they have not been shown. **Checked BEFORE every phase branch in `LifeScreenBottom`, and deliberately NOT gated on `phase`** — resolution can land on a normal options turn or on a graded one, and a phase list here would be a second copy of the engine's state machine that silently stops matching it. **TWO surfaces are excepted, and the rule that picks them is "mid-ACTIVITY, not between turns"**: the feedback overlay (`feedback && phase === 'feedback'` — the player has answered but has not yet seen whether they were right, so taking it away loses the grading entirely) and a card-match review (`phase === 'card-match'` — a partly-matched grid is work already done, `LIFE-EX-10`). Both hand the screen back on their own, and the chip is waiting when they do. Note this is NOT a phase list creeping back in: it is the two states that own the screen *because the player is inside them*, which is a different question from which phase the turn is in. **Known consequence, accepted:** a player who leaves the wurld between the resolve and the final button leaves the scene doc `active` — `closeSceneFlow` runs from the modal's CTA, not from the resolve. That window already existed (the auto-mounted modal also had to be dismissed) and the delay does not widen it in practice, because the chip stands exactly where the options were and the story cannot be continued around it. On return the scene simply carries on and `<scenePace>` keeps counting, which is the same state a mid-scene exit has always produced. |

---

## 10.6 Multi-world model — `LIFE-WORLD-*`

A user owns multiple worlds in parallel. Each world is a character's life — its own seed, language, NPCs, scenes, scenes, and chapter through-line. The legacy singleton `lifeWorld/main` path is retired; everything is keyed by `worldId`.

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-WORLD-1** | Worlds live at `users/{uid}/lifeWorlds/{worldId}` (collection of doc IDs minted by `newWorldId()`). Each world owns subcollections `events/`, `npcs/`, `scenes/`, `scenes/` — all reads/writes are scoped under the world. The legacy top-level collections (`lifeEvents`, `lifeNpcs`, `lifeBackdrops`, `lifeScene`, `lifeWorld`) are read-and-delete only — kept addressable for cleanup but no new data ever lands there. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-2** | Hard cap of 5 worlds per user (`MAX_WORLDS = 5`). The overview's "+ Start a new wurld" CTA disables when the cap is hit; user must delete a world to make room. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-3** | `/life` ALWAYS redirects to `/life/overview`. No deep-link to an active world from the bare `/life` URL. The overview lists every world with a per-card three-dot menu; tapping a card navigates to `/life/{worldId}` and bumps `lifeProfile/main.lastSeenWorldId`. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-4** | Each world's Storage prefix is `lifeAssets/{uid}/{worldId}/...`. Deletion (`deleteLifeWorld(uid, worldId)`) wipes the world doc + every subcollection + the Storage prefix. `Promise.allSettled` is used for subcollection listing so a single failing read doesn't atomically block the wipe. After deletion the `lifeProfile.lastSeenWorldId` may dangle; the overview handles missing ids gracefully. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-5** | The LifeScreen header carries a 3-dot menu offering "Exit to overview" (navigates without delete) and "Delete this wurld" (with a confirm modal → runs `deleteLifeWorld(uid, worldId)` → routes to `/life/overview`). Deletion of the active world is acceptable; the player lands on overview, where the deleted world is gone from the list. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-6** | Onboarding (`/life/start`) creates ONE new world per submission: mints a `worldId`, calls `createLifeWorld({uid, worldId, ...})`, navigates to `/life/{worldId}`. Bio generation + chapter-1 worldArc generation fire in parallel non-blocking — their results land on the world doc within a few seconds. The lifeStep engine reads bio + worldArc when present; absence is non-fatal. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-9** | The overview `WorldCard` renders a scene-thumbnail banner at the top of its tap target: the world's most-recently-persisted scene SVG, resolved READ-ONLY via `useWorldThumbnail(world)`. Resolution order: (1) sync localStorage scene-cache hit (`readBackdropCache(descriptorFromWorld(world), world.id)`); (2) async Firestore mirror (`readPersistentBackdrop(uid, world.id, descriptor)` — a hit also re-seeds the local cache via `writeBackdropCache`); (3) the bundled fallback silhouette for the descriptor's category (`getFallbackBackdrop(pickFallback(descriptor))`), shown immediately while (2) is in flight and kept on miss. Thumbnail resolution NEVER calls `generateBackdrop` or any other Gemini path — zero LLM cost from the overview. A world with no `currentBackdrop` descriptor (fresh world) resolves to `null` and renders NO banner — the card keeps its compact text-only layout, so banner presence is deterministic on `currentBackdrop` presence, never on network timing. |

| **LIFE-WORLD-12** | **The empty-state sample cards are THEME-AWARE — the hue is a TINT over the themed surface, never a fill.** The four illustrative cards on `LifeOverviewEmpty` (`data-testid="LifeOverview-Sample"`, one per `SAMPLE_WORLDS` entry) wear the SAME surface a real `WorldCard` wears: `rounded-2xl` + `bg-(--color-bg-elevated)` + a `--color-border`-derived border, `--color-fg` title, `--color-fg-muted` premise + language label. Per-card identity comes from ONE CSS colour (`SampleWorld.tint`) entering the card as the `--sample-tint` custom property and being mixed down over that surface — a `color-mix(… N%, transparent)` wash (20% → 8%) plus a tinted emoji medallion. **No card may carry a fixed-palette colour class** (`from-rose-200`, `text-slate-900`, `border-white/40`, …): a fixed pastel fill forces fixed dark text to stay legible, and both then ignore the theme — in dark mode that rendered as four bright slabs with near-black text glowing off the dark page (owner report 2026-07-31: "the example cards … colours are a bit off putting"). The four tints stay DISTINCT (variety is the point of the screen) but are chosen mid-saturation / mid-lightness so each reads as a tint over BOTH `#ffffff` and `#1a1928`. The card's tap behaviour is unchanged — every sample navigates to `/wurlds/start` (the samples are not selectable; the player picks their own seed in onboarding). |
| **LIFE-WORLD-10** | **The overview header is a TAB-ROOT header, like every other tab's.** `/wurlds/overview` is where the Wurlds tab lands, so it wears the same header the other tab roots wear (`ChatListScreen`, `ContactsScreen`, `CardsScreen`): `px-4 pt-safe-top pb-3` around a `page-measure` block holding a `text-3xl font-bold` `<h1>` over a `text-sm text-(--color-fg-muted)` subtitle, with any right-hand affordance on the same row as the title. Copy is title "Wurlds" / subtitle "Your other Wurlds" (LIFE-UI-8) — the old single `text-sm font-semibold` line read as a sub-page's header, not a section's. **No back arrow**: a tab root is not a child of anything a chevron could point at, and none of the other three carry one. Back is still fully defined for the Android hardware button and any programmatic caller through `resolveParent('/wurlds/overview') === '/'` (NAV-PARENT-1) — only the redundant on-screen affordance is gone. The streak badge (LIFE-STREAK-4) keeps its place, moving to the right of the title block. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-17** | **The card carries the wurld's name IN BOTH LANGUAGES, and its story line is real prose or nothing.** (a) **Title translation.** Under the practice-language saga title (`saga.title`, `text-gradient-accent`) the card renders `saga.titleNative` as a `text-xs text-(--color-fg-muted)` line, `data-testid="LifeOverview-WorldCard-TitleNative"` — the same pairing `WorldIntroScreen` already shows, brought to the surface the player actually looks at (owner, 2026-08-05: *"make sure to show the translation of the wurld name on the card on the wurld homescreen too"*). Rendered ONLY when non-empty AND not case-insensitively equal to the title: a legacy saga has `titleNative: ''`, and a one-word title that happens to be identical in both languages ("Marseille") would otherwise print twice. It is NOT a fallback for a missing `title` — an untranslated title stays a single line rather than being replaced by its own gloss. (b) **Story line.** The teaser (`saga.playerFacingTeaser`, `line-clamp-3`) is generated at world open per `LIFE-ARCH-8`; `life.overview.story.pending` remains the fallback and is now what it says it is — a world whose saga has not landed yet — rather than the permanent state of every card. |

> `LIFE-WORLD-7` / `LIFE-WORLD-8` are intentionally unallocated — the Wurlds-v2 plan (deleted 2026-09-30; `git show 9c70919:docs/archive/wurlds-v2/00-approved-plan.md`) pinned phase→invariant-id assignments across parallel phase docs (Phase B = `LIFE-WORLD-9`). Do not backfill 7/8 without checking that plan.

### Design decisions (LIFE-WORLD-12 — sample-card colour)

- **Why the fixed pastels were wrong, not just ugly.** `from-rose-200 to-amber-200` is a *light-mode* decision baked into a component that renders in both themes. Because the fill is opaque, the text on top must also be fixed (`text-slate-900`) or it disappears — so ONE fixed choice propagates to every colour on the card, and the border (`border-white/40`) too. The result is theme-proof in the bad sense: flipping to dark changes the page around the cards and nothing on them. Mixing a hue over `--color-bg-elevated` inverts the dependency — the *theme* decides the surface, the sample only shifts its hue — so a future change to the theme tokens carries the cards with it for free.
- **Why a tint and not four accent-only cards.** Dropping colour entirely (four identical `--color-bg-elevated` cards) would have been the safest theme-wise, but the screen's job is to show the RANGE of what a wurld can be; four identical rows undersell that. A ≤20% tint keeps the variety while leaving `--color-fg` at full contrast on every card in both themes.
- **Why `color-mix` inline rather than Tailwind palette classes with an opacity modifier.** `bg-rose-500/10` would also be translucent, but it re-introduces a palette step per card — the thing this invariant bans — and the class list is then the place a future edit can quietly go opaque again. One `--sample-tint` custom property with mixes derived from it makes the "always a mix, never a fill" rule structural. `color-mix` is already in use in `globals.css` (`.skeleton-block`, `.life-scene-building`), so it is proven on every deploy-target WebView.
- **Judgment call left open: the title colour.** The real `WorldCard` titles its cards with `.text-gradient-accent` (violet→magenta). The samples deliberately use flat `--color-fg` instead: four gradient-accent titles stacked under an already-gradient hero read as heavy, and a magenta title over an amber wash fights it. If the owner would rather the samples mirror `WorldCard` exactly, swapping `text-(--color-fg)` for `text-gradient-accent` on the title line is a one-word change — but then the tints should probably drop to ~12% so the two colour systems stop competing.
- **Revisit if:** the theme tokens gain a proper "tinted surface" utility in `globals.css` (this component would be its first consumer), or the sample list stops being a hard-coded four (per-card hues would then need to be derived, not authored).

## 10.6.1 Global engagement streak — `LIFE-STREAK-*`

There is ONE streak per user, not per-world and not per-language. It advances when the user completes EITHER a practice session OR a wurld chapter on a given local-TZ day — the app's two "you did a real unit of learning today" moments feed the SAME counter. It replaces the old per-world `sessionDayCount` badge (which counted world OPENS, not completions) as the surfaced streak. (The practice **dashboard's** own per-language streak header, `PRACTICE-DASH-4/11`, is a separate analytics view and is unchanged; the global streak is additive.)

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-STREAK-1** | The streak is stored on the user doc (`users/{uid}`) as a nested `streak: { count, lastDay }` map (`ZUser.streak`, both append-only/optional). `count` = consecutive local-TZ days ending on `lastDay`; `lastDay` = the local `YYYY-MM-DD` of the most recent completion. Advanced by `recordStreakCompletion(uid)` (`src/lib/streak.ts`) via a read-modify-write keyed on the local day using the PURE `advanceStreak(prev, todayKey)`: no prior state → `count 1`; `lastDay === today` → **unchanged** (idempotent — a second completion the same day, e.g. practice AND a chapter, never double-counts); `lastDay === yesterday` → `count + 1`; older → reset to `count 1`. Same-day / unchanged writes are skipped. The user doc is the source of truth because it is the only per-user singleton already reactively available app-wide (`useUserProfile`) and owner-writable via `setDoc` merge — **no Firestore rules change** (same owner-write path `writeUserSetting` already uses). |
| **LIFE-STREAK-2** | Displayed count is derived for TODAY by the PURE `currentStreak(state, todayKey)`: alive (shows `count`) iff `lastDay` is today OR yesterday (the user still has today to keep it going); a full missed calendar day (`lastDay` ≥ 2 days ago) decays it to 0 without any write. This mirrors the practice dashboard's "no carry" convention (`PRACTICE-DASH-11`) with a one-day grace window. |
| **LIFE-STREAK-3** | Both completion events feed the same helper: (a) **practice** — the `SessionSummary` persist effect calls `recordStreakCompletion(uid)` alongside `recordPracticeSession`, guarded by the same `persistedRef` so strict-mode's double mount can't double-record; (b) **chapter** — `useChapterCompletion.closeScene` calls it when an arc is archived (`arcStage: 'concluding'` → `archiveActiveArc` succeeded, the `{ kind: 'chapter-complete' }` path). Both are fire-and-forget; `recordStreakCompletion` is defensive — a read failure SKIPS the write (never clobbers a real streak to 1) and a write failure logs + swallows. |
| **LIFE-STREAK-4** | The streak indicator (flame + count) renders ONLY in the Wurlds **overview header** (`LifeOverviewScreen`, `data-testid="LifeOverview-StreakBadge"`), reading the global streak via `currentStreak(readStreakState(profile.data))`. The per-`WorldCard` flame badge and the in-wurld `LifeScreenHeader` streak badge were REMOVED — the streak is global, so it does not belong on individual cards, and it is not shown while inside a wurld. |

---

## 10.7 World arcs (chapters) — `LIFE-WORLD-ARC-*`

A world has CHAPTERS. Each chapter is a multi-scene through-line — a dramatic question that ties 3-6 scenes into one shape. Chapters end with a celebration screen; the player chooses Continue (generate next chapter) or Pause (return to overview).

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| **LIFE-ARC-PACE-1** | **The chapter's turn count is RENDERED into the prompt; the pace rule was previously unactionable.** The step SI has long asked for "roughly 25-35 PLAYER TURNS end-to-end … don't let one drag past ~40" — but **nothing ever told the model which turn it was on**, so the rule was expressed in a quantity the model could not observe, and chapters ran indefinitely in real play. This is the same defect class as `LIFE-EX-CADENCE-1`: an instruction stated in terms the model cannot measure is not an instruction. The mechanism to END a chapter was never broken — `scene.resolved.arcStage === 'concluding'` → `useChapterCompletion` → `archiveActiveArc` is wired and tested — but the signal that triggers it defaults to `'continues'` when omitted (`sceneLifecycle.ts`), so silence means "carry on forever". `<chapterPace turns="N" stage="…" ceiling="40">` now renders in the volatile tail. Three tiers, escalating like the cadence directive: **in-range** (≥25) is guidance about SCOPE only — prefer deepening open threads to opening new ones; **due** (≥32) says the next scene to reach its resolution beat should carry `arcStage: "concluding"`; **overdue** (≥40) says bring the through-line home now and names `scene.resolved` + `arcStage` explicitly, because naming the exact field is the difference between a nudge and a mechanism. **Silent below 25 on purpose** — a chapter concluded early is its own failure (`LIFE-WORLD-ARC-*` wants a through-line, not a vignette), so the early game is never nudged toward wrapping up. |
| **LIFE-ARC-PACE-2** | **The pace counter is `world.storyLog.turns` — GENERATED turns — and is NOT `entries.length`.** A graded turn appends TWO log lines: the question node's own, and the one carried by its pre-authored resolution (`LIFE-EX-PLAN-5`), which costs no `life_step` call. Measured over a 25-turn harness run the entry list held **34** lines — 1.36x the turn count — so pacing off `entries.length` would have declared chapters overdue around turn 29 and concluded them near 23, replacing "chapters never end" with "chapters end early". `applyTurnSignals` therefore takes an opt-IN `countsAsTurn`, which `handleSignals` derives from `liveResult.fromResolution` (set only by `resolutionToArrival`). `turns` resets with the log at a chapter boundary, increments even on a turn that carried a `worldDelta` but no `logLine`, and is unbounded — unlike `entries`, which `capStoryLog` pins at `STORY_LOG_CAP` (60), so a runaway chapter keeps escalating rather than going quiet. Worlds written before the field existed seed `turns` from `entries.length` on the next write; that overstates the count, which can only make an already-overlong chapter conclude sooner. |
| **LIFE-ARC-PACE-3** | **`<chapterPace>` is inert without an ACTIVE SCENE, and the model was offering none.** A chapter ends only through `scene.resolved.arcStage = "concluding"` — which requires a scene, which requires the model to emit `scene.hook` and the player to accept it (`acceptSceneHook`). Measured across two harness runs against real Gemini (25 turns and 12 turns): **zero hooks offered, `<scene status="idle"/>` on every single turn.** So no chapter could have ended whatever `LIFE-ARC-PACE-1` said — this is the deadlock underneath the reported bug. Third instance of the same defect class (with `LIFE-EX-CADENCE-1` and `LIFE-ARC-PACE-1`): the SI asked for a hook "about every 5-10 nodes", a quantity the model cannot observe, hedged with "don't force it" — which makes *never* a compliant answer. `<hookPressure turnsIdle="N">` renders in the volatile tail **only while `scene` is absent** (an active scene already IS the through-line, and pressure to open a second fights `LIFE-SCENE-*`) **and never on the opening beat**. DUE at 6 idle turns lets the model place the hook within a node or two; OVERDUE at 10 asks for it on THIS node and states the reason — the chapter cannot resolve until a scene does — because naming the deadlock is what separates a mechanism from a nag. Thresholds sit inside the SI's own "every 5-10 nodes" band, so the block makes the existing rule observable rather than replacing it. `hookIdleStreak` counts `decision` events back to the most recent `scene_hook_offered` / `scene_hook_declined` / `scene_started` / `scene_completed`: **an OFFER resets it even when the player declined**, because the model did its part and re-nagging on the next node would produce exactly the hook spam the `scene_hook_declined` 5-turn cooldown exists to prevent. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-1** | A world has at most ONE active chapter at a time, stored inline as `world.currentArc`. On chapter resolve the arc moves to the **`completedArcs/{arcId}` SUBcollection** (`users/{uid}/lifeWorlds/{worldId}/completedArcs/`, `arcId = chapter-${chapterNumber}` — one doc per chapter, `src/lib/life/arcs.ts` `appendCompletedArc`/`readCompletedArcs`), NOT an unbounded inline array. The world doc carries only a cheap scalar `completedArcCount` (`ZLifeWorld.completedArcCount`, absent ⇒ 0) so the overview `WorldCard` badge reads the chapter count without a per-card subcollection query. `archiveActiveArc` does both in one flow: write the resolved arc to the subcollection, then `updateLifeWorld({ currentArc: null, completedArcCount: prev + 1 })`. **Clean break — no migration/dual-read/backfill; pre-existing worlds that lack the new shape are disposable.** Between chapters `currentArc` is `null` and the world sits in "ready for next chapter" state on the overview. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-2** | A `WorldArc` is `{ chapterNumber, premise, playerTeaser, themes[], status, recurringNpcIds[], completedSceneMemories[], outcomeLine?, startedAt, resolvedAt? }` — the whole arc is persisted as one subcollection doc (`readCompletedArcs` parses each via `ZWorldArc`, drops malformed rows, returns them chapterNumber-ascending). `completedSceneMemories[]` accumulates one entry per scene that resolves inside this chapter — read by both the next scene's generation AND the next chapter's worldArc gen. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-REGISTER** | **The chapter is authored in TWO registers, for two readers** (mirrors the saga's `premise` vs `playerFacingTeaser`). `premise` is the PLANNER register — to-the-point about what the chapter is INTENDED to explore + what it is NOT, read by the scene generator + the next chapter's gen; it MAY name tensions/twists the player hasn't discovered and is NEVER shown to the player. `playerTeaser` is the PLAYER register — the spoiler-safe, evocative chapter teaser shown on the overview `WorldCard`, the `WurldResumeScreen` "this chapter" line, and `WorldArcCompleteScreen`, and fed to the resume-recap generator; it reveals no twist/ending/un-met antagonist. Authored together in one `worldArc` gen call (`worldArcSystemInstruction` OUTPUT SHAPE + the TWO REGISTERS block); `ZWorldArc.playerTeaser` is `z.string().default('')` so a legacy arc (or a model that omits it) parses, and every player-facing consumer falls back to `premise` when `playerTeaser` is empty. **CYOA openness (`LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-1`):** the `premise` is framed as a LOOSE PULL, never a rail — it says what the chapter LEANS toward, and "a good premise survives the player going off-script" (the scene generator + the aggressive `LIFE-STORY-SHAPE-4` replan, soft-tick 5 turns, absorb divergence). |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-3** | Chapter 1 is generated at world creation via `runWorldArcGenerate` (model: `gemini-3.1-flash`, high temp). Fires non-blocking after `createLifeWorld`; the world doc receives `currentArc` within a few seconds. Failure is tolerated: a trivial inline arc lands so the world doesn't stall. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-4** | The lifeStep prompt's `<scene>` block now reads from `world.currentArc` when a scene is NOT active (giving the model the chapter premise + themes + recurring-NPC ids to lean on for hook generation). When a scene IS active, the scene's outline is the primary context; the worldArc colors it but doesn't override. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-5** | The scene-completion envelope is extended: `scene.resolved.arcStage` is one of `'continues' \| 'climactic' \| 'concluding'`. `'concluding'` signals that THIS scene ALSO ends the active worldArc. The model emits `'concluding'` sparingly — only when the arc's through-line genuinely resolves (typically after 3-6 scenes in the same arc). |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-7** | **Chapter LENGTH target** (v2.1, step SI `arcStage` pacing). A chapter should run roughly **25-35 player turns** end-to-end — which lands **~8-12 graded questions** (since ~1 turn in 3 is a question, `LIFE-EX-*`) — about 3-6 scenes before `arcStage: 'concluding'`. The SI is steered to that window: don't conclude in ~10 turns, don't drag past ~40; bring the through-line home once in range. This is the target that makes a chapter roughly a day's play (the owner intends an eventual one-chapter-a-day soft cap — NOT yet a hard block; only the prompt steering is in place). Prompt-first + fuzzy (the model does not count turns precisely); a deterministic turn-count nudge is the reserved safeguard. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-6** | On `arcStage: 'concluding'`, after the player dismisses `SceneCompleteScreen`, the active arc is archived (`archiveActiveArc`) and the `WorldArcCompleteScreen` overlay appears. Two CTAs: **Continue your wurld** fires `runWorldArcGenerate({ uid, world, chapterNumber: N+1 })` — which reads the prior chapters from the `completedArcs` subcollection (`readCompletedArcs`, LIFE-WORLD-ARC-1) and passes them into the prompt builder as `previousArcs` (the world doc no longer carries the arcs inline) — and writes the new `currentArc`; **Pause for now** navigates to `/life/overview`, leaving `currentArc: null` so the overview can offer "Ready for chapter N+1" later. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-7** | **The arc history handed to the chapter-open prompt is BOUNDED — head-and-tail, `COMPLETED_ARCS_CAP` 5 with `COMPLETED_ARCS_HEAD` 2.** `readCompletedArcs` returns the whole subcollection and its own comment rests on *"the arc count is tiny — one per chapter"*, which holds right up until somebody plays a world for a long time. `buildWorldArcPrompt` renders every arc it is given, ~2.7 kB each on a rich world, against ~40 kB of headroom once the system instruction and world block are paid for — so the call crosses `MAX_PROMPT_BYTES` at around **fifteen chapters** and the endpoint refuses it. ⚠ **It then fails SILENTLY**: `runWorldArcGenerate` catches and falls back to `trivialArc`, which is right for a transient fault and exactly wrong for a permanent one — a long-played world quietly stops getting authored chapters, and the worlds that break first are the ones someone cared enough to keep playing. **Head-and-tail rather than `.slice(-cap)`** for the same reason `capStoryLog` and `capCanon` chose it: chapter one establishes the premise, the cast and the world's terms, so keeping only the recent drops exactly the chapters the through-line rests on and retains the churn — a mistake `capStoryLog`'s own comment records making. **Capped at the CALL SITE, not inside the read**, because the read is a plain accessor and a "chapters so far" view would want the full history; this is a fact about what a PROMPT can carry. ⚠ Found only because `SRV-PROMPT-8..13` batch 3 measured it — the built path sums the two halves against the cap and rejected outright, where the legacy path caps them separately and merely postpones the same cliff by a few chapters. The bug predates the migration in both cases. ⚠ **THE CAP IS A MARGIN, NOT A CEILING, and the distinction is the point.** `life_resume_recap` was safe to migrate because every one of its inputs is bounded IN CODE. Here the counts are bounded — `MAX_PRINCIPALS`, `REGULARS_RENDER_CEILING`, `MAX_BIO_SCENE_MEMORIES`, `renderArc`'s six-memory slice, this cap — but `known`, `wants`, `nextMove`, `routine`, `agenda`, `situation`, every `law`, every `premise` and every scene `summary` is model-authored prose with **no length bound anywhere**. So no value of `COMPLETED_ARCS_CAP` turns this payload into a ceiling; it only buys headroom. Measured on a synthetic maximal world: uncapped at 20 chapters **148.8%** of cap (rejected outright), cap 8 **88.7%** (a near miss, about two chapters of drift), cap 6 78.7%, **cap 5 73.7%** — the first that clears 75%, and head 2 + tail 3 keeps the head-and-tail intent. ⚠ If it ever bites anyway the real fix is NOT a smaller number but a **byte budget** on the rendered arc block — render newest-first until the budget is spent — which degrades smoothly on a pathological world instead of stepping off a cliff. That is a deliberate behaviour change, not a tuning knob. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-7** | The scene-resolve envelope can also carry `keyMomentsForArc: string[]` (1-3 sentences). These accumulate into `arc.completedSceneMemories[].keyMomentsForArc[]` and are read by the next chapter's worldArc generator so chapters feel like genuine continuations of the prior chapter. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-11** | **A wurld delete shows a DELETING state, and the wait is BOUNDED.** Reported 2026-07-31: deleting the last wurld left its card faded and stuck until an app restart. The handler already had a `finally` clearing the state and a toast on error, so it was neither of those — the promise never SETTLED. `deleteLifeWorld` ends in `batch.commit()`, which Firestore resolves only on SERVER ack; the local write applies immediately, but on a degraded connection the await pends indefinitely, and **a `finally` is only as reliable as the promise it hangs off**. `settleWithTimeout` caps the wait at `DELETE_TIMEOUT_MS` (10s) and reports `ok` / `failed` / `timeout`. **A timeout is NOT a failure and must not read like one:** the delete is not cancelled — it stays queued in Firestore's offline layer and completes when the connection recovers — so the copy says so, and the UI simply stops claiming to be working. A late rejection arriving after a timeout is absorbed rather than escaping as an unhandled rejection with no UI left to attach it to. **An optimistic variant was tried and rejected** (hide the card immediately, delete in the background): it removes the pending state entirely, and the honest signal that something is still happening is worth more than the instant disappearance. |
| **LIFE-WORLD-ARC-9** | **A world MUST NOT be playable without a `currentArc`. Entering one that has none SELF-HEALS.** `archiveActiveArc` leaves `currentArc: null` by design (LIFE-WORLD-ARC-1/6), and until 2026-07-31 the ONLY thing that refilled it was the *Continue your wurld* CTA on `WorldArcCompleteScreen`.
